# DECISION_LOG.md — Engineering Decision Log

**Status:** Active, append-only (2026-07-16) · **Owner:** Technical Owner · **Authority:** Level 3 (records decisions *below* ADR level; anything architectural belongs in `docs/adr/`, not here — when in doubt, it's an ADR).

**What belongs here:** dependency additions · accepted technical debt (with revisit date) · deferred work backing `DEFERRED(DL-xxx)` markers · accepted performance/quality trade-offs · process/governance changes · notable non-architectural choices (tool selection, test strategy calls, hotfix compressions).

**Entry format (append at top of the table):**

| ID | Date | Owner | Summary | Rationale | Related ADR/doc | Impact | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DL-0259 | 2026-10-26 | Technical Owner | **Stage-2 runbook §3's host-wide `net.ipv4.ip_local_port_range` instruction is withdrawn and replaced by an explicit prohibition; the correction DL-0258 recorded as owed is now complete.** The runbook told operators to run `sysctl -w net.ipv4.ip_local_port_range="50000 50100"` so the media firewall rule would be small. That setting is **host-wide** — on a shared host it re-pools the ephemeral ports of every co-tenant service — and M13.2 (`9cd4e5d`) made it unnecessary by confining VoxVoice's own media sockets in-process. §3 now **prohibits writing the setting by any path** (`sysctl`, `/proc/sys`, `/etc/sysctl.d/`), records the measured baseline **`32768 60999`** as the value that must be preserved, states that **reading it remains fine**, and directs the operator to `VOXVOICE_MEDIA_UDP_PORT_RANGE=51000-51008` — half-open, so UDP ports **51000-51007** — with the sizing rule stated as an **estimate, not a guarantee** plus the worked example for the hub (one global address ⇒ `51000-51008`, no `ip6tables` media rule). **Delivered, four files:** the runbook bumped to **Active v1.1 (2026-10-26)** with the §11 revision record `DOCUMENTATION_RULES` §51 requires; `docs/INDEX.md`'s runbook row updated to match — **metadata only, its description byte-identical**; a new control, `platform/tests/test_operational_docs_guardrails.py` (**9 tests**), which scans **fenced code only** under `docs/operations/` — backtick, tilde and long fences, blockquoted or not, with the closing marker required to match the opening one — so a runnable write is caught while prose may state the rule and read-only inspection stays legitimate; and this row. The control also asserts that §3 **keeps** the replacement guidance and the preserved baseline, and parses the documented range with the **real** M13.2 parser so documentation and implementation cannot drift. Both directions are asserted — seven write spellings in four fence styles, four read forms in three — so it cannot pass vacuously. **One defect found by the control's own first run, and fixed:** a Markdown blockquote `>` was matched as a shell redirect, flagging `> cat /proc/sys/net/ipv4/ip_local_port_range` — a legitimate read — as a write. The blockquote prefix is now stripped before the shell patterns apply, and a test pins both halves: the read passes, a genuine blockquoted redirect is still caught. **Declared gaps, asserted rather than claimed away:** the scan is **not** exhaustive over Markdown. **Four-space indented code blocks are not scanned**, nor HTML `<pre>`, nor fences nested inside a fence of the same character; a test pins the indented-block hole so the module's docstring cannot drift from the truth. The control is scoped to `docs/operations/` only. **Deliberately not changed:** [`epic11-pre-live-handoff.md`](../docs/releases/epic11-pre-live-handoff.md) still recommends narrowing the sysctl, in prose (*"the recommended owner action"*) and in its optional *"narrowed first (recommended)"* checklist item. `DOCUMENTATION_RULES` §16 names only **audits and accepted ADRs** as immutable, and that document's status is `Active`, so a dated notice would arguably be permitted — but no release record carries one, marking the whole document Superseded would be false since its checklists and fifteen-item gate remain live, and it falls outside the approved four-file scope. **Residual risk, explicit: an operator reading only that handoff would still find the narrowing recommended, and the new control does not cover `docs/releases/`.** The runbook supersedes it by name instead. No pre-existing Decision Log row is altered. **Nothing was executed:** the prohibited command was never run and is nowhere recommended; no host was contacted; `net.ipv4.ip_local_port_range` on `140.245.10.153` remains `32768 60999`. | A prohibition that lives only in a decision record is one an operator never reads. The instruction was in the document they would actually follow, on a shared host where it would have taken the ephemeral ports of thirteen neighbours — so the fix belongs in that document, and a control now makes its return a failing test rather than a discovery. Scanning fenced code rather than text is what lets the same document forbid the command and explain why; the blockquote false positive, found on the control's first run, is why the distinction is tested in both directions rather than asserted once. | [DL-0258](#); [DL-0255](#), [DL-0256](#), [DL-0257](#); [stage2 runbook](../docs/operations/stage2-voice-host-runbook.md) §3/§11 (v1.1); `VOXVOICE_MEDIA_UDP_PORT_RANGE` (M13.2, `9cd4e5d`); `DOCUMENTATION_RULES` §16/§51; [INDEX](../docs/INDEX.md) | Documentation and test only — **no production code, no dependency, no host change.** `uv.lock` unchanged at 53; `E10-7` unaffected; no existing test modified; no pre-existing Decision Log row altered. **Stage-2 unchanged:** `E11-15` NOT EXECUTED, `E11-12` INCONCLUSIVE, `R-33` OPEN, 15/15 live-gate items unticked, Epic 11 **NOT CERTIFIED**. DL-0258's six separate infrastructure approvals and the demo session itself remain unauthorized. | Accepted |
| DL-0258 | 2026-10-26 | Technical Owner | **DL-0153 Amendment 1: one attended, time-limited demo of VoxVoice may run on the shared hub `140.245.10.153`. DL-0153's Stage-2 host selection is UNCHANGED — the dedicated Oracle Ampere A1 remains the Stage-2 target and this is not a substitution.** **Demo window:** exactly **one operator-attended session of up to 60 minutes**, on revision `9cd4e5d`, at zero additional infrastructure cost. The operator is present for its whole duration and **must stop the service at the end of the session** — `systemctl disable --now voxvoice-demo` — whether or not the demo succeeded. The service is never left running after the session, never enabled at boot, and never operated unattended. A second session requires a new approval. **Explicitly NOT permitted:** Stage-2 certification, production use, unattended operation, or any claim that `E11-15`, `E11-12`, `R-33` or the fifteen-item live gate is satisfied — all remain open and **unwaived**. **THE HOST-WIDE EPHEMERAL PORT RANGE MUST NEVER BE MODIFIED.** `net.ipv4.ip_local_port_range` was read at baseline as **`32768 60999`** and **must remain exactly `32768 60999`**. It is not to be narrowed, widened, re-ordered or otherwise changed, by this demo or by anything done in support of it, at any time, for any reason — it is host-wide and re-pooling it would take the ephemeral ports of all 13 co-tenant agents. The Stage-2 runbook's §3 instruction to run `sysctl -w net.ipv4.ip_local_port_range=...` **must not be executed**; correcting that document is a separate approval, recorded below as still owed. **Baseline that justifies this (measured read-only 2026-10-26):** Ubuntu 24.04.4 x86_64, 2 vCPU, load 0.08; MemTotal 954 MiB with **MemAvailable 465 MiB**; **swap already ~1,397 MiB in use of 2,047**; memory PSI full 0.00; 19 GB disk free; nginx owns :443/:80; 13 agent units all `NRestarts=0`, 3-20 MB each; **one global address, `10.0.0.140`, private IPv4, no global IPv6**; ephemeral range at the default `32768 60999`; no UDP listeners in any high range; python3 3.12.3; `uv` absent; no VoxVoice footprint. **Resource limits, derived from that baseline:** the unit sets **`MemoryHigh=256M`** and **`MemoryMax=320M`** so the kernel throttles and then kills **VoxVoice** rather than letting the OOM killer choose among the 13 agents. 320 MiB is ~69% of measured available headroom, above the expected peak and below what would pressure the host. **The expected peak is an estimate, not a measurement:** the measured floor is 106 MB RSS import-time plus 78-91 MB for a media-only harness, both on macOS/arm64; **no Linux measurement exists**, which is why the cap is set generously and enforced by the kernel rather than trusted. **Abort immediately, and roll back, if** `MemAvailable` falls below **250 MiB**, memory PSI `full avg60` is non-zero for 60 s, swap-in-use grows more than **150 MiB** above 1,397 MiB, io PSI `full avg60` exceeds 5, load exceeds 1.5 sustained, **any of the 13 agents changes `MainPID` or leaves `NRestarts=0`**, `nginx -t` fails at any point, or media does not flow within the window. **Networking:** VoxVoice confines its own media sockets in-process with **`VOXVOICE_MEDIA_UDP_PORT_RANGE=51000-51008`** (half-open; ports 51000-51007), sized from the measured **single** global address as `1 x (1 call + 1 teardown overlap)` plus six ports of headroom for the orphan limitation below. Only **UDP 51000-51007** is opened, at the OCI Security List **and** host `iptables`; **no `ip6tables` media rule is needed** because the host has no global IPv6. The retired coturn ports **3478, 5349 and 49152-49200 are NOT opened** — VoxVoice refuses `turn:` at startup and deploys no relay. **`VOXVOICE_ICE_STUN_URLS` is mandatory**, because the sole host candidate `10.0.0.140` is unroutable behind 1:1 NAT; this declares a third-party STUN dependency for the demo window (ADR-0054 permits STUN, refuses TURN). **DNS/TLS:** a **DNS-only (grey-cloud)** A record is required for the demo hostname, because a Cloudflare-proxied **wildcard** currently answers every `*.heyagenthive.com` name and the proxy cannot carry WebRTC UDP; the origin therefore needs a publicly valid Let's Encrypt certificate for that name, since the browser connects directly. **Isolation:** own `voxvoice` user, own unit, writes only under `/var/lib/voxvoice`, binds **loopback only**, fronted by **one additional** nginx `server` block for the new hostname — **no existing vhost edited**, `nginx -t` must pass before activation, and nginx is **reloaded, never restarted**. The console bundle is **built off-host and copied**, not built on the hub: an npm build can transiently consume several hundred MB, which this host cannot spare. **`uv` is installed into `/opt/voxvoice` only**, never system-wide. **No existing agent is restarted, reloaded or reconfigured at any point.** **Known limitation, accepted and NOT fixed:** a `CancelledError` escaping aioice's gather loop orphans sockets (`aioice/ice.py:1009` registers protocols only after the bind loop; `:635-637` closes only what is registered), and a bounded range sharpens the consequence — each such event permanently consumes one of the eight ports until the process restarts. Bounded here by six ports of headroom, by the allocator's WARNING line in journald, and by `Restart=on-failure` with `RestartSec=2`, `StartLimitBurst=3` and `StartLimitIntervalSec=300` so a repeatedly killed service **stops trying instead of thrashing the host**. An operator `systemctl stop` is an explicit stop and is never countermanded by `Restart=`, so the end-of-session stop and the abort procedure both take effect immediately and do not conflict with the restart policy. **It is not production-certified and no cleanup guarantee is claimed.** **Rollback — VoxVoice resources only, and it restarts nothing shared:** `systemctl disable --now voxvoice-demo`; remove the unit and `daemon-reload`; delete `/opt/voxvoice`, `/var/lib/voxvoice`, `/etc/voxvoice`; remove exactly the `iptables` rule added, recorded verbatim with line numbers beforehand; remove the DNS record. **The nginx server block is deliberately left in place** — with VoxVoice stopped it affects only the demo hostname, and leaving it avoids touching the shared nginx at rollback time; its removal is a later maintenance step. **Provider keys are rotated only if exposure or compromise is suspected**, not as routine teardown. | A cost of zero is not a risk of zero. This host carries 13 running agents on 954 MiB and is already ~1.4 GB into swap, which is precisely why DL-0153 excluded it — so the exception is granted only for a single attended hour whose resource ceiling is enforced by the kernel rather than by attention, whose every abort trigger is a number measured beforehand, and whose rollback touches nothing another tenant depends on. The one setting that could harm all 13 neighbours at once, the host-wide ephemeral port range, is placed out of bounds absolutely rather than left to judgement. | [DL-0153](#) (amended, not edited); [DL-0255](#), [DL-0256](#), [DL-0257](#); [stage2 runbook](../docs/operations/stage2-voice-host-runbook.md) §1/§3/§5/§6; `VOXVOICE_MEDIA_UDP_PORT_RANGE` (M13.2, `9cd4e5d`); [ADR-0054](../docs/adr/ADR-0054-ice-server-policy-is-explicit-at-the-composition-root.md); `R-E12-5`; AR-6 | Revision authorized: **`9cd4e5d`**. Demo is **one attended session of at most 60 minutes**, stopped by the operator at its end. **Stage-2 unchanged:** `E11-15` NOT EXECUTED, `E11-12` INCONCLUSIVE, `R-33` OPEN, 15/15 live-gate items unticked, Epic 11 **NOT CERTIFIED**. **This approval authorizes none of the following, each of which needs its own:** the OCI + `iptables` UDP rule, the grey-cloud DNS record, the Let's Encrypt certificate, the nginx server block, the systemd unit, and installing `uv` under `/opt/voxvoice`. **Separate correction still owed:** runbook §3 still instructs the prohibited host-wide `sysctl` and must be corrected under its own approval. | Accepted |
| DL-0257 | 2026-10-21 | Technical Owner | **Correction to DL-0255 decision (6), on the consent-expiry path only: ICE consent loss does *not* surface as `connectionState == "failed"`, so R1 as shipped in `530adb8` never fired on the abrupt-loss path it was written for, and that loss was reported as `DISCONNECTED`. Fixed in `02099c9` by observing the ICE *transport* state. No ADR is amended or superseded; DL-0255 and DL-0256 are not edited.** **What was contradicted, and what was not.** DL-0255 (6) is **correct** that the ICE transport reaches `failed`, and **correct** that a dead network path must leave as `FAILED` rather than `DISCONNECTED` (AR-6). Three of its mechanism claims are contradicted **for the consent-expiry path**: that `connectionstatechange` fires with `failed` (it fires with `closed`); that *nothing closed the connection* (aiortc closes it itself); and that *the receiver was never stopped, `recv()` never raised, `inbound()` never returned and the session lived on with no media and no event* (the receiver is stopped, `recv()` raises, `inbound()` returns, and an event is emitted at roughly 33 s). Its claim that aiortc auto-closes *only* on a clean DTLS `close_notify` is likewise too narrow: it also auto-closes when DTLS dies of a connection error. **The hypothesised path is not dead and the original watcher is retained**, because `connectionState == "failed"` is still reachable through DTLS handshake failure (`rtcdtlstransport.py:422-465`) and through ICE negotiation failure in `RTCIceTransport.start()` (`:343-345`), where the peer connection is not closing. **No claim is made that R1 was wrong on any path other than consent expiry.** **Structural cause, read from the installed aiortc 1.15.0 / aioice 0.10.2.** aioice reports consent loss by closing its own connection (`aioice/ice.py:1088-1091`), and that close notifies the receiver **before** releasing the closer (`ice.py:255-259`), so the local DTLS transport dies with `ConnectionError` and reaches `closed` (`rtcdtlstransport.py:424-429`, `:579`). `dtlsStates == {"closed"}` then makes the peer connection auto-close (`rtcpeerconnection.py:1298-1305`), and `close()` sets its closed flag on its **first statement** (`:517-520`) — before the ICE monitor promotes the transport to `failed` (`rtcicetransport.py:363-365`). From then on `__updateConnectionState` answers `"closed"` and never reaches its `"failed" in iceStates` branch (`:1270-1275`). `iceConnectionState` is masked by the same first branch (`:1316-1320`). This is ordering fixed by the library's own code, not a race. **The fix, and why observing was only half of it.** The ICE transport is an `AsyncIOEventEmitter` and emits `"statechange"` (`rtcicetransport.py:372-376`), reached publicly through `getTransceivers() -> receiver.transport -> .transport` and deduplicated by identity because one transport serves every bundled transceiver. But the verdict arrives **after** the track has already ended, so a listener alone is always too late: `inbound()` now waits for it on a condition under a bounded deadline (`ICE_SETTLE_DEADLINE_S = 0.1`, M11.15 R-70) and **fails open** to the previous `DISCONNECTED` on timeout. A hangup therefore cannot become a failure: the only fault this can surface is one an ICE transport actually reported. **Evidence, from an instrumented in-process experiment, two independent passes of three runs per arm (18 runs).** Abrupt loss **6/6**: no verdict known at the instant `inbound()` terminated, ICE still `completed`, the `failed` transition following by 0.386, 0.409, 0.828, 0.599, 0.543 and 0.411 ms; detection at 29.96, 33.65, 35.52, 34.29, 32.25 and 32.50 s. Clean remote close **6/6**: ICE sequence `checking -> completed -> closed`, `failed` never appearing — which is what makes the marker sound. Zero leftover tasks and clean peer-connection teardown in all 18. Regression evidence: eight tests, two against a real aiortc pair asserting an independently observed ICE state sequence; full suite **7033 passed**, 0 failed, under CI's warning flags (21 m 47 s); certification, freeze and application suites **285 passed**; `test_webrtc_peer_loss.py` byte-identical with its nine tests passing unmodified. **Limitations, stated rather than discovered later.** All of it is aiortc-to-aiortc in one process on loopback, on **one machine** (macOS, CPython 3.13.3). **Nothing here establishes browser, real-network, NAT/CGNAT, carrier, Stage-2 or production behaviour**, and the roughly 30 s consent interval remains an **expected bound to validate, never an SLA** — six observations on one host are an anecdote, not a measurement to quote. `ICE_SETTLE_DEADLINE_S = 0.1` is a **candidate** sized at about 120x the worst observed interval, unmeasured on Linux CI; correctness does not depend on it, since the design is order-independent and fails open. A third experiment arm — a track ending while ICE stays healthy — was **inconclusive 6/6** because the inbound track never terminated at all within 25 s, so the full-deadline fail-open path is proven only by a double and that shape is **reachable but unmeasured**. Two gaps are unchanged: a media path dead while ICE still reports a live pair stays indistinguishable from silence, and a caller that merely stops sending without closing anything produces **no event at all**. The fault record is deliberately not cleared in `_safe_close`, because teardown runs microseconds before `inbound()` reads it and clearing it there would silently restore this very defect; residue is limited to a session whose `inbound()` never ran and is cleared on session-id reuse. | An append-only record whose mechanism is wrong is worse than a gap, because the next reader will design against it: DL-0255 described a hang that does not occur and a predicate that cannot match, and both were believed until a real in-process peer was driven to abrupt loss and the result was a clean `DISCONNECTED`. The error is mine and it was found by the test written to prove the opposite, which is the test doing its job. Correcting it costs one row, leaves the original intact and auditable, and keeps the correction no broader than the evidence: one path, named, with the other paths explicitly still covered. | AR-6 ([ARCHITECTURE_RULES](ARCHITECTURE_RULES.md)); `platform/core/providers/transport.py::TransportEventKind` (M11.5); [ADR-0045](../docs/adr/ADR-0045-streaming-adapter-pattern-and-vad-execution.md) SD4; [DL-0255](#) decision (6) (corrected here, not edited); [DL-0256](#) (citation correction, unaffected); [stage2 runbook](../docs/operations/stage2-voice-host-runbook.md) §8; M11.15 R-70 | Commit `02099c9`, independently revertible and touching one production file (`providers/transport/aiortc_endpoint.py`) plus one new test file. The runbook §8 trap row is corrected in this same change set so the operator-facing description matches what the code now does. No endpoint, capability, event type, frozen file or dependency changed: `E10-7` reports **0** modified frozen `platform/api` files, `uv.lock` is unchanged at 53 packages, ruff and `git diff --check` are clean, and no `skip`, `skipif` or `xfail` was added. No ADR amended; no M12 boundary or digest touched. **No push, merge, tag or deploy, and nothing ran on `140.245.10.153`.** **Unchanged and still open:** `E11-15` NOT EXECUTED, `E11-12` INCONCLUSIVE, `R-33` OPEN, and the fifteen-item live gate; whether an `M13_*` control set is created; and **the AgentHive hub-demo exception, which remains an unapproved draft and is not advanced by this row.** | Accepted |
| DL-0256 | 2026-10-20 | Technical Owner | **Correction to DL-0255's authority citation: the `FAILED` / `DISCONNECTED` distinction R1 preserves is owned by AR-6 and `core/providers/transport.py::TransportEventKind` (M11.5), not by ADR-0046. No ADR is amended or superseded.** DL-0255's *Related* column cites [ADR-0046](../docs/adr/ADR-0046-media-path-fault-tolerance-and-provider-mechanism-reuse.md) as *"the `FAILED`/`DISCONNECTED` distinction R1 preserves"*. That attribution is **wrong**: ADR-0046 contains no occurrence of `DISCONNECTED`, `TransportEventKind`, *transport event*, *hangup* or even `AR-6`. Its subject is **provider-call fault tolerance** — FT-D1–FT-D10: AR-23 enforced on the media path, the Provider Runtime's execution runtime declined, `CircuitBreaker` and `FailoverController` reused rather than rebuilt, the thin `runtime/` decorator, per-capability ordered failover, the §A10 degradation ladder, AR-24 recording, the AR-25 discharge of `allow_interruptions` / `llm_model` / `first_message`, and the `ProviderRegistry` name collision as accepted debt. **The direct authority is:** **(1) AR-6** — *"Any component learning of a real-world lifecycle fact (hangup, connect, failure) must emit the event that drives the state machine — status may never be inferred-only from API calls"*; and **(2) `core/providers/transport.py::TransportEventKind` (M11.5)**, whose own docstring already fixes the semantics R1 implements: *"`DISCONNECTED` — the transport went away. The real-world hangup. `FAILED` — the transport could not be established, or died in a way that ends usability. Separate from `DISCONNECTED` because a clean hangup and a broken transport are different facts and lead to different session outcomes."* **The related principle is [ADR-0045](../docs/adr/ADR-0045-streaming-adapter-pattern-and-vad-execution.md) SD4** — *"A vendor disconnect must never be indistinguishable from a caller hangup — that would make session status stop reflecting call reality (AR-6) and would emit the wrong lifecycle event"* — which states the same rule one layer in, for provider **streams** rather than the transport. ADR-0046 is reached from SD4 only as the source of the **typed-error** vocabulary, which is the same narrow role it plays for R1 (`MediaEndpointError` → `FAILED`); it is **unaffected** by R1, which adds no breaker, no failover, no timeout and no degradation record, and touches no provider-call path. **Scope of this correction:** citation only. R1's behaviour, tests and commit (`530adb8`) are unchanged; **no ADR is amended or superseded**, and ADR-0045 and ADR-0046 are not modified. DL-0255 is **not** edited — this log is append-only, so the record stands and this row corrects it. | An inaccurate citation in an append-only record is worse than a missing one: it sends the next reader to a document that does not contain the decision, and it would make a future amendment look as though it belonged to ADR-0046's provider-fault-tolerance scope. The error was mine in the M13.1 design gate and was carried into DL-0255; a read-only governance review of ADR-0046 against R1 caught it. Correcting it costs one row and leaves the original intact and auditable. | AR-6 ([ARCHITECTURE_RULES](ARCHITECTURE_RULES.md)); `platform/core/providers/transport.py::TransportEventKind` (M11.5); [ADR-0045](../docs/adr/ADR-0045-streaming-adapter-pattern-and-vad-execution.md) SD4; [ADR-0046](../docs/adr/ADR-0046-media-path-fault-tolerance-and-provider-mechanism-reuse.md) (scope clarified, unchanged); [DL-0255](#) | No code, test, control or certification change; nothing re-run. The `docs/operations/stage2-voice-host-runbook.md` §8 trap row is updated in the same change set so the operator-facing symptom matches R1's behaviour. **Unresolved and unchanged:** R1's ICE detection is still unvalidated on a real network and its ≈30 s bound is derived from aioice constants rather than measured; a media path that is dead while ICE still reports a live pair remains indistinguishable from silence. **The AgentHive hub-demo exception remains an unapproved draft and is not touched by this row.** | Accepted |
| DL-0255 | 2026-10-20 | Technical Owner | **M13.1 verdict: PASS. An optional per-process call-concurrency guard, ICE-failure cleanup in the media endpoint, and a single-process startup lock — three independently revertible changes, each off or inert unless a deployment asks for it, and no endpoint, capability, event type or frozen file touched.** **Why a guard at all:** `MAX_OPEN_RENDEZVOUS` is not a call limit. It is 64, it counts *reservations* rather than conversations, and `RENDEZVOUS_TTL_S` is measured from reservation, so a slot is reclaimed 120 s after it was opened **even while its call is running**. A deployment on a constrained shared host needs an actual cap; the earlier claim (hub-demo audit) that the registry already returned 429 for a second call was **wrong** and is corrected here. **Delivered:** `api/webrtc/call_slot.py` (`CallSlot`, `CallSlotBusy`, `capacity_from_env`), `api/webrtc/single_call.py` (`SingleCallController`), `api/webrtc/process_lock.py`, the acquisition and eviction in `api/routers/webrtc.py:create_webrtc_session`, composition in `api/webrtc/app.py`, the ICE watcher in `providers/transport/aiortc_endpoint.py`, and `VOXVOICE_MAX_CONCURRENT_CALLS` / `VOXVOICE_SINGLE_PROCESS_LOCK` documented by **name** in `.env.example` and the Stage-2 runbook (§5/§6/§7). **Decisions.** **(1) Capacity is taken in the ROUTER, released in a controller decorator.** The frozen `api/routers/sessions.py:create_session` writes the `Session` row *before* calling `controller.start` and wraps that call in `except Exception` → `502`, so a guard inside the controller chain would have had its `409` laundered into a generic `502` **and** left a `status="failed"` row behind for every caller it refused. `RendezvousRegistry.check_bindable` already establishes that pattern for the same reason. Release stays in the chain, where every teardown path converges. **(2) Capacity is keyed by the rendezvous id and rebound to the session id at `start`** — the session id is minted inside the frozen handler and does not exist when the router must already have decided. The first design keyed it by session id and would have leaked a slot whenever a request died between acquisition and `start`; the gate review caught it. **(3) Reclamation is state-gated, never timed.** `conversation_state` returns `None` once the runtime has forgotten a session and a terminal state once the conversation finished — including the finish a caller hangup produces, since the transport's ended inbound stream drives `orchestrator.end(reason="completed", source="transport")`. So an active-but-silent call is distinguishable from a dead one, and `acquired_at` is recorded for logs and **never read as a reclaim condition**: an earlier draft's 900 s TTL was withdrawn because any elapsed-time rule eventually evicts a legitimate conversation. **(4) Fail closed.** The probe is reached only when capacity is full, so a broken probe cannot block the first call; when the slot is full and the probe raises, the holder is treated as **live** and the caller refused, because inferring *free* from *unknown* is how a cap silently stops being one. The 409 detail is identical for both refusal reasons; the reason is logged, never returned. **(5) Atomicity by construction:** `try_acquire` contains no `await`, so the capacity check and the record it writes cannot interleave; eviction of a finished holder is therefore **returned** to the caller and awaited after acquisition, never performed inside. The router releases on `BaseException`, because `CancelledError` is one and the frozen handler does not catch it. **(6) R1 reports `FAILED`, not `DISCONNECTED`.** aiortc auto-closes only on a clean DTLS `close_notify`; on abrupt loss aioice's consent freshness fails, ICE moves to `failed`, `connectionstatechange` fires and **nothing closed the connection**, so the receiver was never stopped, `recv()` never raised, `inbound()` never returned and the session lived on with no media and no event. The endpoint now closes on `failed` and records the cause so `inbound()` **raises**, because closing ends the track cleanly and a dead network path is not a hangup (AR-6; the console renders *Media failed* and *Caller disconnected* differently). **Detection time is aioice's, not ours** — `CONSENT_FAILURES` × `CONSENT_INTERVAL` ≈ 30 s today, path-dependent, an expected bound to validate on a real host and **not a guarantee this change makes**. **(7) `flock`, not a pid file:** the lock belongs to the open file description, so the kernel releases it on any exit including `SIGKILL` — no stale lock, no pid to validate. It enforces the **single-process precondition** the in-process slot and the registry both depend on (`R-E12-5`) and is **not** distributed or multi-worker coordination. **Validation, executed:** full suite **7025 passed**, 0 failed, 0 skipped/xfail (21 m 23 s); certification and freeze controls **46 passed**; new suites 32 + 18 + 9 + 12 = **71 tests**, including genuinely concurrent `asyncio.gather` attempts, a refusal that writes **no** `Session` row and does not disturb the live call, cancellation both inside and before `controller.start`, an eviction whose teardown itself fails, repeated ICE state changes, a teardown-after-stop orphan check, a clean close still reported as `DISCONNECTED`, and a `SIGKILL`ed lock holder proving kernel release. **`E10-7` reports 0** modified pre-`v4.0.0` `platform/api` files; ruff clean; `uv lock` unchanged at 53 packages; `git diff --check` clean. **Mutation-checked (6):** failing open on a probe error, not recording the holder, narrowing the router's release to `Exception`, dropping the fault branch, and never registering the ICE watcher each break a test. **One gate failure, reported not worked around:** the first full run failed `test_application.py::test_no_http_router_layer_exists_yet`, a literal substring search for `"application"` over `api/routers/**` — tripped by a docstring word, not a real dependency on the app layer. **The code was reworded; the control is byte-identical** (`7662812`). **Not done:** no push, tag, merge or deploy; nothing ran on `140.245.10.153`; no M13 certification control set (deferred by owner decision); `capacity > 1` is implemented but only `None` and `1` are verified. **Carried limitations:** per process only; it is a call count and **not** a memory limit (systemd `MemoryMax` remains separate and neither substitutes for the other); an alive worker whose media is dead while ICE still reports a live pair is **not distinguishable** from a quiet call and remains operator-bounded; reclaim is on-demand, so a stale entry can persist unobserved between acquisitions; an eviction whose `end()` fails may leave a `SessionWorker` in the runtime until the process restarts. | A cap that is wrong is worse than none: evicting a long quiet call, or inferring *free* from *unknown*, would each break a working conversation to admit a new one. So the two things that could do harm — timing and uncertainty — were made impossible by construction rather than tuned, and the whole feature defaults to inert so no existing deployment inherits a limit it never chose. | [ADR-0055](../docs/adr/ADR-0055-developer-platform-productizes-the-m11-runtime-model.md) Amendment 6 (the closed M12 range this milestone sits after); [ADR-0043](../docs/adr/ADR-0043-m10.13-certification.md) CD7 (additive modules under `E10-7`); [ADR-0046](../docs/adr/ADR-0046-media-path-fault-tolerance-and-provider-mechanism-reuse.md) (the `FAILED`/`DISCONNECTED` distinction R1 preserves); [ADR-0054](../docs/adr/ADR-0054-ice-server-policy-is-explicit-at-the-composition-root.md); [stage2 runbook](../docs/operations/stage2-voice-host-runbook.md) §5/§6/§7; `R-E12-5`; AR-6; AR-14; AR-25 | Commits `788db92` (guard), `530adb8` (R1), `0177939` (lock), `7662812` (control fix) — each independently revertible, and unsetting `VOXVOICE_MAX_CONCURRENT_CALLS` makes the guard inert with no code change. **This is the evidence for the single-concurrency pre-deployment gate of the proposed AgentHive hub demo exception, which remains an unapproved draft.** **Owner decisions still open:** whether R1 warrants an ADR-0046 amendment rather than this record; whether an `M13_*` control set is created; and the hub-demo amendment itself. | Accepted |

| DL-0252 | 2026-10-07 | Technical Owner | **M12.14 verdict: the Playground is observable, and it was built as a projection — no new event type, store, bus, state machine or transcript path, and no runtime change.** **Decision 1 — reuse, do not duplicate.** Epic 10's durable log already holds every event a session publishes in `seq` order, and M11's transcript sink holds every finalized line. `api/console/diagnostics/` renders those two; one SSE endpoint streams them. The Playground is a consumer of runtime truth and never the source (ADR-0055 DP1, and the Phase-11 rule this milestone set itself). **Decision 2 — SSE, over a WebSocket or the data channel.** The stream is one-directional, text and already ordered; SSE is a plain GET, so it carries the console's own HttpOnly cookie, needs no new handshake, no new browser credential and no dependency. The signalling token was rejected as a carrier because it is single-purpose and belongs to the media path (S1). **Decision 3 — stage diagnostics stay per-turn, and ADR-0038 was NOT amended.** The brief's example log (`STT_STARTED`, `LLM_STARTED`, …) would need six publishes per turn inside the voice-to-voice budget, which **TP10 rules against** because `InProcessEventBus.publish()` is synchronous run-to-completion — and it would mean editing Epic 11 runtime code whose latency certification rests on that ruling. Owner chose the per-turn option: `endpoint`/`stt`/`llm`/`tts`/`egress` durations arrive on `metric.turn` as each turn completes, and an unmeasured stage stays **absent, never 0** (E11-10). Recorded as a documented partial, not as complete per-boundary tracing. **Decision 4 — no interim transcript.** It reaches `on_transcript` with `is_final=False` and nothing persists it; surfacing it would create a second transcript path able to diverge from the persisted conversation. Owner chose finalized-only, and the UI states the limit rather than leaving a gap. Live and persisted therefore agree **by construction**: they are the same rows. **Live and replay are one read**, so there is no live-versus-durable distinction to draw: a watcher asks for events after the `seq` it has seen, a reader of a finished call asks from 0, and a dropped stream resumes losing nothing. **Security:** `developer`+ (the Playground's rule, not Conversations'), org-scoped lookup so another organization's session is 404, and redaction that **fails closed** — `data` is never passed through, each type names its allowed fields, every value is re-checked, and provider *names* are withheld. A diagnostics failure is never a call failure (DL-0013). **No ADR added**, deliberately: no architectural decision was introduced, one was applied. **Validation:** 30 platform tests, 27 console tests (5,000 events stay bounded at 300 DOM rows; reconnect resumes by `seq`; all eight M12.13 templates drive the same path), browser journey extended with live diagnostics, post-call retention, label verification and detail expansion. | A user who cannot see what the runtime is doing cannot tell a working call from a broken one — and the way to show it was to surface what the runtime already records, not to record it again. | [m12-runtime-diagnostics.md](../docs/testing/m12-runtime-diagnostics.md); [ADR-0038](../docs/adr/ADR-0038-runtime-latency-taps-and-the-metric-turn-producer.md) TP10 | The Playground is operationally transparent within the existing event architecture. Open: per-boundary stage events (an ADR decision), interim transcript, diagnostics export, and real-provider validation. No deployment, push, tag, merge or SDK publication. | Accepted |

Contract: `docs/testing/m12-e2e.md`. **Defects found and fixed:** (1) **the SDK could not run in a browser at all** — it called the global `fetch` unbound, which a browser rejects with `Illegal invocation` and the SDK reported as "Could not reach the VoxVoice API"; bound in `client.ts`/`signalling.ts` with a regression test that fails without the fix; (2) console tables had no horizontal cell spacing, so a long email ran into the next column (spacing plus an opt-in `.cell--break`, because breaking every cell also split short words); (3) the Developer docs page overflowed horizontally at ≤ 1024 px because a wide code block set the page grid's minimum width (`minmax(0, 1fr)`); (4) a `/favicon.ico` 404 on every page load (an empty `<link rel="icon">`); (5) **documentation defect** — `POST /v1/sessions` fails with 502 on a voice-enabled server rather than starting an unjoinable session; corrected in the developer contract, the SDK README and the console docs. **Decisions:** no feature, API, runtime, telemetry or schema change; the harness is test infrastructure and is explicitly not real-provider evidence (deterministic providers, so the console's "real providers" label is a harness artefact); harness output is never committed (an API-key screenshot holds that run's throwaway key). **Validation, executed:** full suite **6871 passed**, 0 skipped/xfail/failed, under the CI warning flags (22 m 39 s; 6863 + 8 new), including E10-7, certification and freeze, and M12.2–M12.9. Console **87 tests** (new: one create request however fast the button is clicked), typecheck, build, audit 0. SDK **41 tests** (new: the global fetch is used the way a browser requires), typecheck, package smoke, audit 0. Ruff (CI scope), `uv lock` 53, `git diff --check` clean, no pre-`v4.0.0` api file touched, 0 vendor identifiers. **Secret scan:** no credential committed (every match is a placeholder or a test asserting a variable *name*). **Performance baseline** (SQLite, 200 conversations / 1,200 events / 5 agents): overview 16–17 ms, each analytics endpoint 12–15 ms, conversations 5–6 ms, agents 3–4 ms; one overview request replaces ~62 ms of analytics calls; 43 page loads with no request fan-out. **Not executed:** a real spoken call with real providers (no credentials, and a person is needed) and Postgres (no driver). **Classified DEFER:** F-1 barge-in (verified unchanged), F-4 `en-IN` default, F-5 hard delete, the `PATCH`-null 500 (the fix belongs in a frozen router), no rate limiting, no durable key audit trail, unpublished SDK, offset-less timestamps, the M12.6 turn-query growth, and conversation detail rendering every turn. | The milestones become one product: verified across the seams, in a real browser, with its limits written down rather than hidden. | [m12-e2e.md](../docs/testing/m12-e2e.md); [e2e/README](../e2e/README.md); [ADR-0055](../docs/adr/ADR-0055-developer-platform-productizes-the-m11-runtime-model.md) | The product is ready for M12.11 certification. **Owner actions:** one real spoken call (local-run §7a); decide F-1, the key audit trail, rate limiting and SDK publication. | Accepted |
| DL-0246 | 2026-09-21 | Technical Owner | **M12.9 verdict: PASS WITH DOCUMENTED RISKS. `@voxvoice/sdk` 0.1.0 is a thin, typed client of the developer API exactly as it exists: no API, runtime, provider or authentication change.** **Delivered:** `sdk/` (TypeScript, ESM with declarations, Node ≥ 20.3, zero runtime dependencies, `private`): `VoxVoiceClient` with `agents.create/list/get/update/delete`, `sessions.create/get/transcript/end` and `webrtc.createRendezvous/startSession` (x-api-key); `@voxvoice/sdk/signalling` `SignallingClient.getOffer/sendAnswer` (x-signalling-token only, browser-safe); `VoxVoiceError {status, detail, message}`; `{signal, timeoutMs}` on every call; README; the contract snapshot `sdk/contract/developer-openapi.json` generated by `api.console.developer_contract`; the CI job `sdk`; SDK sections in `developer-api.md` and the console docs. **Decisions (ADR-0055 Amendment 4, DP14):** (1) One package with two entry points, superseding LLD §9's four-package plan. The high-level browser `Call`, React hook and Python helper are deferred (a browser-safe session start does not exist in v1). (3) Source of truth = server OpenAPI. The snapshot has drift tests on both sides: platform (file equals the generated contract) and SDK (every method is exactly one documented operation, with the right credential and a schema-conformant body; every type has exactly the schema's fields, enforced by typed samples). Types are hand-written but bound by those tests. (4) Nothing added: no retries (no idempotency keys), no pagination (none in the API), no default timeout, no auth caching, no logging; `null`s and offset-less UTC timestamp strings are passed through. SDK: typecheck, **40 tests** (12 contract; configuration, base-URL normalization incl. trailing slashes and a path prefix, GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE, headers, JSON bodies, null vs omitted, 204, empty and malformed bodies, errors 400/401/403/404/409/422/500/502, 422 summary without the echoed input, network failure and no retry, timeout per client and per call, caller abort as AbortError, id encoding, concurrency, public surface, the key absent from URLs, errors, serialized client and resources, and console output; signalling offer/answer with the token only), the built-package import by name (exports, internals not importable), a separate TypeScript consumer typechecking against the packed tarball (including rejecting `name: null` and a nonexistent `conversations`), and `npm audit` 0. Console: 86 tests, build, audit 0. Ruff (CI scope), `uv lock` 53 and `git diff --check` clean. No pre-`v4.0.0` api file touched; 0 vendor identifiers. **Live SDK HTTP smoke** (built package, local server, signalling on): agent create, list, get, update and delete; session create, get, transcript and end (null and no-offset timestamps preserved); rendezvous created; wrong signalling token 401; foreign agent 404; 422 readable; invalid key 401; key revoked in the console, then the **same client instance** 401; key in no error or server log. **Not executed:** a real-provider spoken call, and a browser-driven WebRTC exchange through `SignallingClient` (only its HTTP contract is tested). **Carried risks:** the SDK is unpublished (install from the repository); timestamps carry no offset; the server's null-on-non-nullable 500. The API limits documented at M12.7 apply unchanged. | Developers get a faithful typed client without the SDK inventing capabilities the API lacks. | [ADR-0055](../docs/adr/ADR-0055-developer-platform-productizes-the-m11-runtime-model.md) Amendment 4 (DP14); [sdk/README](../sdk/README.md); [developer API §8a](../docs/apis/developer-api.md) | SDK available from `sdk/`. | DL-0244 | 2026-09-21 | Technical Owner | **M12.7 verdict: PASS WITH DOCUMENTED RISKS. Developer API keys and the developer contract are built on the one existing M11 key model; no second key table, hash, middleware or auth path was created.** **Delivered:** `/v1/console/api-keys` list/create and `/{id}/revoke` (`api/console/api_keys/`, `routers/api_keys.py`); OpenAPI credential schemes and `x-voxvoice-surface` on every operation (`api/console/openapi.py`, installed at composition); `docs/apis/developer-api.md` (the external contract); console **API keys & docs** (`/developers`) and **Developer docs** (`/developers/docs`, reference generated from `/openapi.json`). **Discovered:** M11 `apikey` = id, org_id, key_hash (SHA-256), label, created_at, last_used_at, with no revoked flag and no prefix; the frozen `get_org` authenticates `x-api-key` per request with no cache. **Decisions:** (1) Reuse M11 wholesale: the frozen `generate_api_key`/`hash_key` create keys, and revoke deletes the `apikey` row (as LLD §8 designed), so the next request is 401. (2) **`dp_api_key_meta` not built** (ADR-0055 Amendment 3, superseding LLD §8 / DB §3.5 in that detail): creator, display prefix and a post-revocation record are not needed to create, list or revoke safely, a prefix of the secret only adds exposure, and a migration for audit metadata alone is not justified. Audit goes to the `voxvoice.console.api_keys` INFO log (key id, org, user; never key material). (3) Owner and admin only, list included (LLD §3); CSRF header on mutations; raw key only in the create 201, with `Cache-Control: no-store`; the console keeps it in component state until Done. (4) The OpenAPI document is annotated at composition (no frozen router touched): `ApiKeyAuth`, `SignallingToken`, `ConsoleSession`; surfaces developer, signalling, console and public; credential headers no longer duplicated as parameters. (5) The contract documents only real, callable developer routes. Conversations and analytics are console-only. It states plainly what is unavailable: no scopes, expiry, rotation, rate limits, pagination, session listing, barge-in (F-1), usage or cost, SDK, or hosted service. A test holds the document's endpoint list equal to the OpenAPI developer surface. (6) Console routing now honours the server's declared capabilities, as the frontend architecture already specified. **Validation, executed:** full suite **6844 passed**, 0 skipped/xfail/failed, under the CI warning flags (22 m 01 s; 6824 + 20 new), including E10-7, certification and freeze, and the M12.2–M12.6 suites. Backend tests: create once, stored as SHA-256 only, no key material in any column; list without key or hash, with last_used; immediate server-side revocation; missing and malformed keys 401; label validation; role matrix (viewer and developer 403 on list, create and revoke; admin and owner allowed); CSRF 403; key management needs a console session (an API key is refused); org isolation (foreign key revoke 404, org A key reaches no org B agent or session in 7 operations, and the reverse); the console cookie does not open the developer API; no key in logs, errors or other responses; OpenAPI security per operation; contract equals the OpenAPI developer surface; the docs page names only real operations; no credentials in docs; and one end-to-end developer journey from key to revocation. Console: typecheck, **78 tests** (10 new: list, one-time display and copy with no storage or URL, clipboard fallback, revoke with confirm, revoke error, loading and error, developer role, docs sections, OpenAPI reference filtering, reference error), build and `npm audit` 0. Ruff (CI scope), `uv lock` 53 and `git diff --check` clean. No pre-`v4.0.0` api file touched; 0 vendor identifiers; no dependency, table or migration. **Live HTTP smoke** (scratch DB, signalling on): create 201 no-store; list without secret; DB has hash only; key creates an agent, lists, starts a session and opens a rendezvous; foreign agent 404; foreign key revoke 404; missing and malformed key 401; revoke without CSRF 403; revoke 204, then the same key 401; OpenAPI has the three schemes; docs page served; no `vf_` in the server log. **Not executed:** a real-provider spoken call (credentials absent; unchanged from M12.6). Visual browser check not achieved (known headless-cookie limitation). **Carried risks:** the audit lines are INFO and are not shown by uvicorn's default logging; no durable key audit trail; no rate limiting on the developer API; F-1 unchanged. | The platform's existing credential becomes a usable, documented product surface without a second auth system. | [ADR-0055](../docs/adr/ADR-0055-developer-platform-productizes-the-m11-runtime-model.md) Amendment 3 (DP13); [developer API](../docs/apis/developer-api.md); [LLD §8](../docs/architecture/epic12/m12-lld.md) | API keys and developer docs live in the console. **Owner decisions pending:** F-1; whether a durable key audit trail (`dp_api_key_meta`) is required; developer-API rate limiting. M12.8 not started. | Accepted |
| DL-0243 | 2026-09-21 | Technical Owner | **M12.6 verdict: PASS WITH DOCUMENTED RISKS. Analytics is a read-only query layer over the conversation data, with a written contract; no second source of truth, telemetry writer or metrics store was created.** **Delivered:** `GET /v1/console/analytics/{summary, latency, timeseries, agents}` (viewer+; `range` = today, 7d or 30d, or `from/to` UTC days ≤ 92; filters `agent_id`, `source`), `api/console/analytics/service.py`, the console Analytics page (summary, usage over time, latency table, agents, outcomes; per-panel loading/empty/error; no chart dependency), and the contract `docs/architecture/epic12/m12-analytics-definitions.md`. **Decisions:** (1) **Sources only:** `session` (conversations, status, times, origin), `agent`/`dp_agent_state`, and `metric.turn` + `session.started` in `evt_event`. No table, event type, producer, writer, index, migration or dependency (ADR-0055 Amendment 2, DP12). (2) **Missing is never 0:** unmeasured latency is counted as `missing` and excluded from statistics; unfinished conversations have no duration; conversations the log never observed have unknown turns and are excluded from per-conversation averages; statistics over nothing are null; counts of nothing are 0. (3) **UTC only**, membership by `session.started_at`, half-open `[start, end)`, daily buckets including empty days; a conversation and its turns belong to its start day. (4) **Turns are `metric.turn` records**, counted as the M12.5 detail lists them; the producer's latency semantics are used unchanged (F-7). Percentiles are nearest-rank p50/p95 over observed samples. (5) **Not provided:** cost/tokens/provider usage (no authoritative metering), end reasons (not recorded; the session holds a lifecycle state only), barge-in rate (F-1). (6) Turns are selected by `IN` over the population rather than a join (no multiplication), with numeric fields extracted in SQL; durations and percentiles are computed in Python because they are not portable SQL across SQLite and Postgres. **Validation, executed:** full suite **6824 passed**, 0 skipped/xfail/failed, under the CI warning flags (21 m 07 s; 6793 + 31 new), including E10-7 and the M12.2–M12.5 suites. New tests cover exact aggregation against a population built to catch double counting, measured-0 vs missing, range boundaries and midnight, presets/custom/invalid ranges, filters, archived agents, tenant isolation on every endpoint (incl. events filed under the wrong org either way, foreign agent = missing agent 404, client-supplied org ignored), the role matrix, 401, read-only, and **F-7 end to end**: a real M11 conversation's `voice_to_voice_ms` reaches M12.5 and M12.6 unchanged, and without audio endpointing is missing at every step. Console: typecheck, **68 tests** (6 new: populated, not measured vs 0, filters, custom range, empty, loading/error/retry per panel), build and `npm audit` 0. Ruff (CI scope), `uv lock` 53 and `git diff --check` clean; no frozen api file touched; 0 vendor identifiers. Live HTTP smoke on a scratch DB: three developer-API sessions → 3 conversations, all recorded, 0 turns (genuine), voice-to-voice not measured, foreign agent 404, signed out 401, `/console/analytics` served. **Performance:** 45–140 ms per request at 5,000 conversations / 845,000 events on SQLite. **Finding:** SQLite plans the turn read from `ix_evt_event_type`, so cost grows with all `metric.turn` rows in the log; the bounding indexes belong to frozen schemas (M11 `session`, Epic 10 `evt_event`) and were not added. **Not executed:** a real-provider call, so the Analytics representation of live voice-to-voice (and F-7 live) is unverified; provider keys ABSENT from the environment (presence-only check; `platform/.env` not read). Visual validation of the Analytics page: not achieved (the headless screenshot reaches only the sign-in redirect, then Chrome hangs). **Carried risks:** the above, F-1 pending, Postgres not exercised (no driver installed). | Analytics as honest arithmetic over existing facts, with its semantics written down before anything consumes it. | [ADR-0055](../docs/adr/ADR-0055-developer-platform-productizes-the-m11-runtime-model.md) Amendment 2 (DP12); [analytics definitions](../docs/architecture/epic12/m12-analytics-definitions.md); [LLD §7](../docs/architecture/epic12/m12-lld.md) | Analytics live in the console. **Owner action:** one real spoken call per local-run §7a steps 1–5 (Conversations, then Analytics). **Owner decisions pending:** F-1; whether to amend a frozen schema for an analytics index when the log grows. M12.7 not started. | Accepted |

Provider credentials were ABSENT from this environment (checked by presence only; `platform/.env` deliberately not read), and a spoken call needs a person. The rendezvous registry is in-process (single worker; R-E12-5). | DL-0234 | 2026-09-20 | Technical Owner | **THE FIRST REAL VOXVOICE CONVERSATION RAN. Two genuine production defects were found by running it, both invisible to the entire hermetic suite.** A human operator spoke into a browser and heard a real answer: browser microphone → WebRTC → Deepgram → Silero → endpointing → Groq → clause pipeline → Cartesia → Playback Engine → WebRTC → browser speaker, locally, with every real provider. **Defect 1 — the transport sent 6× too much data, five-sixths of it stale memory.** `av` allocates a decoded plane far larger than the audio it holds: a 960-sample stereo frame arrives in a **23040-byte** buffer of which only **3840** bytes are audio, and `_to_audio_frame` did `bytes(planes[0])`, taking the whole allocation. The signal level stayed plausible — the real speech *was* in there — so every RMS check looked healthy while Deepgram recognized nothing and returned empty finals. Fixed by slicing to `samples * channels * 2`. **Defect 2 — `AudioNormalizer` normalized sample rate but not channel count.** `aiortc`'s Opus decoder is hard-coded to `layout = "stereo"`, so every browser frame is 48 kHz stereo while the session's canonical format is mono; Silero and Deepgram both correctly refuse non-mono audio, and the first turn died with `CapabilityUnsupported`. Downmixing was added to the normalizer — the one ingress conversion ADR-0044 **AD4**/**AR-41** permit — and averages rather than dropping a channel, so a microphone on one side of a stereo pair is not silently muted. **Neither was reachable hermetically:** every fake produced mono 20 ms frames with exact-length buffers, and the aiortc↔aiortc tests asserted only `channels >= 1`. Both now have regression tests built on stand-ins that reproduce the real shape. **Two configuration mismatches were also found and NOT papered over:** the schema's default agent language `en-IN` is not declared by `DeepgramSTT` (`("en",)`), and the configured Groq default `llama-3.3-70b-versatile` is unavailable on the account in use — both resolved per-agent through existing fields rather than by widening a declared capability or editing a default to make a smoke test pass. | Three of my diagnostic hypotheses were wrong — the credential, the conversion, the frame size — and each cost the owner a retry. What found the defect was instrumenting the wire and comparing `frame.samples` against `len(plane)`. Recorded because the lesson is the milestone's thesis: an architecture can be fully tested and still not work, and only running it finds out. | ADR-0044 AD2/AD4; AR-41; AR-12 | `providers/transport/aiortc_endpoint.py`, `runtime/pipeline/audio/resampler.py`, regression tests in `tests/test_webrtc_transport.py` + `tests/test_audio_foundation.py`, [`docs/runtime/local-run.md`](../docs/runtime/local-run.md) | Accepted |

(3) The NAT outlook is better than "host only" implied, but this does **not** remove the public-IP requirement for the documented Stage-2 deployment. **Deliberately not fixed:** choosing an ICE-server policy is an architecture decision, plausibly an ADR, and §22 of the milestone prefers zero production-code changes. | DL-0223 | 2026-09-20 | Technical Owner | **The `SdpExchange` seam is implemented as a bounded rendezvous on the existing Control Plane — not as a signalling server, and entirely as *additions*.** Four endpoints (`POST /v1/webrtc/rendezvous`, `GET …/offer`, `POST …/answer`, `POST /v1/webrtc/sessions`) in a new router, a new `api/webrtc/` package, and a new WebRTC deployment application (`api.webrtc.app`) that mounts the **unmodified** `agents` and `sessions` routers. `POST /v1/webrtc/sessions` **is** the existing session endpoint: it calls `api.routers.sessions.create_session` directly and carries the rendezvous claim beside it in a context variable that a controller wrapper reads between the session row being written and the runtime being started — so agent lookup, org scoping, persistence, the 502 mapping and the `session.started` webhook are the ones that already exist, not a second copy. **The runtime offers and the peer answers**, fixed by M11.16.3: the endpoint attaches its outbound track before negotiating. Over HTTP the peer is a client and cannot be pushed to, so the offer waits in a rendezvous the peer fetches — and the create call therefore **overlaps** the signalling exchange, because `connect()` returning means the connection is usable (AR-6) and it cannot be usable until the peer has answered the offer that same call produced. **No room, roster, presence, renegotiation, ICE-candidate channel, broker, database, TURN or SFU** (§5/§6): aiortc emits a complete non-trickle local description, so candidates ride the SDP and a second channel would carry nothing. **Signalling does not ride the org API key**: reservation mints a single-purpose token, returned once, stored only as a SHA-256 hash and compared in constant time, able to exchange one SDP pair for one session and nothing else. Every wait and every store is bounded — 64 open slots, 120 s TTL, 8 s answer, 5 s offer poll, 256 KB SDP (pinned equal to the transport's own bound by test). CORS exists **only** when origins are configured, never `*` outside dev, never with credentials. The Control Plane imports neither `aiortc` nor `runtime` nor `providers` — asserted by test. | The alternative shapes all cost more than they earn: a WebSocket adds a second protocol for one round trip; a broker or table adds infrastructure for a value that lives eight seconds; a trickle-ICE channel carries nothing aiortc does not already put in the SDP. | AR-6, AR-12, AR-14, AR-21, AR-25; [ADR-0043](../docs/adr/ADR-0043-m10.13-certification.md) CD7; [ADR-0049](../docs/adr/ADR-0049-provider-credential-injection.md) | `api/webrtc/`, `api/routers/webrtc.py`, 55 tests, [`docs/providers/webrtc-external-client.md`](../docs/providers/webrtc-external-client.md) | Accepted |
| DL-0222 | 2026-09-20 | Technical Owner | **R-34 is NOT closed, and the WebRTC path is only *partially* ready — infrastructure exists, deployment does not.** M11.16.3 supplies the three things M11.16.2 found missing: a first-party `PeerSignaller`, the `bootstrap.build_webrtc_media_endpoint` composition path, and an installed `aiortc` (by syncing the venv to the existing lock — **no dependency policy change, no version change, 53 packages before and after**). Two real `RTCPeerConnection`s now negotiate in-process and carry real PCM16 both ways through genuine SDP/ICE/DTLS/RTP. **That is hermetic real-WebRTC-stack evidence and is explicitly NOT `E11-15` evidence**: aiortc ↔ aiortc inside one process is not a Stage-2 external peer, and §23 forbids confusing them. **R-34 stays open** because its exact condition — a profile end-to-end runnable against a *browser* — is unmet: no `SdpExchange` implementation ships here and no browser client exists. What changed is that the profile is runnable against a real peer *at all*, which R-34's closure requires but is not by itself. R-54, R-58, R-63, R-66, R-68 and R-69 are untouched; `E11-12` and `E11-15` remain exactly as M11.16 left them. | Recorded because "we can now negotiate WebRTC" is the single most tempting sentence in this milestone to over-read, and the gap between *the stack works* and *it was deployed against a real client* is precisely the gap certification exists to police. | R-34; [ADR-0051](../docs/adr/ADR-0051-epic11-certification-control-set.md) CE5#15 | `E11-15` still NOT EXECUTED; Stage-2 + credentials + an `SdpExchange` remain outstanding | Accepted |
| DL-0221 | 2026-09-20 | Technical Owner | **A fourth WebRTC gap was found while closing the other three: `send()` could never have worked.** `AiortcMediaEndpoint.send()` read `self._outbound[session_id]`, and **nothing in the repository ever populated that map** — `connect()` set `_connections`, `_tracks` and `_seq` but not `_outbound`, and `_OutboundTrack` was an unused stub holding a queue and subclassing nothing. Every assistant frame would have raised `"session … has no outbound track"`. It went unnoticed because no test could reach it: the hermetic suites use a `MediaEndpoint` fake, and `AiortcMediaEndpoint` itself was only ever exercised by its import-availability guard (**R-33**). The endpoint now builds a real `MediaStreamTrack` subclass at call time (preserving the lazy-import boundary), attaches it to the peer connection **before** negotiation so the offer advertises sendrecv, bounds the queue at 64 frames beneath the Playback Engine's 400 ms (AR-40), and puts a sentinel on teardown so a track parked in `recv()` cannot outlive its session. | A latent defect that only a real peer could expose is exactly what R-33 predicted, and it is the strongest argument for this milestone existing: the outbound half of the media path had never run. | AR-40; [ADR-0044](../docs/adr/ADR-0044-audio-representation-resampling-and-media-buffers.md) AD4; R-33 | `providers/transport/aiortc_endpoint.py`; layer-B tests now exercise both directions | Accepted |
| DL-0220 | 2026-09-20 | Technical Owner | **First-party WebRTC signalling: two seams, and no signalling *server*.** `SdpExchange` is the deployment's — one async method moves an SDP out and brings one back over whatever channel already exists. `PeerSignaller` is first-party and complete — it owns the negotiation sequence, a transport-only state machine (`NEW → NEGOTIATING → CONNECTED → CLOSED`, `FAILED` reachable), SDP validation and a bound. **Rooms, membership, auth, TURN and an SFU were all deliberately not built** (§6): none is needed to obtain one peer connection for one session, and each would be architecture this milestone has no authority to add. **The local side offers**, because the endpoint attaches its outbound track first, which keeps `connect()` a plain await that either yields a usable connection or raises — the contract AR-6 depends on. Bounds reuse existing conventions rather than inventing numbers: negotiation 10 s (= `ProviderGuard.DEFAULT_CALL_TIMEOUT_S`), SDP ≤ 256 KB, outbound queue 64 frames. **AR-12 widened by exactly one module, inside the same plugin:** M11.5's own interface `negotiate(session_id, pc) -> track` has the signaller drive the peer connection, so it must construct `RTCSessionDescription`; keeping it library-free would have meant changing an interface this milestone must preserve. A second guard now asserts every aiortc importer lives in `providers/transport/`, so the widening cannot drift. | Signalling is control-plane and carries **no credential** — asserted by a test that the module contains no credential vocabulary at all. Full SDP is never logged: it carries host candidates and network topology nothing here needs to emit. | AR-6, AR-12, AR-40; [ADR-0049](../docs/adr/ADR-0049-provider-credential-injection.md); [P-ADR-0002](../docs/adr/P-ADR-0002-implementation-profile-epic11.md) | `providers/transport/signalling.py`, `bootstrap.build_webrtc_media_endpoint`, 28 tests, [`docs/providers/webrtc-signalling.md`](../docs/providers/webrtc-signalling.md) | Accepted |

| DL-0218 | 2026-09-19 | Technical Owner | **R-66 re-characterised, not closed: the `stt` stage is not *structurally unmeasurable* — it is measured against the wrong two instants, and the honest replacement needs a real recognizer.** [ADR-0053](../docs/adr/ADR-0053-stt-latency-is-measured-from-end-of-audio-not-from-the-endpoint-decision.md) proposes **SM1**: derive `stt` from `speech_end → stt_final` (*"how long after the recognizer had all the audio did it produce the transcript?"*) instead of `endpoint_decided → stt_final`. **SM2** keeps the metric chain untouched — no instant moves, no boundary is added, no second timing chain appears, and `voice_to_voice_ms` is unchanged; only which two existing instants the `stt` key subtracts changes. **SM3** leaves §A8's 150 ms number alone: the budget is not what is wrong. **SM4 is the limitation that stops this from resolving the blocker** — in a hermetic profile the fake recognizer finalizes on the first silent frame, so the re-scoped interval measures the harness's frame pacing, and the observed +193.6 ms **must not be quoted as an STT latency**. **SM5** states outright that ratifying SM1 does not by itself unblock E11-12. The ADR is **PROPOSED**: re-scoping how a Constitution §A8 budget is read is the Technical Owner's to accept, and §37 forbids changing §A8 silently. | Four alternatives were rejected explicitly, including the two that would have produced a green certification today: reporting `stt = 0` on a negative interval (fabricates a measurement, ADR-0038 TP5, and makes a well-behaved recognizer indistinguishable from an absent one) and re-stamping `stt_final` at the endpoint decision (destroys the only record of when the transcript actually arrived). | Constitution §A8; [ADR-0038](../docs/adr/ADR-0038-runtime-latency-taps-and-the-metric-turn-producer.md) TP3a/TP5; [ADR-0047](../docs/adr/ADR-0047-audio-driven-endpointing-and-metric-turn.md) ME1/ME6 | ADR-0053 awaiting ratification; R-66 stays **OPEN**, re-characterised | Accepted |
| DL-0217 | 2026-09-19 | Technical Owner | **The R-66 timeline, measured: STT finishes 46.5 ms *before* the endpoint decision, so the negative interval means the recognizer was early — not that anything is slow.** One controlled run, `t = 0` at true end-of-speech: `speech_end +0.0 ms` → `stt_final` **+193.6 ms** → `endpoint_decided` **+240.1 ms** → `llm_first_token +240.3 ms`. The derived `stt` key is therefore **−46.5 ms** and ADR-0047 **ME6** drops it. **This is a measurement-definition issue, not a runtime bug** (§15.9): §A8's wording *"STT final ≤ 150 ms **after endpoint**"* encodes an assumption about pipeline *shape* — that recognition begins once the endpointer has decided — which ADR-0047 **ME1** invalidated by making endpointing audio-driven. The recognizer now runs **concurrently with the trailing-silence hold** and is finished before the hold expires, so its work is fully absorbed and it is **not on the critical path at all**. That is the best outcome the architecture can produce, and the metric reports it as an absence. **ADR-0047 ME6 predicted this could not happen** — *"under ME1 the derived stages follow the real order, so the condition should not arise"* — and the measurement falsifies the prediction on every turn. | Recorded because the shape of the finding matters more than the number: a metric that reports *"ready 46.5 ms early"* as *"NOT MEASURED"* is the kind of defect that looks like a performance problem and is actually an accounting one. The ME6 guard is kept, and keeping it is exactly right — a guard is most valuable when the prediction that it would never fire turns out to be wrong. | [ADR-0047](../docs/adr/ADR-0047-audio-driven-endpointing-and-metric-turn.md) ME1/ME6; [ADR-0038](../docs/adr/ADR-0038-runtime-latency-taps-and-the-metric-turn-producer.md) TP3a; Constitution §A8 | Timeline is the evidence base for [ADR-0053](../docs/adr/ADR-0053-stt-latency-is-measured-from-end-of-audio-not-from-the-endpoint-decision.md) | Accepted |

Converting either into an accepted risk would have produced a green certification whose green came from not looking. **Consequence for M11.17:** two GOVERNANCE §10 release gates — *latency budgets met* and the runtime-tier verification — are not satisfied, so freeze and release cannot proceed on this evidence. | The temptation was to mark E11-12 SATISFIED because the headline I1 number passes comfortably, and to treat E11-15 as an accepted limitation. The control now asserts vendor **branching** across all three planes and sweeps vendor identifiers in the planes Epic 11 actually built. **E11-16** reported the Epic 10 certification and freeze modules as improperly modified. They *were* modified — and **ADR-0052 authorized it** at M11.2.1/M11.5.1, because both previously asked *"does the working tree differ from HEAD?"*, a question about today's checkout rather than about the commit M10.13 certified, under which any later epic authorized to touch a guarded path retroactively un-certified Epic 10. The control now admits that exception **by file and by authorizing decision** and asserts unconditionally the half that carries the meaning: both Epic 10 digests still reproduce. **A third defect was mechanical:** E11-10 counted the metric *buffer* as a second `metric.turn` producer because a substring scan matched a module that subscribes to exactly that type; it now asks the AST for `publish(EventType.METRIC_TURN, …)`. | Epic 10's own module states the rule this follows: *"a certification control that fails on authorized state is a defective control, not a discovered defect — and deleting the control instead would be certifying by omission."* Recording the corrections is what keeps that distinction auditable. | [ADR-0051](../docs/adr/ADR-0051-epic11-certification-control-set.md) CE12; [ADR-0052](../docs/adr/ADR-0052-certification-is-historical-not-a-permanent-tree-lock.md); AR-21 | `tests/certification/epic11.py` | Accepted |

Epic 10's `certify_epic10` is called in exactly **one** place — E11-16, to observe that its historical digest still reproduces — which CE2 permits as reading data rather than executing a foreign control, and a test asserts it stays exactly one. **The new outcome matters:** Epic 10's four outcomes had no way to say *"this control's evidence was never produced"* as distinct from *"the evidence could not be gathered"* or *"it passed"*. `NOT_EXECUTED` says it, and like `INCONCLUSIVE` it is excluded from the passing set, so a live control can never pass hermetically — asserted by a test that checks precisely that. Certification **reads and never writes** (CE4), also asserted structurally. | Without a distinct outcome, an unexecuted live control would have had to be recorded as either inconclusive (which misdescribes it — nothing was ambiguous) or satisfied (which would be false).
