| DL-0213 | 2026-09-19 | Technical Owner | **R-70 CLOSED: the conversation harness now waits on runtime facts, and there is no spin budget left anywhere in it.** M11.11 waited a fixed number of event-loop spins for a response. That is a bet on scheduling and an unwinnable one, because `SileroVoiceActivity` runs inference on a **worker thread** — the iterations between pushing audio and a turn finishing depend on the OS, not the runtime. Two facts replaced it, **both of which already existed**: the Turn Engine's own `turn.completed`/`turn.cancelled` events (awaited on *both* terminal phases, because a resilience test usually expects the turn to end by failing), and the VAD provider's own `buffered_ms()`, against which frames are now **paced** so the analysis lag is exactly one frame however many sessions interleave. One additive observability method was needed — `inference_failed(session_id)` — because once inference stops, `buffered_ms` never falls again: without it a dead worker and a slow one can be told apart only by waiting, which is a timeout pretending to be a diagnosis and cost seven tests ten seconds each. Timeouts remain **only as deadlines**: correctness comes from the fact being waited on, and the deadline exists so a stuck runtime fails instead of hanging. **Evidence: ten consecutive runs of the three conversation suites under 8-way CPU contention on a 10-core machine, 152 passed every time**, against 1-2 failures per run before. An AST guard asserts no `settle(spins=…)` survives in either harness. | The alternative — raising the spin count until it stopped failing — would have converted a real synchronization defect into a slower, rarer one, and the M11.14 metering flake would have come back on someone else's machine. | [ADR-0045](../docs/adr/ADR-0045-streaming-adapter-pattern-and-vad-execution.md) SD7; DL-0207 (R-67) | `tests/fake_conversation.py` (`TurnWatcher`, `vad_drained`), `providers/voice_activity/silero.py` (`inference_failed`), `tests/test_hardening.py` | Accepted |
| DL-0212 | 2026-09-19 | Technical Owner | **R-69 narrowed, not closed: durable-write failure is fully defined by ADR-0035 DW4 — and the consequence nobody had written down is that the in-process event bus goes *silent*.** M11.14 recorded live-session durable recovery as NOT DEFINED. Injecting the failure and reading the contract shows the *runtime* behaviour was never undefined at all: **DW4** says *"do not dispatch the failed event; no subscriber may observe an event that is not durable"*, so a write failure means the event reaches **nobody** — not the metric buffer, not the gateway, not operator tooling. The producer sees no exception (AR-31), `seq` is not rewound so a reader sees a gap, the failure hook fires exactly once per failed write, the session keeps talking, and shutdown still completes. **The runtime stays available; its observability does not** — and that is the operationally significant half, now documented and tested. It also has a testing consequence recorded because it will surprise the next person: *no harness that waits on a bus event can be used while a durable sink is failing*, because the awaited event is contractually forbidden from arriving. What remains open is **in-session replay only**: ADR-0035 states M10.6 implements no recovery, and the outbox ladder (ADR-0036) and recovery driver (ADR-0037) exist but are not driven from a live conversation. **No outbox was introduced** — that would have been the second durable queue §6 forbids — so R-69 stays OPEN, narrowed from *"undefined"* to *"defined everywhere except in-session replay, which is Epic 10's to define."* | Recorded because "not defined" was the wrong description of a behaviour three ADR clauses already specified, and because the silent-bus consequence is the kind of thing an operator discovers during an incident rather than before one. | [ADR-0035](../docs/adr/ADR-0035-durable-write-interposition-on-the-event-bus.md) DW4; [ADR-0036](../docs/adr/ADR-0036-outbox-retry-ladder-dead-letter-and-quarantine.md); AR-31 | [`docs/runtime/hardening.md`](../docs/runtime/hardening.md); `tests/test_hardening.py` R-69 block | Accepted |
| DL-0211 | 2026-09-19 | Technical Owner | **R-48 CLOSED: a degradation is an *episode*, not an event — coalescing taken from the repository's own `duplicate_suppression` semantic rather than invented as a rate limit.** M11.14 measured a dead VAD publishing one `auxiliary_disabled` **per inbound frame** — ~50/second/session — which rolled the 64-entry history in about 1.3 seconds and destroyed the record of everything else in the incident. Bounded recording, unbounded telling. `InterruptPolicy.duplicate_suppression` (*"collapse repeat confirms within one assistant utterance"*) already solved this shape, so degradations now key an episode by **`(capability, kind, provider)`**: the first occurrence publishes and is retained, repeats are **counted only**, and the episode ends when the capability demonstrably works again — at which point a new failure publishes again. **There is no rate limit, time window or sampling ratio, and therefore no number to tune**, which §5 explicitly required. `detail` is excluded from the key because it carries vendor text that varies per frame and would defeat coalescing for exactly the noisy failures that motivated it; the *first* detail is the one retained. Counts increment **before** any suppression decision, so coalescing can never make an outage look smaller than it was, and `is_active()` answers *"is this still happening?"* so suppression can never be mistaken for recovery. The recovery signal is reused, not invented: the breaker already treats a successful call as *"usable again"*, and auxiliary capabilities — which never reach `call()`/`stream()` — get `auxiliary_recovered()` from the VAD tap. **No recovery is ever announced**: one successful call is not health, and a `recovered` event would be the false recovery §5 forbids. | The tempting fix was a per-second cap. That would have required defending a number nobody measured, and would still have dropped the *first* event of a burst under load — the one that matters most. | Constitution §A10; AR-24; `runtime/interrupt/policy.py` (the precedent) | `runtime/faults/degradation.py`, `runtime/faults/guard.py`, `runtime/pipeline/pipeline.py`; `tests/test_hardening.py` R-48 block; 1 M11.4 test updated | Accepted |
| DL-0210 | 2026-09-19 | Technical Owner | **M11.14 closes no risk. R-47 to R-67 all remain; R-48 is now measured rather than merely suspected; two new risks.** **R-48** (sustained VAD-failure degradation noise) was deliberately *not* closed: the record is bounded (64 retained entries, un-truncated counts) but the **event rate is not** — a dead VAD publishes one `auxiliary_disabled` per inbound frame, which at the 20 ms media cadence is **50 per second per session** for the length of the outage. Bounded recording and unbounded telling are different questions, and only the first is solved. **R-56** and **R-61** (no mid-stream LLM / mid-utterance TTS failover) are confirmed *as designed* and counted at the vendor rather than asserted in prose. **R-54/R-58/R-63** stand: every test here is hermetic and live-provider resilience is **NOT EXECUTED** — no credential was available and §34 makes the live smoke optional. **R-68 (new):** failover is mechanism-complete and **unexercised in any deployed composition** — the composition root wires exactly one provider per capability, so rung 1 of the §A10 ladder has no chain to walk in production; it is proved at the guard, against a deployment that does not yet exist. **R-69 (new):** durable-write recovery *within a live session* is **NOT DEFINED** — Epic 10 owns an outbox retry ladder (ADR-0036) and a recovery driver (ADR-0037), neither of which is driven from a call, so a persistence failure during a conversation is reported through `on_durable_error` and not re-attempted for that session. Defining it here would have been the second retry mechanism §7 forbids. | Recorded because the three defects M11.14 fixed are all of one shape — a failure path that no unit test covered because each component behaved correctly in isolation — and R-68/R-69 are the two places where that shape could still be hiding. | Constitution §A10; [ADR-0046](../docs/adr/ADR-0046-media-path-fault-tolerance-and-provider-mechanism-reuse.md) FT-D5/FT-D6; [ADR-0036](../docs/adr/ADR-0036-outbox-retry-ladder-dead-letter-and-quarantine.md) | R-68/R-69 tracked into M11.15 | Accepted |
| DL-0209 | 2026-09-19 | Technical Owner | **Failure scope is a first-class design decision, and a response failure is turn-scoped — not session-scoped.** The §A10 evidence forced the question §6 asks: which of frame / utterance / response / turn / session / process does each failure affect? The answers are now documented as a matrix populated from *measured* behaviour, with two cells deliberately marked **NOT DEFINED** and the reason given rather than filled with a plausible guess. The load-bearing choice: an LLM, TTS or transport-send failure ends **this answer**, not the call. The capability guard has already typed the error and recorded the degradation, so the only remaining obligation is to retire the turn deterministically and let the caller speak again — ending the session instead would be exactly the process-wide recovery for a turn-local failure that §6 forbids. The converse also holds and is asserted: STT failure and a broken media path *are* session-scoped, because a conversation with no transcript and no audio path is not a degraded conversation but an absent one. Session-scoped failure routes through the approved state machine (§A4.4 forbids IN_CONVERSATION → FAILED, so a mid-call fault goes ENDING → ENDED carrying the cause), and is now a **clean** failure in the full sense: providers released, playback stopped, subscription unregistered, exactly one terminal event. | The alternative — treating every provider failure as fatal — would have made the system trivially "resilient" by ending calls, and would have been indistinguishable in a test suite from correct behaviour. Scope is what separates the two. | Constitution §A10, §A4.4; [ADR-0046](../docs/adr/ADR-0046-media-path-fault-tolerance-and-provider-mechanism-reuse.md) FT-D6 | [`docs/runtime/resilience.md`](../docs/runtime/resilience.md) failure matrix; `tests/test_resilience.py` (91 tests) | Accepted |
| DL-0208 | 2026-09-19 | Technical Owner | **Injecting failure into a *running session* found three defects that every component-level test had passed over — because each component was behaving correctly in isolation.** **(1) A failed turn made the session permanently deaf.** A response that failed skipped the endpoint reset and left the Turn Engine parked in `ASSISTANT_THINKING` for a turn that would never complete; the next utterance asked for that transition again, the engine correctly refused it, and *every later turn in the call* failed at its first transition — no transcript, no audio, no second degradation to explain it. One vendor hiccup cost the whole call, silently. `_respond_once` now retires the turn (`TURN_CANCELLED`, terminal metric released, endpoint reset) and the caller speaks again. **(2) A broken media path was indistinguishable from a hangup.** The transport emitted `transport.failed` on the event seam — correctly, since M11.5 — and then handed the runtime the *same* end-of-stream sentinel a hangup produces. `run()` reads exhaustion as *the caller hung up*, so the conversation ended `completed` and recorded nothing. `inbound()` now raises `ProviderStreamTruncated` when the path broke, and **only** then: a fix that turned every goodbye into an outage would be worse than the defect. **(3) A failed session never released its providers.** `fail()` terminalized without closing them and `end()` returned early when already terminal, so a failed call left its transport connected, its playback engine running and its metric subscription on a discarded bus. Both terminal paths now release, idempotently. | All three are the same shape: a *path between* correct components, invisible to tests that exercise components. It is the argument for resilience evidence being session-level rather than unit-level, and it is why M11.14 injects at the vendor seam rather than at the guard — raising a typed error at the guard skips detection and classification and asserts the rest against an input the runtime never has to interpret. | Constitution §A10; [ADR-0045](../docs/adr/ADR-0045-streaming-adapter-pattern-and-vad-execution.md) SD4; AR-6, AR-23, AR-24 | `runtime/pipeline/pipeline.py`, `runtime/orchestrator/orchestrator.py`, `providers/transport/webrtc.py`; 2 M11.5 tests strengthened | Accepted |
| DL-0207 | 2026-09-19 | Technical Owner | **M11.13 closes no risk; R-47 to R-65 all remain; new risks R-66 and R-67.** Measuring processing time is not measuring quality: **R-47** (VAD accuracy), **R-49** (endpoint thresholds), **R-52/R-53** (clause policy) and **R-60** (playback capacity) are untouched, because no evaluation dataset was introduced and no threshold was tuned. **R-54/R-58/R-63** remain because no live vendor call and no real WebRTC peer were executed. **R-66 (new, the important one):** the `stt` stage is **structurally unmeasurable** in the current turn shape and `llm` is not LLM cost. A recognizer finalizes when the caller stops speaking, before the 240 ms trailing-silence hold elapses, so `stt_final` precedes `endpoint_decided`, the `stt` interval is negative and dropped (ADR-0047 ME6), and the `llm` stage absorbs the remainder of the hold — Profile A reports `llm` p50 = 287 ms with a provider that returns *instantly*. Any future reader or dashboard treating `llm` as model latency will be wrong by that margin. **R-67 (new):** the harness must deliver inbound frames at real audio cadence; delivering an utterance in one event-loop turn makes every trailing-silence frame inherit the stale speech verdict from the last completed VAD window, so the endpoint never closes. That is an artefact of instantaneous delivery rather than a runtime defect, but it means any future load or concurrency harness that batches frames will produce nonsense unless it paces them. | Recorded because a latency table is the easiest place in an engineering document to imply precision nobody measured, and because R-66 in particular is a trap laid for whoever reads these numbers next. | [ADR-0038](../docs/adr/ADR-0038-runtime-latency-taps-and-the-metric-turn-producer.md); [ADR-0047](../docs/adr/ADR-0047-audio-driven-endpointing-and-metric-turn.md) ME6; [DL-0204](#) | R-66/R-67 tracked into M11.16 | Accepted |
| DL-0206 | 2026-09-19 | Technical Owner | **First I1 baseline: 110 warm turns per profile, 0 failures, every boundary stamped — and four findings, two of which are about the measurement rather than the speed.** Warm p50 (ms), hermetic / provider-simulated: `endpoint` 240 / 240, `stt` **NOT MEASURED** / **NOT MEASURED**, `llm` 287 / 374, `tts` 0 / 80, `egress` 0 / 5, **`voice_to_voice_ms` 241 / 447**. **(1)** `endpoint` ≈ 240 ms *is* the configured trailing-silence hold, because `speech_end` is backdated to true end-of-speech while `endpoint_decided` is stamped when the decision is made — it sits inside §A8's 250 ms budget **by construction**, which is not validation. **(2)** `stt` is negative every turn and dropped, and `llm` therefore absorbs the rest of the hold (R-66). **(3)** The decomposition is otherwise sound and the arithmetic closes: an injected 80 ms TTS delay moves `tts` 0 → 80, an injected 5 ms transport delay moves `egress` 0 → 5, and `endpoint` is unmoved by either. **(4)** **No Epic 10 telemetry overhead was detectable**: +0.3 ms p50 per ~1450 ms session across 27 sessions per arm, which is within this harness's noise — the honest reading is *not detected at this resolution*, not *0.3 ms*. **Nothing was optimized.** No threshold, buffer, provider or architecture was changed to improve a number. | The temptation in a baseline milestone is to fix what the numbers reveal. Findings 1 and 2 are both invitations to retune the endpoint hold, and both were left alone: measuring is this milestone, changing is not. | Constitution §A8; [`docs/performance/i1-baseline.md`](../docs/performance/i1-baseline.md) | `docs/performance/i1-baseline.json` | Accepted |
| DL-0205 | 2026-09-19 | Technical Owner | **The baseline reads the runtime's own `metric.turn` and adds no second timing chain; percentiles a sample cannot support are withheld rather than printed.** The harness (`platform/benchmarks/`, excluded from the installed distribution and imported by no production module) drives the **production** runtime end to end and consumes `metric.turn` through the M11.12 observability plane. A harness with its own stopwatch would have described the harness; reading the runtime's metric means these are the numbers production reports, and a broken tap surfaces as a **missing boundary** rather than as a plausible number. **Three profiles, named for what they are evidence of:** A hermetic (runtime cost only), B provider-simulated (delays the *harness* injects, to prove each lands in the stage the runtime attributes it to), C live — **NOT EXECUTED**, no credential. **Statistical discipline is enforced in code, not in prose:** nearest-rank percentiles (never interpolated, so a reported latency was actually observed), `p95` withheld below 20 samples and `p99` below 100 — printed as `—` with the threshold named — and zero-variance results explicitly labelled as the deterministic harness rather than a consistent system. Sample: 55 sessions × 2 turns = **110 turns**, the smallest sample making p99 a real rank, plus 3 warmup and the first session reported separately as cold. **Regression tests assert the instrument, never a speed** — no `assert latency < N`. | The alternative — a benchmark script outside the repository producing a number in a chat message — is how performance claims become folklore. Making the artifact machine-readable, revision-stamped and re-runnable is what lets the first live run be *compared* rather than argued about. | Constitution §A8; AR-42; [ADR-0038](../docs/adr/ADR-0038-runtime-latency-taps-and-the-metric-turn-producer.md) | `platform/benchmarks/**` (4 modules), `tests/test_i1_baseline.py` (24 tests), `docs/performance/**` | Accepted |
| DL-0204 | 2026-09-18 | Technical Owner | **M11.12 closes no carried risk; R-47 to R-63 all remain; new risks R-64 and R-65.** Wiring telemetry does not measure anything: recording that a threshold exists is not evidence that it is the right threshold, so R-49, R-52 and R-60 are untouched, and R-54/R-58/R-63 remain open because M11.12 made no live vendor call either. **R-64 (new):** **provider usage is not metered.** The only usage signal wired is `metric.turn` (per-turn latency), because it is the only one the frozen `MeterSource`/`MetricStreamPort` pair already has a decoder for (`MetricTurnBuffer`, M10.10). STT seconds, LLM tokens and TTS characters have `MeterSource` members but **no platform decoder**, and building one is a separately authorized step — Billing's own docstring says an unwired port means no ingestion, not invented usage. Anyone reading "metering is wired" should read it as *latency metering*, not *consumption billing*. **R-65 (new):** `metric.turn` is produced only for sessions on the M11.11 playback path (`streaming_playback=True`); a deployment on the legacy whole-utterance path is wired for durable events but produces no per-turn metric, because that path has no `tts_first_audio` producer. | Recorded because "telemetry and metering are wired" is exactly the sentence that would be read as "usage billing works", and it does not. | [ADR-0039](../docs/adr/ADR-0039-metric-stream-and-metering.md); [ADR-0023](../docs/adr/ADR-0023-metering-and-billing-boundary.md) M2; [DL-0200](#) | R-64/R-65 tracked into M11.13/M11.16 | Accepted |
| DL-0203 | 2026-09-18 | Technical Owner | **Metering is wired as a pull boundary: the runtime publishes `metric.turn`, the platform buffer decodes it, and Billing pulls — the runtime never calls Billing.** Billing's frozen `MetricStreamPort` is explicit that it is *"the only inbound path"*, read-only and pull-shaped, with *"no emit, publish, or write method — Billing is structurally incapable of producing an operational metric"*. So M11.12 wires **nothing into billing at all**: it subscribes M10.10's platform-owned `MetricTurnBuffer` (which lives outside `billing/**` precisely so Billing never subscribes to a runtime bus) to the session bus for `metric.turn` **only**. Three independent idempotency layers protect exactly-once accounting, and none is a substitute for the others: the lifecycle boundary does not produce the duplicate, `DurableEventLog.append` is idempotent on `Event.id`, and Billing ingests idempotently on `(event_id, meter_code)`. **No pricing, rates or money** — M11.12 records measurements; rating, invoicing and the ledger are Epic 9's and are untouched. **Provider consumption is deliberately not metered** (R-64): the frozen model has `MeterSource` members for STT/LLM/TTS but no decoder for them, and inventing one would be exactly the "invented usage" the port forbids. | The temptation was to push usage into the metering engine directly, which would have inverted a boundary Epic 9 designed to be uninvertible. Following the pull contract meant the entire metering wiring is one `subscribe` call. | [ADR-0039](../docs/adr/ADR-0039-metric-stream-and-metering.md) MB1; [ADR-0023](../docs/adr/ADR-0023-metering-and-billing-boundary.md) M2 | `runtime/orchestrator/orchestrator.py`; 9 metering tests | Accepted |
| DL-0202 | 2026-09-18 | Technical Owner | **No EventType was added: the frozen Epic 10 vocabulary already represented every runtime fact, and the mapping is asserted by test rather than described.** Session (`session.*`), transport reality (`transport.*`, AR-6), turn (`turn.*`), speech (`speech.*`), endpoint (`endpoint.*`), **barge-in (`interrupt.*` — no new event invented for it)**, assistant audio (`assistant.*`), provider (`provider.*`) and per-turn latency (`metric.turn`) all pre-existed. **One producer per semantic event** (AR-2): the existing `RuntimeEventBridge` maps each engine's native observer hook to exactly one type from one source, so no fact is emitted by two layers. **Correlation** reuses the envelope's own `session_id`/`conversation_id`/`turn`, stamped by the bus-bound publisher — no parallel ID scheme; two concurrent sessions sharing one durable log stay separable by correlation alone. **Ordering** is the bus's strict monotonic `Event.seq`, never wall-clock time, preserved verbatim by the log (ED3). **Duplicates are prevented at the owning lifecycle boundary**, not by dropping at persistence: repeated `stop()` yields exactly one `session.ended`. **No durable write per audio frame** — the stream is lifecycle-shaped, asserted by count against a turn's frame count, so storage never lands on the audio path. | The milestone's main risk was casually minting events. Mapping first and adding nothing is what kept Epic 10's historical certification semantics untouched. | [ADR-0032](../docs/adr/ADR-0032-durable-event-foundation.md) ED2/ED3; AR-2/AR-6/AR-32 | `core/events.py` unchanged; 34 wiring tests | Accepted |
| DL-0201 | 2026-09-18 | Technical Owner | **The runtime is connected to Epic 10 through one frozen value — `ObservabilityPlane` — built at the composition root, and every attachment is independently optional with `UNWIRED` as the default.** Epic 10 had already provided every extension point and none had a producer: `InProcessEventBus(durable=…, on_durable_error=…)` (ADR-0035 DW2/DW4), `MetricTurnBuffer.subscribe(bus)` (ADR-0039 MB1), and `TurnLatencyTap(publisher)` — whose `latency=` parameter on `RuntimePipeline` had existed since M10.9 and which **nothing had ever passed**, so `metric.turn` was unproduced in production. **A value, not a manager:** anything that *did* something would have been the second event bus, telemetry system or metering path the milestone forbids; this carries references and answers one question. **Layer discipline:** `runtime/` may not import `events`, `billing` or `observability`, so the plane names none of them — the attachments are structural Protocols and the composition root supplies the concrete objects, the same arrangement `LatencyObserver` already used. The caller was answered at the *next* utterance or at hangup; the first hermetic end-to-end conversation produced a user transcript and no assistant reply at all. **Fix:** `_close_audio_turn` schedules the response via the existing `_start_response` when a transcript is pending and no response is active. **Scheduled, not awaited:** this runs inside the VAD tap on the inbound path, so awaiting the LLM there would stall ingress for the whole turn — endpointing still invokes no LLM or TTS inline, which is the ADR-0047 constraint it was written to respect. `_start_response` consumes `_pending_text`, so a later segment cannot answer twice, and the Turn Engine independently refuses a second `ASSISTANT_THINKING` transition. | Category 1 under the milestone rule: without it the M11.11 path does not execute. | DL-0195 | 2026-09-18 | Technical Owner | **The `first_message` greeting is implemented as a bounded, cancellable pre-cache owned by the Playback Scheduler — never at bootstrap (ADR-0048 PB8, D-11).** `precache_greeting()` synthesizes the agent's greeting **when an active session asks**, holds the frames, and `play_greeting()` plays them **exactly once**, releasing them afterwards. | DL-0194 | 2026-09-18 | Technical Owner | **The playback buffer is bounded in milliseconds of audio and applies back-pressure; outbound speech is never silently dropped.** Capacity is **400 ms** (configurable), expressed in duration rather than frame count because a frame means different things at 8 kHz and 48 kHz and the thing being bounded is how far ahead of the caller's ear the runtime may run. A full buffer makes the producer **`await`** — never a synchronous block, which would stall the very event loop the transport needs in order to drain — so a slow transport propagates all the way back through synthesis instead of accumulating audio. A second ceiling of 256 frames bounds `OPAQUE` audio that declares no duration (the local profile), because a duration-only bound would be no bound at all for it. A frame longer than the whole capacity is admitted when the buffer is empty: refusing to speak is worse than exceeding the bound by one frame. **Only cancellation discards audio** (`frames_discarded` counts exactly that), which is the invariant separating a barge-in from silent loss — and the capacity is simultaneously what makes the barge-in flush *bounded* (PB6) rather than a best-effort drain. Both defaults are unmeasured (R-60). | M11.3 set the asymmetry — inbound may drop oldest, outbound must not — and M11.5's R-35 left the playback side open. | DL-0193 | 2026-09-18 | Technical Owner | **Playback lands as two provider-neutral components — Engine (mechanism) and Scheduler (policy) — and clause ordering is guaranteed structurally rather than by a check.** **Engine:** a single consumer task pulling from one bounded buffer, so frames leave in exactly enqueue order; it imports `core.audio` and the standard library **and nothing else** (asserted), so it cannot reach an LLM, a VAD, endpointing, a provider or the Epic 10 spine. Its route to the transport is **late-bound via `attach()`**, because the guarded transport behind the single egress normalizer is a *session* fact while the Engine is an ordinary object. **Scheduler:** a `ClauseSink`, so it plugs into M11.9's seam unchanged; clause *n+1* is not synthesized until clause *n*'s audio is fully handed over, so overtaking is impossible by shape — no parallelism was introduced, because it buys latency this milestone may not claim and risks the one invariant it must not lose. Two components rather than one because AR-50 forbids a module whose responsibility needs an "and". **Contract:** `TTSCapabilities.streaming_input` and `TTSProvider.synthesize_stream` are added **additively** (ADR-0045 SD5/SD6); `synthesize()` stays the abstract method, `LocalTTS` is untouched, and the default `synthesize_stream` **raises** rather than quietly falling back — deciding to adapt is the runtime's job, and a silent fallback would make the degradation invisible. **Integration:** `RuntimePipeline(playback=…)` defaults `None`, so the default response path is byte-for-byte M11.9's; when wired, the whole-input wait before synthesis is gone for that session (PB2), which is why PB2 is called discharged *for sessions that wire playback* rather than outright. **Metric boundaries mark real events:** `tts_first_audio` on the first frame that actually exists, `first_audio_out` after the transport accepted it — so back-pressure moves the metric and a silent response stamps neither. | The Engine could not be given the transport at construction without either the pipeline constructing it (hiding it from tests) or the Engine reaching into a session (breaking neutrality); `attach` is the smallest seam that avoids both. | Constitution §A8; [ADR-0048](../docs/adr/ADR-0048-llm-tts-clause-pipelining-and-playback.md) PB1/PB2/PB5-PB10; [ADR-0045](../docs/adr/ADR-0045-streaming-adapter-pattern-and-vad-execution.md) SD5/SD6; [ADR-0038](../docs/adr/ADR-0038-runtime-latency-taps-and-the-metric-turn-producer.md) | 2 new runtime modules, 3 new provider modules; 153 tests | Accepted |

M11.9 documents this rather than inventing a parallel mechanism, as the authorization directed. | Recorded so the milestone's limits are explicit. | DL-0190 | 2026-09-17 | Technical Owner | **The clause-boundary policy is explicit, bounded, and confirms a punctuation boundary one character late.** Boundaries: sentence-terminal punctuation (`.` `!` `?` `…` plus the danda and the full-width stops — a punctuation claim for the Indian-English market, **not** a Hinglish quality claim), newlines, an explicit end-of-stream flush, and a bounded-buffer overflow. A comma is deliberately **not** a boundary. **One character late:** a terminator only *marks* a candidate end and the next character decides — a closing quote or bracket extends it, anything else confirms it — because a sentence ending in a quotation mark ends at the mark, and emitting on the full stop hands the synthesizer a clause beginning with a stray quote. The cost is one token of delay for a mid-stream clause and **none** for the last clause, which `flush()` emits. **One narrow linguistic exception:** a `.` with a digit on both sides is a decimal point, so `3.5 units` and `1.2.3` stay whole; abbreviations are not handled (R-53) and that limitation is pinned by a test. **`min_clause_chars` defaults to 0 (off):** the first value tried (12) silently swallowed ordinary short sentences such as "I am fine.", withholding a complete clause from the speech path to satisfy an invented threshold. **Overflow emits rather than truncates** — the concatenation of every clause equals the whole input, asserted directly — because silently truncating a caller-visible answer is the worse failure by far. | Every threshold is named in `ClausePolicy` and none is a bare number at a call site (AI_AGENT_RULES rule 19). Each default that remains a guess is registered as a risk rather than described as tuned. | Constitution §A8; [ADR-0048](../docs/adr/ADR-0048-llm-tts-clause-pipelining-and-playback.md) PB1 | `runtime/speech/clauses.py`; 41 tests | Accepted |
| DL-0189 | 2026-09-17 | Technical Owner | **Streaming LLM lands as three layers behind the unchanged `LLMProvider` contract, and clause detection lands in `runtime/speech/` — never inside the vendor adapter.** **Provider:** `groq_client.py` is the only `groq` importer (AR-12, AST-asserted, lazy); `groq.py` is the first-party library-free provider (context validation, chunk normalization, typed failures, lifecycle); `completion_stream.py` is the neutral seam. | DL-0187 | 2026-09-16 | Technical Owner | **R-46 is closed by M11.8; R-47/R-48 and R-43/R-44/R-45 remain; new risks R-49/R-50/R-51.** **R-46 closed:** the turn/endpoint consumers no longer inherit text-era timing for the *endpoint* decision — endpointing is now duration-based, provider-neutral and driven by real VAD verdicts. **R-49 (new):** the thresholds (120 ms speech / 240 ms trailing silence) are **unmeasured implementation defaults**. 240 ms was chosen to sit inside §A8's *"endpoint decision ≤ 250 ms after true end-of-speech"*; conversational systems commonly hold 0.5–0.8 s to tolerate mid-sentence pauses, so this favours the documented budget over pause tolerance and needs measurement (M11.13) before it is called right. **R-50 (new):** when an STT `Final` arrives *before* end-of-speech, the transcript stays pending and is answered at the next transcript or at stream end, because endpointing runs inside the inbound audio path and may not start a response there (C: *do not directly invoke LLM/TTS*). With M11.6's multi-Final behaviour (R-41) the common case resolves on the next result; a turn-level response scheduler is M11.9/M11.10 work. **R-51 (new):** only *endpointing* became duration-based — Smart Turn arbitration and the interrupt engine still use their M3.x **frame-count** policies, which were tuned when a "frame" meant one text payload rather than 20 ms of audio. Nothing regressed (they are off or unchanged by default) but their units are now inconsistent with the endpoint layer's. **R-47 unchanged:** still no accuracy measurement of any kind — this milestone asserts composition and determinism, never detection quality. **R-48 unchanged:** per-frame degradation records on a sustained VAD failure; M11.8 deliberately did not alter that semantics. | Recorded so the milestone's limits are explicit. An endpointing milestone is exactly where a plausible-sounding "tuned for natural conversation" claim would be easiest to make and impossible to support without recordings. | [ADR-0047](../docs/adr/ADR-0047-audio-driven-endpointing-and-metric-turn.md); [DL-0183](#); [DL-0041](#) | R-46 closed; R-49/R-50/R-51 tracked into M11.9+ | Accepted |
| DL-0186 | 2026-09-16 | Technical Owner | **Minimum-scope defect fix: a terminal latency tap attributed every `metric.turn` to turn 0 whenever a Turn Engine was wired, so no metric was emitted at all.** `_respond` calls `turns.on_turn_completed()` before `_tap_terminal("completed")`; the completing transition **archives** the turn, so `_turn_index()` found no current context and returned `0`, while the turn's boundaries had been recorded under its real index. `TurnLatencyTap.complete()` then popped an empty key and published nothing. Invisible until now because M10.9's latency tests wire **no** Turn Engine (index 0 throughout) and the orchestrator does not wire the tap at all (M11.12 owns that). **Fix:** `_turn_index()` remembers the last active index and falls back to it, so a terminal belongs to the turn that just completed. Two turns still never share a key — the remembered index only advances. | M11.8's metric remap cannot be verified end-to-end while no metric is emitted end-to-end, so this is the minimum defect that blocks the milestone (authorization §L). Nothing else in the tap, the pipeline or the contract changed. | [ADR-0038](../docs/adr/ADR-0038-runtime-latency-taps-and-the-metric-turn-producer.md) TP6/TP8; AR-42 | `runtime/pipeline/pipeline.py` `_turn_index()` | Accepted |
| DL-0185 | 2026-09-16 | Technical Owner | **Audio endpointing is opt-in per session, refused without a VAD, and its thresholds are audio-duration constants — never wall clock.** `StartSessionRequest.audio_endpointing` → `AgentSpec.audio_endpointing`, default **False**, so every existing profile keeps M3.3 segment-driven behaviour unchanged. Selecting it with **no** voice-activity provider registered is refused at session start with `ConfigError` — the identical stance `_check_interruption` already takes for `allow_interrupt`, and the alternative (silently running segment-driven while an operator believes otherwise) is the accepted-but-inert configuration AR-25 forbids. **Timing:** every threshold accumulates the duration each frame *carries* (PCM16 → bytes ÷ 2 ÷ channels ÷ rate; an `OPAQUE` frame declares none, so the provider's declared `frame_ms` is the composition root's fallback). Nothing sleeps, reads a clock or depends on scheduling, which is what makes the tests deterministic under burst-fed audio. **Defaults:** `min_speech_ms=120`, `trailing_silence_ms=240`, `max_utterance_ms=30_000`, each documented with its rationale and all configurable (R-49 tracks that they are unmeasured). **Pre-roll: none, deliberately** — the VAD tap is pass-through, so STT already receives every frame from the utterance's first sample; there is no lost onset to buffer, and adding one would duplicate the transport's buffer for no benefit. | Opt-in is what lets a milestone that changes *when a turn ends* ship without altering a single existing profile's behaviour, and the refusal keeps the choice honest. Duration-based thresholds are the only way the same policy can hold across a 20 ms transport frame and a 32 ms Silero window. | AR-25; [ADR-0044](../docs/adr/ADR-0044-audio-representation-resampling-and-media-buffers.md); [ADR-0047](../docs/adr/ADR-0047-audio-driven-endpointing-and-metric-turn.md) | `core/session_control.py`, `runtime/contracts.py`, `runtime/service.py`, `runtime/faults/validation.py`, `runtime/orchestrator/orchestrator.py` | Accepted |
| DL-0184 | 2026-09-16 | Technical Owner | **Audio-driven endpointing lands as one new provider-neutral detector plus two additive signals; the `metric.turn` stage chain is remapped to the §A8 order with every key name preserved (ADR-0047 ME1–ME8).** **New:** `runtime/endpoint/audio.py` — `AudioEndpointDetector` (a four-state machine over `speech: bool` + `duration_ms`, O(1) state, no queue, no task, no audio buffered) and `AudioEndpointPolicy`. **Additive to existing subsystems, nothing redesigned:** `EndpointEngine.note_speech_end` (a neutral signal that confirms the decision), `EndpointPolicy.audio_driven` (a transcript segment records evidence but **cannot** confirm — two deciders would produce two `USER_FINISHED` transitions for one utterance), `EndpointCoordinator.on_speech_end`, `VoiceActivityCoordinator.last_sample` (read-only per-frame verdict; `observe()` still returns only net state changes), and `LatencyObserver.record(..., backdate_ms=)`. **Backdating is the load-bearing detail:** a threshold is only crossed *after* the fact, so stamping `speech_end` at recognition time would understate every `voice_to_voice_ms` by the entire silence hold; the detector reports how much audio ago the boundary was and the tap subtracts it from its own clock (TP7 intact — one clock, still monotonic). **Turn lifecycle:** `IDLE → LISTENING → USER_SPEAKING → USER_FINISHED` is untouched and remains the Turn Engine's; endpointing closes the *user* half and **never invokes LLM or TTS** — the response stays on the existing transcript-driven path. An utterance producing no transcript is retired as `TURN_CANCELLED` at the next utterance rather than wedging the engine in `USER_FINISHED`. **Metric:** chain reordered to `speech_end → endpoint_decided → stt_final → …` with names `endpoint`/`stt`/`llm`/`tts`/`egress` preserved (ME2, no version bump, no new EventType), plus an additive `speech_start` boundary and `user_speech` stage held **outside** the chain so the five stages still sum to `voice_to_voice_ms`; a segment-driven profile's now-inverted `stt` interval is dropped, never emitted negative (ME6). **Mutation-checked:** bypassed thresholds, one-frame silence ending a turn, duplicate start, duplicate end, inverted comparison, and an outage counted as silence each fail the test that names the property. | The detector is separate from the provider because *how much* evidence makes an utterance is a runtime policy identical for every vendor (AR-21), while *is this window speech* is the provider's one question (M11.7). Keeping the decision record in the existing `EndpointEngine` is what avoids a second turn state machine. | [ADR-0047](../docs/adr/ADR-0047-audio-driven-endpointing-and-metric-turn.md); [ADR-0038](../docs/adr/ADR-0038-runtime-latency-taps-and-the-metric-turn-producer.md) TP3a; [ADR-0045](../docs/adr/ADR-0045-streaming-adapter-pattern-and-vad-execution.md); [DL-0182](#) | 1 new module; 5 additive seams; `metric.turn` remap; 1 test file (77 tests) | Accepted |
| DL-0183 | 2026-09-14 | Technical Owner | **No VAD quality, latency or CPU result exists after M11.7, and none is claimed. New risks R-43…R-48.** The only real-library evidence is one functional run through the real provider on a 5.39 s macOS-synthesized speech clip (168 windows analysed, speech reported, 0 ms dropped, no task left) plus a direct model run (synthesized speech 120/168 windows ≥ 0.5; digital silence max 0.009; white noise max 0.030). That is proof the integration works, not a detection-accuracy result. **R-43** — licensing: every component is MIT (wrapper, Silero weights, ggml) or runtime-excepted (`libgomp`, GPL-3.0 + GCC Runtime Library Exception, Linux wheels only), but the wheel carries **only the wrapper's notice**; any redistributed artifact must add the Silero and ggml notices, and the bundled ggml is built from a modified tree (`0.9.4-dirty`) so its provenance is not byte-traceable to an upstream release. The Linux wheel also installs a stray top-level `lib64/` into site-packages. Not recorded as legal clearance. **R-44** — the model is 16 kHz only and the provider never resamples, so a session on the `silero` profile must declare `sample_rate=16000`; a 48 kHz session raises `CapabilityUnsupported` per frame (recorded, frame passes through) rather than being validated at session start. **R-45** — a native inference call cannot be interrupted: `stop()` returns without it and discards its result, but the worker thread lives until that call returns. **R-46** — the existing Turn, Endpoint and Interrupt coordinators and the `speech_end` latency tap now receive *real* verdicts through their unchanged M3.x/M10.9 wiring; their thresholds (engine hysteresis defaults of 1 frame) were tuned for text-payload VAD and are untouched, since tuning them is M11.8 endpointing work. **R-47** — no false-positive/negative measurement, and none on telephony-band, noisy or Hinglish speech. **R-48** — a sustained VAD failure (sticky inference failure or a wrong-rate session) records one degradation per frame through the existing M11.4 auxiliary path, which is truthful but noisy. | Recorded so the absences are explicit. The first real detector is exactly where a plausible-sounding accuracy or latency claim would be easiest to make and hardest to check. | [DL-0172](#); [DL-0180](#); [silero-vad.md](../docs/providers/silero-vad.md) | R-43…R-48 tracked into M11.8+ | Accepted |
| DL-0182 | 2026-09-14 | Technical Owner | **Real streaming VAD lands behind the unchanged `VoiceActivityProvider` contract with background inference and an O(1) `detect()`; VAD is explicitly not endpointing.** Three layers mirroring M11.5/M11.6: `speech_model.py` (the `SpeechModel` Protocol), `silero.py` (`SileroVoiceActivity`, first-party, library-free), `silero_model.py` (the only `pysilero_vad` importer, lazy). **Buffer:** a per-session `bytearray` bounded at 320 ms (10 × 32 ms windows); overflow drops the oldest audio in one C-level delete. **Inference:** one named task per session drains whole 512-sample windows, awaiting `run_in_executor` on a **single-worker executor owned by the session**. **`detect()`:** validate → append → set an `asyncio.Event` → return the latest verdict; no await, no loop, no model reference. **Startup state** `speech=False, confidence=0.0`. **Failures:** model load `ProviderUnavailable`/`ProviderTimeout` (a 10 s in-plugin deadline)/`CapabilityUnsupported`; format `CapabilityUnsupported`/`ProviderProtocolError`; background inference failure is **sticky** — every later `detect()` raises. **AR-23:** VAD `start()` is **not** routed through `ProviderGuard.call`, because M11.4 deliberately governs the auxiliary VAD path via `auxiliary_failed`, and a pinned M11.4 test requires the first VAD start-failure record to carry its fallback; the model-load deadline therefore lives in the plugin. **AR-24:** one additive optional core Protocol, `ReportsDroppedAudio.drain_dropped_ms()`; `VoiceActivityCoordinator.drain_dropped_ms()` relays it; the pipeline's VAD tap records a new `DegradationKind.AUDIO_DROPPED` when non-zero. No turn, endpoint, interrupt or metric code changed. **Bootstrap:** `voice_activity="local"\|"silero"` (default none, unchanged); `silero` checks library importability at startup and refuses without it, **no `LocalVoiceActivity` fallback**; conflicts with `include_voice_activity=True`; `speech_model_factory=` is the test seam. **Conformance:** `silero` registered in the shared VAD suite over a fake model, probed with PCM16 through `ProviderUnderTest.frame`; `test_emits_confidence_flag_is_truthful`'s pre-existing skip for real-score providers was replaced by an assertion (a real score must not only ever be the `1.0` sentinel), keeping the suite at 0 skips. **Mutation-checked:** synchronous inference in `detect()`, removing the bound, returning a stale verdict after failure, and declaring a score while reporting 1.0 each fail the relevant tests within 0.2 s; `BlockingModel` refuses to run on the main thread so a synchronous-inference mutant fails rather than deadlocks. 25 consecutive runs of the VAD tests were stable. | ADR-0045 SD7/SD8 are satisfied by construction rather than by review. A thread per session keeps model state serial and prevents one session's backlog from starving another. Recording overflow through a structural Protocol keeps `providers/` free of `runtime` (AR-11) while still making the drop observable (AR-24). No capability fields were added: nothing in the runtime reads a sample-rate or window field yet. | [ADR-0045](../docs/adr/ADR-0045-streaming-adapter-pattern-and-vad-execution.md); [ADR-0046](../docs/adr/ADR-0046-media-path-fault-tolerance-and-provider-mechanism-reuse.md); [DL-0013](#); [DL-0177](#); [DL-0179](#) | 3 provider modules; 1 core Protocol; 1 `DegradationKind`; coordinator relay; pipeline tap record; bootstrap VAD profile; conformance hook + confidence assertion; 4 test files | Accepted |

| DL-0179 | 2026-09-14 | Technical Owner | **Real streaming STT lands behind the unchanged `STTProvider` contract, on a vendor-neutral session seam, selected explicitly with no fallback.** Three layers mirroring M11.5: `providers/stt/transcription_session.py` (Protocols `TranscriptionClient`/`TranscriptionSession`, neutral `FailureKind`, `StreamSpec`, `TranscriptionResult`); `providers/stt/deepgram.py` (`DeepgramSTT`, first-party, library-free); `providers/stt/deepgram_client.py` (the **only** Deepgram SDK importer, lazy). **Duplex:** an owned upload task pulls → validates → sends one frame at a time while the generator yields results concurrently; empty frames never sent. **Failures:** adapter classifies handshake statuses and close codes into `FailureKind`, provider maps onto `core.errors`; vendor exceptions discarded `from None`. **Cleanup:** upload task cancelled and awaited via `asyncio.wait` on every exit, session closed idempotently. **Bootstrap:** `build_registry(stt="deepgram", credentials=…)` resolves exactly `deepgram_api_key` through the ADR-0049 `CredentialProvider` and passes the resolved value (CR3/CR4); the streaming-proof test was restructured after the batch mutant exposed that it would *hang* rather than fail. **No core contract changed.** | Same seam discipline M11.5 proved for aiortc: the SDK behind one door, every behaviour provable without a network. Refusing rather than downgrading follows DL-0175's rule — an operator who selected real STT and silently got the echo transcriber would be misinformed about their own configuration. | DL-0175 | 2026-09-07 | Technical Owner | **AR-6 discharged: four additive transport-reality EventTypes, emitted only for facts that actually happened.** `transport.connected`, `transport.media_ready`, `transport.disconnected`, `transport.failed` — additive under AR-32, and **deliberately not** folded into `SESSION_*` (which describes what the Orchestrator *requested*) or `PROVIDER_*` (registry resolution, plus M11.4 degradation — a third meaning would have made the type useless). **The reality rules, each separately tested:** `MEDIA_READY` fires on the **first real inbound frame**, never on `start()`, because a connection can complete with nothing flowing; `DISCONNECTED` and `FAILED` are decided by *how the media path ended* — the endpoint's iterator returning normally is a hangup, raising is a fault — and an **unmapped library exception is still `FAILED`**, never ordinary exhaustion, because the pipeline reads exhaustion as caller hangup (ADR-0045 SD4). Cancelling `start()` emits **nothing**. `LocalTransport` does not implement `ObservableTransport` and emits none of these, so the loopback never claims a connection it does not have. **Verified:** with the four members added, the Epic 10 certification digest still reads `7e57bc14764201b1…` — M11.5.1's repair working exactly as designed. | AR-6: *"any component learning of a real-world lifecycle fact (hangup, connect, failure) must emit the event that drives the state machine — status may never be inferred-only from API calls."* The runtime **relays** what the transport observed and never synthesises a fact from a call it made itself, which is the inversion AR-6 exists to prevent. The observer seam is a `core` Protocol so the plugin emits without importing `runtime` (AR-11), and the kind→EventType mapping lives in the runtime's event bridge so the plugin never learns the event vocabulary. | AR-6; AR-32; [ADR-0052](../docs/adr/ADR-0052-certification-is-historical-not-a-permanent-tree-lock.md) Amendment 1; [DL-0173](#) | 4 EventTypes; `core.providers.transport` observer seam; event-bridge mapping; orchestrator relay | Accepted |

The import is **lazy** (inside the constructor) because the tree carries native extensions; that keeps `providers.transport` importable, and the deterministic tests runnable, with no library present. **Pipecat remains retired** — zero imports in the production path, asserted. | A media library is commodity plumbing (ADR-0002's own reasoning); an orchestration substrate is architecture. | DL-0173 | 2026-09-07 | Technical Owner | **M11.5.1 — the third form of the ADR-0052 defect is closed: certification controls no longer derive digest inputs from live-tree counts. No digest re-baselined.** **The blocker.** M11.5 needed four additive `EventType` members for AR-6 session-reality events; adding them moved Epic 10's published certification digest `7e57bc14764201b1…` → `1b65916c39c21a21…` **while every control still reported `satisfied`**. Cause: `examined` is a digest input (`{control, outcome, violations, examined}`) and **E10-5** computed it as `len(current)` — a count of **live** `EventType` members. M11.2.1 had fixed the *git-diff* and *digest-recomputation* forms of this defect; the *counting* form survived. **Audit result:** three controls derived `examined` from a live scan — **E10-5** (EventType members, blocking now), **E10-3** (`evt_` table/index names, latent — would block any future events table), **E10-6** (Billing modules, effectively inert while `billing/**` is frozen, but the same shape). | DL-0146 | 2026-08-22 | Technical Owner | **M10.14 — Epic 10 Freeze. Governance/freeze evidence, not a production architectural change — so no ADR-0044 is required.** Owner rulings **R-1…R-7** at the M10.14 decision gate, recorded here in full. **R-3 (ADR vs decision log):** DOCUMENTATION_RULES §3.1 triggers an ADR on *"any architectural decision or change"*, and the M10.14 pre-flight found **every** architectural category negative — no new production behaviour, API surface, database change, migration, event type, provider integration, performance requirement, certification control, operational control or security/privacy change. The trigger therefore does not fire, and this entry follows the **M9.25 / DL-0136** precedent: a repository-wide search confirms **no ADR mentions M9.25**; Epic 9's freeze was carried entirely by its decision-log entry. Roadmap §4 DoD requires an ADR only *"if the change is architectural"* and a DECISION_LOG entry *"for notable non-ADR decisions"* — both discharged. **R-5 (module location):** M10.14 creates an **independent Epic 10 freeze manifest under `tests/freeze/`**, adjacent to but separate from Epic 9's `tests/freeze/manifest.py`, which is **not modified, extended, imported, wrapped, re-exported or invoked**; Epic 9's freeze implementation and artifacts remain untouched. The exact path is chosen at implementation but must carry a clear independent module identity — the ADR-0043 **CD2** pattern, already proven by `tests/certification/epic10.py`. **R-1 (historical digest):** Epic 9's manifest digest is **quoted from `v4.0.0`** as `eed692224f0aeb82c1a64e8edb39b3b553a709c67cf5d37ae50090bcb466b65c`; the current post-M10.10 value `cdafb4ab6cba00bcaed0c9b6a3ae477253ce66e492b5b743c9835072704d1fb7` may be recorded **separately** for state distinction. M10.14 **must never recompute, mutate or replace** Epic 9's historical manifest digest — the two-state treatment ADR-0043 **CD6** ratified and control **E10-10** already implements. **R-4 (verdict vocabulary):** Epic 10 owns its own freeze verdict vocabulary — **`FROZEN`**, **`FROZEN_WITH_ADVISORIES`**, **`NOT_FROZEN`** — and does not import or reuse Epic 9's. **Incomplete or `INCONCLUSIVE` evidence must never produce `FROZEN`** (the ADR-0043 CD4/CI6 and Epic 9 `PASSING_OUTCOMES` rule: a control that could not look has not passed). The certified M10.13 state — verdict `certified`, zero blocking violations, zero advisories, zero inconclusive — is expected to produce **`FROZEN`** if all freeze evidence remains satisfied. **R-2 (commit semantics):** roadmap §M10.14 (*"Freeze does not commit — that is M10.15"*) and blueprint §21 (*"M10.14 freezes, M10.15 commits and tags"*) bind. M10.14 records the commit state **at the freeze boundary** and **does not commit its own artifacts**; **M10.15 owns commit, tag and release**. The `commit_state` representation must distinguish the **already-committed M10.13 implementation at `db8c508`** from the **intentionally uncommitted M10.14 freeze artifacts at freeze time** — two facts Epic 9's single classification cannot express. The freeze state is **historical evidence of the M10.14 freeze boundary, not a permanent present-tense claim** once M10.15 commits, exactly as Epic 9's manifest still declares `commit_state="uncommitted"` today. **R-6 (documents):** M10.14 creates **no release document, no release notes and no `v5.0.0` tag**; **M10.15 owns release documentation and the release/tag operation** (§M10.15: *"Release notes state plainly whether any advisory was fixed or limitation closed"*). A freeze evidence document may be created **only** to make the verdict and attestation human-readable and reproducible; the primary deliverable remains the **machine-readable freeze manifest and its verification suite**. **R-7 (ruff scope):** the manifest records ruff cleanliness **together with the exact permitted verification scope** — `ruff check . --exclude voicebot` and `ruff check billing/ tests/`, both verified clean — and **never claims repository-wide cleanliness**, which is false: `ruff check .` reports two **pre-existing** findings in `voicebot/bot.py` (I001, F401). `voicebot/bot.py` is **outside M10.14 scope and must not be modified**. | **The freeze principle is "freeze the certified state; do not improve the architecture during the freeze,"** and three of the seven rulings exist to keep M10.14 from quietly doing more than that. **R-3 is the one that shaped the milestone.** Epic 10 had ratified an ADR before implementation at nine consecutive milestones (ADR-0035…ADR-0043), so the reflex was ADR-0044 — but the reflex is not the rule. DOCUMENTATION_RULES §3.1's trigger is an *architectural* decision, and a freeze introduces no production module, table, migration, route, port method or runtime behaviour: it reads the tree M10.13 certified and writes down what is there. Epic 9 reached the same conclusion at M9.25 and recorded it as DL-0136 with no ADR, and blueprint §21 instructs Epic 10 to follow that freeze → release split. Writing an ADR for a milestone that decides nothing architectural would have inflated the ADR sequence and, worse, implied that freezing is a design act. **R-1 and R-7 are both about not overclaiming.** The Epic 9 manifest digest moved at M10.10 under an authorized re-baseline, so *"quoted as history, never recomputed"* has two candidate values; quoting the current one as history would falsify the record, and reverting the current artifacts to restore the original would undo an authorized change to make a document read better — the inversion ADR-0043 CR5/CR6 already rejected. Quoting `v4.0.0` and recording the current value separately keeps both true. Likewise `ruff check .` is **not** clean, and Epic 9's manifest records a bare `ruff_clean=True` with no scope; repeating that shape would have inherited a real weakness, against the same manifest's own stated principle that *"a manifest that pretended to derive them would be claiming a reproducibility it does not have."* Recording the command alongside the boolean costs one field and removes the overclaim. **R-2 is where Epic 10 differs from Epic 9 in fact but not in rule.** D-11 (*"implementation exists but is uncommitted at freeze"*) is **already discharged** for the implementation: every milestone M10.5–M10.13 took its own commit, so the certified tree is committed at `db8c508` — unlike Epic 9, whose implementation was uncommitted until M9.26. That discharge is **not** a licence to pull M10.15's commit into M10.14; the split is the roadmap's instruction regardless of why it exists, so M10.14's own artifacts stay uncommitted and the manifest says so plainly rather than rounding the two states to one. **R-4 and R-6 keep ownership straight.** Epic 9 reached `FREEZE_WITH_ADVISORIES` because `NS-5`/`ST-3` are open against Billing; those advisories remain open and remain **Epic 9's**, and Epic 10 asserting its own clean `FROZEN` neither closes them nor re-owns them — the same honest-inheritance discipline ADR-0043 CD10 applied to LIM-7. And a freeze that shipped release notes would be doing M10.15's job under cover of governance work, which is the ownership theft a freeze must not perform. **One divergence from Epic 9 is recorded rather than smoothed over:** Epic 9 produced its release document *at* the freeze milestone (DL-0136 lists it, plan §16 placed it at M9.25), whereas R-6 defers it to M10.15. Blueprint §21's *"exactly"* is scoped by its own sentence to the freeze → release **split**, not to artifact placement, and Epic 10's plan has no §16 equivalent while §M10.15 explicitly places release notes there — so Epic 10's own roadmap governs. **This entry records the rulings only — no M10.14 implementation exists.** | [ADR-0043](../docs/adr/ADR-0043-m10.13-certification.md) (CD2, CD4, CD6, CD10, CD16, CI6, CR5, CR6, E10-10), DL-0136 (M9.25 freeze precedent), DL-0139, DL-0141, [DOCUMENTATION_RULES](DOCUMENTATION_RULES.md) §3.1/§3.7, [GOVERNANCE](GOVERNANCE.md) §10, [epic10 implementation plan](../docs/roadmap/epic10-implementation-plan.md) §1/§4/§M10.14/§M10.15, [durable-event-telemetry-spine blueprint](../docs/architecture/epic10/durable-event-telemetry-spine-blueprint.md) §21, [epic9 freeze](../docs/architecture/epic9/freeze.md), `platform/tests/freeze/manifest.py`, `platform/tests/certification/epic10.py` | **M10.14 is bounded by these rulings.** IN SCOPE: an independent Epic 10 freeze manifest, its verification suite, an explicit frozen-boundary attestation, a fresh Epic 10 freeze digest, the historical Epic 9 manifest digest quoted from `v4.0.0`, an Epic 10-specific freeze verdict, this decision-log record, and the exact ruff-clean scope. OUT OF SCOPE: Epic 9 freeze/certification/release artifacts · M10.13 certification (consumed as evidence only, unchanged) · migrations (`0034/0035/0036` holds) · production code · `platform/billing/**` · `platform/replay/**` · observability · release notes · the `v5.0.0` tag · any commit, tag, push, staging, amend or rebase · M10.15 · Epic 11 · Epic 12 · `voicebot/bot.py` · unrelated cleanup or formatting. **DL-0139 stays `Resolved`, discharged at M10.10, and is not reopened; DL-0141 stays `Resolved`.** Gate sequence: **1** governance ruling (complete) → **2** this entry → **3** implementation → **4** validation → **5** commit, which **R-2 defers to M10.15** and which is therefore not an M10.14 operation at all. no transport-specific exception hierarchy, no import of `platform/tool`'s taxonomy into `events/`, and **no built-in rule** for ambiguous auth/configuration failures. **E-6** append-only **`evt_outbox_failure`** (surrogate PK, indexed `event_id`, no FK, `attempt`, `outcome`, `occurred_at` from the injected clock, error detail), N rows per event, no update/delete API. **E-7** **no operator redrive** — `DEAD_LETTERED` is terminal. **E-8** **no operator-driven quarantine** and no `DEAD_LETTERED → QUARANTINED`; `QUARANTINED` is terminal. **E-9** K-3 architecture — pure I/O-free `OutboxLadder` + `DeliveryOutcome` + a `DeliveryTransport` **contract only**, no concrete transport shipped. **E-10** `platform/events/webhooks.py` stays **byte-for-byte unchanged**; its pre-existing `json.dumps` behaviour is recorded as deferred, not fixed. **E-11/E-12** migration `0036` with `ALTER TABLE` authorized, adding exactly `attempt_count`, `next_attempt_at`, `dead_lettered_at`, `quarantined_at`, `quarantine_reason` — two explicit terminal timestamps, **no** generic `terminal_at`, **no** `last_error`. **E-13** ratify ADR-0036 before implementation. **E-14** see DL-0144. | The gate established from repository evidence, not convention: `core.fsm` already provides a validated transition table with derived terminality and loud refusals, and the persisted-FSM precedent states its own rule — *"What it **refuses** is the specification"*; `BillingReconciliationRow` supplies the append-only-evidence shape, and one row per event provably cannot hold N attempts, which is exactly what ED8's *"failure history retained"* requires; the only failure taxonomy lives in `platform/tool/**`, a plane `events/` may not import, and every in-repo retry precedent takes retryability as a **caller-supplied predicate**; | DL-0142 | 2026-08-18 | Technical Owner | **ADR-0035 ratified at the M10.6 decision gate, before implementation — Owner rulings D-1…D-6 recorded.** The M10.6 design gate reported **STOP — OWNER DECISION REQUIRED** on three entangled questions ADR-0032 left open, and the Owner ruled **APPROVED** on all six items: **D-1 (T3)** for a producer with no originating durable transaction, the durable append **is** the state change — **ED6 is not amended, weakened, exempted or superseded**, still binds literally where a transaction exists, `append_many` remains the all-or-nothing primitive, and **no database, `UnitOfWork`, transaction or persistent state enters `runtime/` or the voice engines**; **D-2 (I3)** an **additive, default-disabled** persistence seam inside `InProcessEventBus`, writing **after `seq` stamping and before enqueue and before any subscriber can observe the event**, preserving the sole sequence authority, the `EventBus` contract, reentrant publish, FIFO dispatch and subscriber isolation, with **no change to `core.events.EventBus`** and **no producer changes** — `bus.py` may import `DurableEventLog` from `events/ports.py` but never `sqlmodel`, `sqlalchemy` or `events.persistence`; the subclass fallback I2 is **rejected**; **D-3 (F4)** a failed durable write never raises into the producer, is not dispatched, does not rewind or reuse its `seq`, returns the stamped envelope, and surfaces through an injected typed hook with bounded in-process accounting — **no retry, backoff, DLQ, quarantine or durable failure-history at M10.6**; **D-4** immutable `evt_event` is separated from a mutable **`evt_outbox`** (one row per event, `event_id` reference, no second identity, no competing sequence authority, in-process synchronous subscribers **not** destinations, ED9's `un-drained` predicate provided, additive expansion left for M10.7); **D-5 (R2)** EV5 is proven by a hermetic **test-only** compound producer writing both rows through the same `EventDatabase.session` and rolling both back; **D-6** M10.6 delivers the seam, structure, persistence, migration, tests, ADR and boundary validation but **must not modify `runtime/orchestrator/orchestrator.py` or wire the durable sink into the runtime**. | The gate established from repository evidence — not from general outbox-pattern assumptions — that ED6's premise was absent: `platform/runtime/**` has no `Database`, `Session` or `UnitOfWork`, so the producers ED6 governs hold no transaction; `InProcessEventBus.publish()` stamps `seq` and drains to completion in one method, so a decorator can only persist an unstamped event (breaking ED3) or persist after dispatch has already run (breaking ED6); and `core.events.EventBus.publish` contractually **"must not raise into the producer"**, which together with ED2/ED6 left the failure path unresolved. The ordering answer is this repository's own precedent rather than convention: `SqlUnitOfWork.commit()` already **"commit[s] the DB transaction first; publish[es] events only after durable success"** and discards staged events on rollback. Ratifying **before** implementation follows DOCUMENTATION_RULES §3.7 and the Epic 10 plan's own instruction that a persistence seam on `InProcessEventBus` **"requires a new ADR, ratified before implementation."** ADR-0035 **completes ADR-0032 where ADR-0032 is silent and supersedes nothing** — no invariant was weakened to make M10.6 executable. | [ADR-0035](../docs/adr/ADR-0035-durable-write-interposition-on-the-event-bus.md); [ADR-0032](../docs/adr/ADR-0032-event-durability-and-delivery-semantics.md) (immutable, ED1–ED12 preserved); [ADR-0033](../docs/adr/ADR-0033-metric-stream-seam.md) / [ADR-0034](../docs/adr/ADR-0034-durable-storage-seam-core-only-packages.md) (unchanged); DL-0017; DL-0141; [Epic 10 plan](../docs/roadmap/epic10-implementation-plan.md) §M10.6 | ADR-0032, ADR-0033 and ADR-0034 unchanged; AR-31, AR-32 and DL-0017 preserved; `core.events.EventBus` untouched; no producer changed; `platform/runtime/**` unchanged; migrations `0001`–`0034` unchanged; `platform/billing/**` byte-for-byte unchanged; `v4.0.0` not re-tagged. **Declared M10.6 limitation:** a durable-write failure leaves the event neither durably recorded nor delivered, and the in-process failure record does not survive process termination — **M10.7** closes this gap. **M10.6 provides the durable write-side capability and proves its semantics, but the production runtime is not yet wired to the durable sink.** M10.7 owns retry/DLQ/quarantine; M10.8 owns recovery; M10.5 retention stays deferred behind M10.10 per DL-0141; M10.10 owns the metric seam. This entry records ratification only — **no M10.6 implementation exists**. | Accepted |
| DL-0141 | 2026-08-18 | Technical Owner | **M10.5 (Retention) is `DEFERRED / RE-SEQUENCED`, not cancelled.** The M10.5 design gate established that the approved retention guarantee cannot be implemented truthfully at its documented position. Retention is **time-based over a closed window**, and `evt_event` already carries an indexed `recorded_at` that could define one — but the two **guards** cannot be evaluated. ADR-0032 **ED9** and ADR-0034 **DS13** require that retention *"never deletes an un-drained event"* and *"never deletes an un-metered event"*, verified by **EV8** and **SV9**; the **drained** guard needs the platform-owned metric buffer's consumption state surfaced through `MetricStreamPort.drain()` (**M10.10**) — **CORRECTED at M10.5 (2026-08-20) by [ADR-0041](../docs/adr/ADR-0041-retention-guards-drained-by-outbox-and-the-metered-watermark.md) RT1/RT5: the authoritative drained predicate is the ADR-0035 DW5 outbox predicate `evt_outbox.state == DELIVERED` (M10.6), not metric-buffer consumption; `DEAD_LETTERED` and `QUARANTINED` are terminal but not drained. This clause reflected ADR-0035 DW11's since-overruled premise that the buffer is an outbox destination, which ADR-0039 MB1 reversed; ADR-0035 and ADR-0039 are unamended. The rest of this entry stands as written, and this deferral remains Open**, the **metered** guard needs Billing's ingestion state keyed `(event_id, meter_code)` (**M10.10**, Billing side frozen), and delivery/consumption state generally needs the transactional outbox (**M10.6**). The M10.2 foundation contains **neither** — verified by inspection: `evt_event` has no delivery, drain or metering column, and `grep -rniE 'drain\|metered\|delivered\|consumed'` over `platform/events/` returns only unrelated queue-drain docstrings in `bus.py`. **Deleting solely by `recorded_at` would satisfy DS12 while violating DS13, EV8 and SV9.** | **The milestone is deferred rather than downgraded, because the alternative was to ship a retention feature that could not retain.** Two rejected dispositions: an *inert subset* (configuration plus the closed-window computation, with enforcement that deletes nothing because it cannot prove the guards) would have produced a "Retention" milestone that cannot retain, with EV8/SV9 still untestable until M10.6/M10.10 — a green milestone standing in for a capability that does not exist; and *adding guard columns now* would pull M10.6/M10.10 schema backward and decide the outbox's state model before the milestone that owns it. Deferring costs nothing that the other options preserve: no approved semantic is altered, no guarantee is weakened, and the work is not lost. **The dependency is recorded rather than the original order being treated as executable** — the plan documented M10.5 before M10.6, and that ordering is a plan defect, not a design defect. **ED9's own words are the standard being upheld:** *"A retention policy that can delete a fact before it has been consumed and metered is a revenue defect wearing an operations costume."* | [epic10-implementation-plan §M10.5 / §2](../docs/roadmap/epic10-implementation-plan.md), [ADR-0032 ED9/EV4/EV8](../docs/adr/ADR-0032-event-durability-and-delivery-semantics.md), [ADR-0034 DS12/DS13/SV9](../docs/adr/ADR-0034-durable-storage-seam-core-only-packages.md), [M10.0a OD-5](../docs/architecture/epic10/m10.0a-decision-gate.md), DL-0140 | **Governance re-sequencing only — no code, schema, migration, guard column, test or dependency was created or changed, and no M10.6/M10.10 work was pulled backward.** M10.5's approved retention semantics remain **unchanged and unweakened**; ADR-0032 and ADR-0034 remain **immutable and unaltered**; **no milestone is renumbered** — M10.5 keeps its number and its place in the plan, and only its execution position moves. Derived ordering: M10.5's **earliest executable position is after M10.10** (its last prerequisite), completing **before M10.12/M10.13**, since hardening and certification must cover it. **Resumption condition:** M10.5 resumes only when its prerequisites exist and EV8/SV9 can be written as real assertions rather than aspirations. **RESOLVED at M10.5 (2026-08-21), under explicit Owner authorization.** The paragraphs above are preserved as the historical record of why the deferral was taken, and the reasoning in them was correct *of the repository as it then stood*. What changed is the repository, not the reasoning. (1) **Both prerequisites now exist.** **M10.6** supplied the durable delivery state (`evt_outbox`, `SqlDurableEventSpine`, commit `dd531c5`) and **M10.10** supplied the metering seam (`platform/api/metering/`, commit `3ca738e5`) — the two milestones this entry named as blocking, both completed and committed. (2) **M10.5 was subsequently implemented, validated and committed** as `911eb6dad36a9552e5041e6676e6667c25019d08` (*feat(events): implement M10.5 retention guards*), ratified in advance by [ADR-0041](../docs/adr/ADR-0041-retention-guards-drained-by-outbox-and-the-metered-watermark.md) per DOCUMENTATION_RULES §3.7. (3) **EV8 and SV9 are now real, passing assertions rather than aspirations** — the precise condition this entry set. `tests/test_events_retention.py` proves each clause against the production implementation: *deletes nothing when unconfigured* (SV9), *deletes no un-drained event* — every non-`DELIVERED` outbox state, plus a missing row and an unrecognized state — and *deletes no un-metered event* — absent, negative and unavailable attestations (EV8). Full suite at the resolving commit: **5211 passed**, with the sole pre-commit failure being the expected clean-tree assertion, which passed once the change set was committed. Recorded honestly: those tests cite ADR-0041's **RT7/RT8** labels rather than the **EV8**/**SV9** labels, so the mapping above is by substance and not yet by citation — a traceability note for M10.13, not a gap in coverage. (4) **The deferral is therefore discharged**, exactly as **ADR-0041 RT13** anticipated: *"DL-0141 remains `Open` until M10.5 implementation is complete, at which point its disposition is a separate governance decision."* This is that decision. (5) **No milestone was renumbered and no approved retention semantic was weakened to reach this state** — M10.5 kept its number and its place, only its execution position moved, and ADR-0032/ADR-0034 remain immutable and unaltered. (6) **DL-0139 is not reopened** — it remains `Resolved`, discharged at M10.10, and nothing here disturbs it. (7) **This closure authorizes no M10.12 work.** Hardening, certification, freeze and release remain unstarted and separately authorized; the ordering constraint *"before M10.12/M10.13"* was satisfied, not waived. | Resolved — discharged at **M10.5** (2026-08-21) by Owner-authorized implementation commit `911eb6da`, after prerequisites **M10.6** (`dd531c5`) and **M10.10** (`3ca738e5`); **EV8/SV9 now real passing assertions**; **DL-0139 not reopened**; **no M10.12 work authorized**; owner: Technical Owner |
