Verdict **FREEZE_WITH_ADVISORIES**. | **The freeze principle is "freeze the certified state; do not improve the architecture during the freeze,"** and holding to it was the milestone's main discipline: M9.25 introduces no financial fact, no table, no migration, no route, no Studio page and no production module, and it closed **none** of the nine declared limitations — closing another milestone's limitation under cover of governance work is exactly the ownership theft a freeze must not perform, and a test asserts no limitation is owned by M9.25. Four decisions carried it. First, the manifest is **split into derived and declared halves**: module, schema, route, event, lifecycle and namespace counts are recomputed from the tree on every run, while the repository-wide regression result and the ruff result are recorded as **declared release inputs**, because they are facts about *running* the suite rather than reading it — a manifest that claimed to derive them would overstate what it can prove, and FZ-12's "reconstructable from the manifest and its declared release inputs" means exactly that pair. Second, **the digest reuses M9.21's canonicalizer**; the manifest imports no `hashlib` and declares no second canonicalizer, which certification control `DT-3` independently asserts. Third, **the freeze fails closed**: every one of the twelve invariants is an assertion rather than a report, and the suite pins the literal certification digest so a drifting tree fails the freeze instead of quietly re-certifying to a new value. Fourth, and most consequentially, **FZ-12 is reported as not satisfied.** Epic 9 is entirely uncommitted — `HEAD` is `a376e27`, Epic 8's release commit, and all of `platform/billing/`, its suites, ADR-0023, ADR-0031 and `docs/architecture/epic9/` are untracked — so there is no commit from which the tree can be reconstructed. This is roadmap risk **D-11**, anticipated when freeze (M9.25) and repository release (M9.26) were split with the mitigation *"commit state is a stated DoD line, not a footnote"*, and it is the same condition Epic 8 recorded at M8.20 §22. M9.25 therefore records `commit_state: uncommitted` and performs no commit and no tag; discharging it is M9.26's work. **Two defects were found by the freeze and fixed at the owning layer**, both about determinism of evidence and both owned by M9.25 because deterministic freeze evidence is its mandate. D-1: the certification digest was **context-dependent** — `NS-2` counted every index in the process, so `examined` was 1193 in isolation and 1199 once `api.models` registered its five tables, and `examined` enters the digest; M9.24 never caught it because no M9.24 test pinned a literal digest across execution contexts. The fix counts Billing's indexes while still comparing their names against every table in the process, so the control's strength is unchanged and only its reported coverage became deterministic — and a **first attempt at that fix silently weakened the control**, using `setdefault` so a Billing table registered as its own index's owner and cross-plane detection stopped entirely while still looking green, caught only by running the collision cases explicitly rather than trusting the fix. D-2: the freeze manifest repeated the same defect class by reading the process-wide `SQLModel.metadata`, fixed by reusing the certification runner's Billing-table resolution rather than writing a third implementation. Neither defect changed the catalogue, a severity or a clause, and the corrected certification digest is **identical** to the value M9.24 recorded, so no historical report is invalidated. | [freeze.md](../docs/architecture/epic9/freeze.md), [epic9-billing-platform-v1.0](../docs/releases/epic9-billing-platform-v1.0.md), [epic9-implementation-plan §10](../docs/roadmap/epic9-implementation-plan.md), [ADR-0023](../docs/adr/ADR-0023-billing-sole-owner-of-revenue.md), [ADR-0031](../docs/adr/ADR-0031-billing-plane-repository-placement.md), M8.20 Epic 8 freeze precedent | Epic 9 architecture frozen; extension policy stated (additive-only, new migrations after 0033, limitations closed by their owning milestones). **M9.26 Repository Release** owns commit, tag and baseline v4.0.0, and inherits the uncommitted-state condition together with nine owned limitations and two advisories. | Accepted |
| DL-0135 | 2026-08-17 | Technical Owner | **M9.24 — Billing Certification.** The final Epic 9 domain milestone, taken from the roadmap rather than from the milestone number: `epic9-implementation-plan.md` §10 P6 specifies *"independent certification against ADR-0023, the Blueprint and the Provider Strategy"*, output *"a certification report with an honest verdict"*, DoD **"certification is reproducible as tests, not a one-off document."** Delivered as a control catalogue + a runner + a 71-test suite, all under `tests/certification/` — **M9.24 adds zero production code**. 27 controls, verdict **CERTIFIED_WITH_ADVISORIES**, 0 blocking violations, 0 inconclusive, ruff clean. | **M9.24 owns no financial fact, and enforcing that structurally was the first decision.** It ships no table, no migration, no repository, no command bus, no customer API and no Studio page — and, after a defect of my own, no production module either: the runner was first placed in `billing/application/`, where it failed **nine of the repository's own architecture tests** at once because a certification runner must import `sqlmodel`, `billing.persistence` and `billing.customer` in order to inspect them. The layer rules were right and the placement was wrong; a cross-layer exemption for a new `billing/certification/` package was considered on the DL-0010 composition-root precedent and rejected, because inventing an architectural exemption at the last milestone before a freeze — in the milestone whose job is catching exactly that — is the wrong trade. The relocation also made a name-based scanner exclusion dead code: the scanner is now structurally outside the corpus it inspects. Each absence is argued rather than omitted: a *stored* verdict is a claim about a tree that has already moved on, so the authority is the suite re-run and the report is content-addressed on the evidence it examined; and an org-scoped endpoint serving a repository-wide engineering fact would be a lie about ownership in the one milestone whose job is catching those. The migration chain is deliberately unchanged at 0033, asserted by test. Four further decisions carried it. First, **controls are data, not test functions**: declared in one catalogue so *"what is certified?"* is a table somebody can read, with two structural guards — every declared control must produce a result (deleting one raises rather than shrinking the suite), and every control must cite a ratified clause, so a control cannot be somebody's opinion rendered as a test. That rule bit immediately: 39 module-local view mappers share generic names across engines, and because no ratified clause covers naming taste it is recorded as prose in the certification document rather than smuggled in as a control. Second, **`INCONCLUSIVE` is a first-class outcome and never a pass**, and a blocking control that is inconclusive fails certification — a guarantee nobody could verify is not a guarantee. An evaluator that raises is converted to `INCONCLUSIVE` with the exception recorded, proven by monkeypatching one to raise and observing the verdict flip to `NOT_CERTIFIED`. Third, **the namespace defect class was closed structurally**, as Phase 15 required: the audit found **10 colliding names across 21 definitions** spanning M9.12–M9.22, all latent (every internal call site imports by module path, so nothing computed a wrong value) and all fixed by renaming the losing side per the M9.22/M9.23 precedent. Two were identity functions — `derive_evidence_digest` defined by both M9.19 and M9.20, `derive_policy_version_id` by both M9.17 and M9.18 — which is the dangerous kind. **The detector itself had to be fixed twice, and the second fix matters most**: the first draft scanned `__all__` only and reported a reassuring 1,405 symbols while being blind to 110 of 183 Billing modules including `commercial_reporting`; the second fired 39 times on module-local helpers that never share a namespace. The shipped control checks the two routes by which a name actually reaches a package namespace — what `__init__.py` imports and what a submodule declares in `__all__` — inspects 2,669 names, and found the final two shadowings (`entry_view` Ledger-vs-Revenue, `run_view` Rating-vs-Reconciliation) that were ambiguous *by import style* and that the first draft missed entirely. Fourth, **two findings were classified advisory on mechanism, not on convenience**, and both are recorded as owned limitations rather than relabelled away. `NS-5`: 29 Pydantic model names collide across customer schema modules, so FastAPI emits module-qualified OpenAPI names — advisory because FastAPI *detects* the collision and disambiguates, the opposite of the silent shadowing `NS-1` blocks on, so no client is ever handed an ambiguous contract; the classification test applied was *"would the same judgment hold at a count of 2?"* and it does. `ST-3`: two Studio pages rescale integer minor units by a hardcoded 100, correct for all eight supported currencies (every one has exponent 2) and wrong the day a 0- or 3-exponent currency is added — so `ST-1` was narrowed to the act of *creating* a financial value client-side (nothing does; no page contains a `.reduce(`) and `ST-3` covers *rendering* one, a real semantic split rather than a softened control. Nine limitations are declared at freeze, each with an owning milestone, and **none was closed by stealing ownership**: M9.23's inherited gaps stay with M9.15, M9.16, M9.18 and M9.8, which own the missing reads. Three honest limitations of the certification itself are recorded: it is **self-administered** (the roadmap asked for independent certification and this is not that), it certifies **structure rather than behaviour**, and one of its own controls was wrong in the direction of false confidence before it was right. No ADR is amended; ADR-0023 PY5 now holds at a sixth altitude, asserted across all 29 declared lifecycles at once. |
| DL-0134 | 2026-08-17 | Technical Owner | **M9.23 — Billing Period Scope & Consolidation Engine.** Which accounts belong to a reporting scope, for which period, at which source revisions, across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI. 11 commands, 14 queries, 10 tables via migration 0033, 22 paths under `/consolidations`, 13 Studio screens, 121 tests, ruff clean. | **The organizing constraint is that M9.23 owns scope and owns no financial fact.** M9.22 shipped naming a gap rather than hiding it: every period-scoped read Billing publishes is keyed by `(org, account, period)`, because an accounting period is a property of an account — so a filing covering a group of accounts had no authoritative period read to consume. This milestone owns that gap, and the largest risk in building it is a consolidated figure that looks authoritative while quietly containing an account somebody guessed at. Five decisions carried it. First, **the hierarchy is consumed, never redefined**: M9.8 already owns `parent_account_id`, `payer_account_id`, `AccountHierarchyResolver` and `PayerResolver`, so `ScopeResolutionService` takes both as constructor arguments and calls them — a test asserts the class defines none of `ancestors`, `descendants`, `find_cycle`, `chain`, `root`, `depth`, `tree` or `expand`, and `ScopeAccountReader` exposes exactly `get`, `children_of` and `all`, so this milestone is structurally unable to change a hierarchy it reads. Second, **scope is a snapshot**: the snapshot id derives from a digest over its own membership, so resolving an unchanged hierarchy twice writes one row and an account that moved produces a different snapshot beside the old one rather than editing it; each member records the account's parent and payer edges as they stood, and there is no update path anywhere in persistence. Third, **attribution is never guessed** — a fact that cannot be attributed to exactly one member is `UNATTRIBUTABLE_SOURCE` (M9.8's ceiling) or `ACCOUNT_NOT_IN_SCOPE` (a scope question), never assigned to the first active account, the payer, the parent, the newest or the largest; a scan asserts no tie-break heuristic exists in any file. Fourth, **a gap never becomes a zero**, enforced independently four times: the factory *discards* an amount offered alongside a non-resolved status so no code path can produce a gap carrying a number, the aggregate skips null amounts, a **named** exception is raised (no kind is called `FAILED`, `ERROR`, `UNKNOWN` or `OTHER`, and one raised with an empty diagnostic is refused), and the wire carries `null`. `EMPTY` — the domain answered and had nothing — stays distinct from `UNAVAILABLE`. Fifth, **finalization cannot skip its checks**: `FINALIZED` is reachable only from `READY`, `READY` only from a validation that passed, `BLOCKED`'s only outgoing edge returns to collection, acknowledging a blocking exception does **not** unblock (`blocking_count` counts acknowledged ones deliberately), and finalization is refused outright if the source digest moved since validation. Two identities exist and the reason is stated plainly rather than papered over: the **run** id derives from `(org, snapshot, period, corrects)` — the immutable request, since source revisions are not knowable until the run has read them — and the **result** id derives from the run plus a digest over every pinned revision, so the finalized artifact is content-addressed on exactly what it consumed. Currency has no conversion member and no FX rate at any altitude; `_sum_minor` is the only function that adds money and refuses a mixed input. Scale is answered by two split tables and a **keyset** cursor rather than a bigger limit. Three limitations are recorded rather than smoothed: **three of seven source domains (M9.15, M9.16, M9.18) publish no period-scoped read at all**, so a scope requiring any of them comes back `BLOCKED` with named gaps; `MAX_SCOPE_MEMBERS` is a ceiling standing in for a paginated account read that belongs to **M9.8**; and `PAYER_GROUP` resolution reads the organization because M9.8 publishes no reverse payer index. ADR-0023 is **not amended**: PY5 is unchanged and now holds at a fifth altitude. One defect was fixed, and it is the same class for the third milestone running: an **index-name collision** — `cp_billing_consolidation_period.basis` and `cp_billing_consolidation.period_basis` auto-generate the identical SQLModel index name across two tables — caught only when `create_all` raised at wiring time. A repository-wide uniqueness test now covers all 1,193 indexes. Four symbol collisions were caught *before* landing by a pre-wiring namespace check run deliberately because M9.22 shipped seven. A second, more interesting defect was found by reading the capability table rather than by a test: **a scope consuming the integrity record could never finalize**, because an operational queue read has no period concept and that absence was being raised as a blocking `PERIOD_NOT_SUPPORTED` — making §16's integrity boundary structurally unusable. `SourceCapability.period_expected` now separates a gap (a domain that describes things happening *in* a period and cannot answer for one) from a category (a queue read that never had one), and two regression tests hold both directions so the fix cannot have turned a real gap into a shrug. |
| DL-0133 | 2026-08-17 | Technical Owner | **M9.22 — Billing Compliance & Regulatory Reporting Engine.** What must be reported, for which period, under which versioned definition, from which authoritative sources, across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI. 11 commands, 14 queries, 10 tables via migration 0032, 21 paths under `/reports`, 12 Studio screens, 116 tests, 4564/4564 repository-wide, ruff clean. | **The organizing constraint is that a report is derived evidence and never financial truth.** Nine milestones own financial facts and none can answer a regulator, because a filing spans all of them under a rule that changes on its own schedule — and the largest risk in building one is a filing that looks authoritative while quietly containing a figure nobody owns. Five decisions carried the milestone. First, **every field names its source**: `ReportFieldSpec` binds a field to a domain, an artifact type and a transformation drawn from a closed set of seven, so there is no `source = "computed"` escape and a regulatory formula cannot be written inline in a service. Exactly one member touches money — `SUM_SAME_CURRENCY`, which adds integer minor units of facts that already share a currency and **refuses a mixed-currency input rather than converting it**; an AST scan asserts one `sum(` in the whole Foundation and no `Money.times`, `allocate`, `percent` or `apply_rate`, and there is **no FX rate anywhere** because conversion is a rule nobody here has approved. Second, **definitions are versioned and immutable**: re-publishing a version with different content is refused, since every filing already bound to it would silently change meaning, and a report stores the version that produced it so `latest()` never reinterprets one. Third, **inputs are pinned and identity follows them**: the manifest records each source at the revision it was read, the report id derives from its digest, and a moved source therefore produces a new filing *beside* the old rather than rewriting it — demonstrated by a snapshot that stays byte-identical after the sources move. Fourth, **missing evidence never becomes zero**, enforced independently in four places: the transformation returns no value at all, completeness returns `BLOCKED`, a **named** exception is raised (there is no generic "report failed" — no `ReportExceptionKind` member is called `FAILED`, `ERROR` or `OTHER`), and the wire carries `null`. `SourceStatus` keeps `EMPTY` — we looked and there was nothing, which is a filing — apart from `UNAVAILABLE`, which is a gap. Fifth, **finalization cannot skip its checks**: `FINALIZED` is reachable only from `READY` and `READY` only from a validation that passed, acknowledging an exception records that somebody saw it but does **not** unblock the filing, a finalized report is never edited, and a correction opens a new report while the original keeps every figure and gains only a back-reference. The headline architectural finding is that **every period-scoped read Billing publishes is keyed by account**, because an accounting period is a property of an account — so an organization-wide filing has no authoritative period read to consume, and M9.22 reports that gap rather than aggregating across accounts on a regulator's behalf or scanning the org in Python. M9.18 publishes no period read at all, recorded the same way. ADR-0023 is **not amended**: PY5 is unchanged and now holds at a fourth altitude. Three defects were fixed, and the first is a pattern rather than an incident: **seven symbol collisions** with existing names (`derive_snapshot_id`, `PeriodState`, `IntegrityPosture`, `CompletenessService`, and three Application view mappers), caught by ruff's `F811` at import time; **two index-name collisions** in the schema, one with SQLModel's own auto-generated name and one across two tables, caught only when `create_all` failed; and an `_emit(subject=...)` keyword shadowing a positional parameter. That is the third consecutive milestone in which duplicate naming was the defect class, and the second in which a linter rather than review caught it. |
| DL-0132 | 2026-08-17 | Technical Owner | **M9.21 — Financial Investigation & Audit Evidence Engine.** What a human found out about an integrity incident, on what evidence, and whether an auditor can check it without application logs, across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI. 16 commands, 14 queries, 7 tables via migration 0031, 29 paths under `/investigations`, 12 Studio screens, 149 tests, 4478/4478 repository-wide, ruff clean. | **The organizing constraint is that an investigation records what was observed and concluded and never becomes the source of financial truth.** M9.19 and M9.20 both stop short of the question an examiner actually asks, and the largest risk in answering it is a case file that reads as authoritative while quietly containing a guess, a pointer presented as history, or a gap nobody mentioned. Five decisions carried the milestone. First, **a reference is not a snapshot**: a `LIVE_REFERENCE` structurally carries no body and no digest and can never back a confirmed conclusion, a snapshot structurally must carry both, the digest is recomputed by the factory rather than accepted from the caller, and — the part that makes it hold — the evidence store declares **no update path at all**, so a capture's mode is fixed for the life of the row rather than until somebody calls a setter. Second, **evidence is content-addressed and append-only**: the same immutable source captured twice converges on one row, a changed representation computes a different id and becomes a new link in a chain of custody, and a source becoming unavailable, redacted or superseded appends a link naming the one it follows — nothing is ever replaced, so *what did we hold first and what changed* survives without a backup. Chains are keyed without the revision precisely so a document moving from revision 1 to 2 shows the change inside one chain rather than hiding it in two. Third, **"could not retrieve" never becomes "verified"**: `UNAVAILABLE` is a first-class capture carrying when the source was last held, it blocks closure, it makes an audit package `INCOMPLETE`, and every gap is named — a package that silently dropped what it could not fetch would present as thorough exactly when it is least trustworthy. Fourth, **a guess cannot become a finding**: the observation identity includes the kind, so re-submitting a hypothesis as a fact appends a second record and the trail shows both; an `OBSERVED_FACT` must cite a capture that actually supports it, which excludes live references and unavailable ones; a `CONFIRMED` conclusion requires an available snapshot and `INCONCLUSIVE` requires none, so nobody is ever pushed into naming a root cause to shut a case. Fifth, **closure is earned**: the FSM has no edge from any working state to `CLOSED`, so the only route runs through a recorded conclusion, and `CLOSED` has no outgoing edge at all. Exactly one canonicalization algorithm exists, and it **refuses floats** rather than coercing them, because a digest that depends on `repr()` is stable on the machine that wrote it and different on the machine that audits it. **No balance, total, amount or currency exists anywhere** — not a column, not a wire field, not on the Studio screen; the single figure in the milestone sits inside a captured body as verbatim evidence of what an owning domain said. M9.19 keeps reconciliation and M9.20 keeps correlation, resolution and root-cause classification: a conclusion that disagrees records a **discrepancy** for the owning workflow and changes nothing, and M9.21 has **no outward command at all** — not even M9.19's repair request, which it references rather than issues. ADR-0023 is **not amended**: PY5 is unchanged and now holds at a third altitude. Two defects were fixed: an audit package that contained the record of its own creation, so exporting unchanged state twice minted two packages and the export-determinism guarantee was false; and a `ReasonBody`/`NoteBody` pair that shadowed the Ledger's and made FastAPI qualify *its* schema name in the public OpenAPI document — the same duplicate-name failure M9.20's D-2 recorded one milestone earlier, caught again by a test rather than by review. |
| DL-0131 | 2026-08-12 | Technical Owner | **M9.20 — Financial Integrity Correlation & Audit Engine.** Which reconciliation findings belong to the same incident, and whether an auditor can reconstruct the chain, across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI. 11 commands, 14 queries, 6 tables via migration 0030, 25 paths under `/integrity/incidents`, 10 Studio screens, 108 tests, 4186/4186 repository-wide, ruff clean. | **The organizing constraint is that correlation is an *evidence relationship* and never manufactures a root cause — enforced structurally rather than by discipline.** This closes the limitation M9.19 shipped with, and the single largest risk in doing so was a plausible-sounding cause nobody can prove. Four decisions carried the milestone. First, **anchors are extracted, never inferred**: a declared precedence over identifiers the *owning* domains already made immutable — the cause the Ledger itself records, then the document revision traced through M9.14/M9.16/M9.18's own facts, then the source fact — and amount, timestamp, account, invoice number, description, customer and payment method correlate nothing, alone or together, asserted by a test that shares all six between two findings. Precedence is deliberately **conservative**: findings resolving at different levels do not correlate, because an incident that should have been one and is two is a reporting inconvenience, while one that should have been two and is one has merged two unrelated financial problems and will close both when somebody fixes one. Second, **no invented causes**: `RootCause` declares no `DATABASE_FAILURE`, `NETWORK_FAILURE` or `PROVIDER_FAILURE`, because a missing ledger entry looks identical whether the database rejected it, the process died, or nobody ever tried; conflicting divergences classify as `UNKNOWN` rather than as the first, the commonest or the worst; and impact is *classified* by table, with an AST scan asserting the classifier contains no arithmetic at all. Third, **repair is never resolution**: the FSM has no `REPAIR_IN_PROGRESS → RESOLVED` edge, so the only path runs through verification, which re-reads every attached finding and refuses when anything is still divergent *or unreadable* — treating "I could not check" as "it is fine" is how an unrepaired incident gets closed. Severity is **monotonic** for the same reason: an incident must not slip off a dashboard because one of three findings was repaired, and no lifecycle transition touches the field. Fourth, **history is never rewritten**: merges and correlation corrections are new facts naming both the previous and the corrected belief, links are detached rather than deleted, the trail is append-only with a stored sequence, and status is a projection over it. **No balance, total, amount or currency exists anywhere** — not a column, not a wire field, not on the Studio screen; every figure stays on M9.19's finding where the domain that decided it put it. M9.19 keeps reconciliation ownership entirely: M9.20 reads its findings, writes to none of its tables, and its only outward command is M9.19's own `RequestFindingRepair`, which itself only asks an owning domain — two removes from any financial mutation. ADR-0023 is **not amended**: PY5 is unchanged and now holds at a second altitude. Two defects were fixed: a no-op correlation correction that detached a finding and silently orphaned it, and a derived-id helper whose name shadowed M9.11's usage-correction identity — caught by ruff, which is why the lint gate is part of the definition of done rather than a formatting preference. |
| DL-0130 | 2026-08-12 | Technical Owner | **M9.19 — Billing Reconciliation & Financial Integrity Engine.** Whether the six financial domains agree with one another, across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI: durable reconciliation runs, findings and evidence-backed resolutions over six declared relationships. 10 commands, 15 queries, 5 tables via migration 0029, 25 paths under `/integrity`, 10 Studio screens, 109 tests, 4078/4078 repository-wide, ruff clean. | **The organizing principle is that a reconciliation result is an *observation*, not permission to mutate another domain — and it is enforced structurally rather than by discipline.** This milestone imports no other domain's repository, dispatches no financial command of its own, and `PostJournalEntry` is provably unreachable from it (asserted by scan), because an engine that could post a journal entry would create the financial fact it claims to have observed and every `MISSING_COUNTERPART` finding would resolve itself into existence. The mandatory audit found six existing reconciliation artifacts and **changed none of them**: M9.2's `ReconciliationVerdict` is reused and every divergence projects onto it through a table; M9.2's `ReconciliationFinding` and M9.16's `PaymentReconciliation` are untouched, which is why this domain is named for *financial integrity* and declares `IntegrityFinding` beside them — shadowing a taken name would give one word two meanings. Payment↔**provider** is deliberately absent from the matrix: M9.16 owns it, and two owners for one pair is how a divergence gets resolved twice and reported once. M9.14 and M9.15 already owned their comparisons, so M9.19 **delegates** to `InvoiceReconciliationService` and `TaxReconciliationService` and adds only the durability they lacked — the existing reads were ephemeral, so nobody could say the books disagreed in March, who was told, or whether it recurred. Three decisions carried the milestone. First, the **evidence digest**: a run's id is derived from what it looked at and nothing volatile, so two workers converge on one run, an unchanged re-run writes nothing, and a re-run *after a repair* has different evidence and is therefore genuinely a new observation — which is what makes "reconciliation observes the result later" real rather than a rewrite. Second, **PY5 as an absent edge**: the finding lifecycle has no transition out of `RESOLVED` or `CLOSED`, so a stale worker cannot reopen a settled divergence because the machine has nowhere to put it, and `ResolveFinding` re-reads both sides from the owning domains and refuses unless the new facts actually agree — without that guard the command is `auto_fix()` under another name. A recurrence creates a *new* finding in a *new* run rather than rewriting a closed row, so history stays auditable. Third, **the matrix as data**: six relationships in a table declaring source, counterpart, authoritative side, matching identity, compared fields, mode and correction owner, checkable by iteration — six hand-written comparisons would drift in six different ways. Severity is likewise a table: a fabricated financial fact, a duplicate posting and an amount mismatch are always CRITICAL and no divergence is ever informational, while `PERIOD_MISMATCH` is only a warning because M9.13's `LateEntryDisposition` makes it legitimate and a critical that fires on correct accounting is one operators learn to ignore. **No balance, total or monetary sum exists anywhere** — not a column, not a wire field, not on the Studio screen; a "total divergent value" would be a figure this engine computed, and somebody would report it beside figures the Ledger owns. ADR-0023 is **not amended**: PY5 is unchanged and RS2/RS6 are untouched. Three defects were found and fixed, the serious one being an evidence filter that dropped `UNPOSTED` recognitions as "not recognized yet" — the exact state meaning revenue earned with no ledger entry, and the headline case the relationship exists to catch. |
| DL-0129 | 2026-08-12 | Technical Owner | **M9.18 — Revenue Recognition & Deferred Revenue Engine.** When a billed amount becomes earned, across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI: immutable versioned recognition policies, schedules derived from issued invoice snapshots, dated period entries, corrections as new facts. 10 commands, 16 queries, 5 tables via migration 0028, 24 paths under `/revenue`, 10 Studio screens, 110 tests, 3969/3969 repository-wide, ruff clean. | **The accounting model was decided explicitly: scheduling *plus posting*, and deliberately not a deferred-revenue subledger.** ADR-0023 RS6 says recognition state is a projection over the ledger and not a second bookkeeping system, so there is no stored balance anywhere — deferred revenue is `basis amount − recognized − adjusted`, derived on every read, and the schedule row holds a *count* rather than money. M9.13 stays the sole owner of financial recording and is reached only through `PostJournalEntry`. Two decisions carried the milestone. First, the **basis digest**: a hash over document, revision, snapshot content, amount, currency, service window, method, policy version, remainder rule and granularity — and nothing volatile, because a timestamp inside it would make every re-derivation a new schedule and destroy the idempotency it exists to provide. The schedule id *is* that digest, so re-deriving unchanged inputs writes nothing, two workers collide on a primary key, and **changing any input yields a different schedule rather than a mutated one** — which is the whole of "a policy edit in April cannot restate what March recognized". `latest()` appears exactly once in the Application, on the path that binds a *new* basis; a historical schedule reads the version pinned in its own basis. Second, **conservation as a property of one function**: every division of money goes through M9.2's largest-remainder `Money.allocate`, there is no second allocator and no floating-point arithmetic on money, and the factory runs a conservation check at construction so a future allocator change fails there rather than in a quarterly report. | [revenue-recognition-engine.md](../docs/architecture/epic9/revenue-recognition-engine.md), ADR-0023 RS5/RS6, ADR-0021, ADR-0031, DL-0126, DL-0128 | **M9.2's `RecognitionState` is reused unchanged and M9.13's close semantics are consumed, not restated.** The audit found two different meanings of "deferred" already in the codebase — M9.13's `PostingOutcome.DEFERRED` is *period* deferral — and they are never conflated: recognition stores the *disposition* M9.13 reported. `PostingSource.REVENUE_RECOGNITION` and `PostJournalEntry.recognition` are additive extensions on the M9.16 precedent (DL-0126); reusing `MANUAL` would have made every recognized period read as operator-entered in the one report where that is the question. **ADR-0023 is not amended.** `paid is not earned` is enforced by importing no payment or collection module at all, proven by an import scan and tested in both directions. **Two defects were found and fixed.** D-1 was the serious one: reversing a recognized period reported deferred revenue *greater than the invoice*, because the correction was subtracted twice — the entry left the recognized set **and** the adjustment carried its own negative amount. `RECOGNIZED_ENTRY_STATES` is now gross of corrections and includes `REVERSED`; the revenue was recognized, a ledger credit exists for it, and the reversal is the separate fact. One architectural decision is worth recording: `period_boundaries` first used `datetime`, which two frozen assertions correctly rejected because the Foundation bans the module outright so it cannot read an ambient clock. **The assertions were not weakened.** The calendar is now pure integer arithmetic — Hinnant's `civil_from_days`, no days-in-month table and no leap-year branch — and it is proven against the standard library across 33,000 days in both directions, in the test layer where `datetime` is importable. A calendar that is checked beats a ban that was relaxed. | Accepted |
| DL-0126 | 2026-08-10 | Technical Owner | **M9.16 — Payment Engine.** Implemented external money movement across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI: operator-declared provider routing, collection objectives, append-only payment attempts, a durable provider-operation claim, verified provider callbacks, refunds as independent financial facts, reconciliation, diagnostics, search and an append-only audit trail — 14 commands + 17 queries, 8 tables via migration 0026, 30 API paths under `/collections`, and an 11-screen Studio. | Payments is the first Billing milestone whose effects happen **outside the database**, in a system Billing does not own and no transaction here can roll back. ADR-0023 PY1/PY4 is made structural: **the provider moves money, Billing owns the financial fact that it moved** — a callback is an observation recorded verbatim and never trusted as state. Four decisions carry the milestone. (1) **Intent is split from attempt.** M9.2's `Payment` carried a mutable `attempt` counter on one row, so a retry overwrote the previous attempt's state, failure code, provider reference and timestamps; "we tried three times and the third worked" and "it worked" are different histories and only one is true. `PaymentIntent` and append-only `PaymentAttempt` sit **beside** `Payment`, which is unreshaped — the call M9.13, M9.14 and M9.15 each made. (2) **The external transaction boundary is three phases**, with the provider call between two transactions and the `ProviderOperation` row committed *first*. Its identity is a **composite primary key on `(org_id, operation_key)`** — a database constraint, not an `if not exists`, because between the check and the insert there is a window and the window is the incident. The loser of the claim **does not send**; it retrieves. A crash anywhere in phase 2 or 3 leaves a `sending` row naming the exact key to ask about. (3) **Ambiguity is a state, not an error.** A timeout yields `AttemptState.AMBIGUOUS`, which **blocks every further attempt on its intent** and leaves only by `RESOLVE_SUCCESS`/`RESOLVE_FAILURE`, which only reconciliation calls — retrying past an unresolved outcome is the most expensive available bug and the only defence that works is refusing to. `ALREADY_PROCESSED` is deliberately not success: it is an instruction to retrieve. (4) **No provider is named anywhere.** Routing is operator-declared `PaymentRoutePolicy` rows with a deterministic total order and the *reason* recorded onto the attempt; migration 0026 seeds nothing; `NullPaymentProvider` returns `UNAVAILABLE` rather than `FAILED`, because "no provider configured" and "the issuer declined" are different facts. | [payment-engine.md](../docs/architecture/epic9/payment-engine.md), ADR-0019, ADR-0021, ADR-0023 PY1–PY9, ADR-0031, M9.0C | Additive: `BillingRepositories.payment_engine`/`BillingContext.payment_engine` and `BillingContainer.build(payment_providers=…)` default to `None`; an unwired registry collects nothing rather than half. M9.2's `payments.py` and `refunds.py` are unreshaped and `cp_billing_payment`, `cp_billing_payment_instrument`, `cp_billing_refund`, `cp_billing_dunning` and `cp_billing_reconciliation` are untouched; `PaymentState`, `PaymentTrigger`, `PaymentMethodKind`, `PaymentInstrument`, `OperationIdentity`, `ProviderReport`, `ReconciliationVerdict`, `ReconciliationFinding`, `RefundState`, `RefundReason`, `RefundDestination` and `Dispute` are **reused unchanged** (§A2.5); `ProviderOutcome.to_report()` and `PaymentReconciliation.to_finding()` are the **single** derivations of M9.2's thinner records. **Three amendments, recorded rather than silent**: `PostingSource` gains `PAYMENT` and `REFUND` (reusing `MANUAL` would make every captured payment read as operator-entered in the one report where that distinction is the question; letting Payments post under its own vocabulary would put a second poster beside M9.13); `PostJournalEntry` gains `source`/`source_id`/`payment_id`, all defaulted to the previous behaviour so every existing caller is unchanged; and `BillingContainer.build` gains `payment_providers`. 19 new `billing.payment.*`/`billing.reconciliation.*` events (211 total), 17 new diagnostic codes (211 total), foundation exports 788→873, application 552→671. Migration chain extended to 0026; no `ALTER`, no seed, no Epic 5–8 table touched, Billing still absent from `platform/bootstrap.py`. The **mandatory pre-implementation audit** of M9.2–M9.15 found one modelling problem (the collapsed intent/attempt, resolved by additive extension), no duplicate provider abstraction, no competing refund model, and no vendor SDK anywhere. The **M9.8 attribution ceiling was re-evaluated rather than repeated**: Payments consumes exactly one account through M9.8's existing contract and adds no attribution logic — it does not block this milestone and is not solved by it, and now blocks seven. **Three defects found and fixed**, one financially material: a ledger refusal (no open accounting period) unwound the transaction that was recording a capture which had *already happened*, so the money was gone and nothing in Billing said so — fixed by keeping the capture, raising an `UNPOSTED_CAPTURE` divergence, and repairing through the explicit, attributed, idempotent `PostPaymentToLedger`; a duplicate webhook reported itself as `applied` because the idempotent append returned the original row, making a redelivery indistinguishable from a first delivery at exactly the surface built to distinguish them; and `PaymentRoute.provider_code` had no default, so the unrouted case could not be constructed. **Four frozen assertions updated, three strictly strengthened and none weakened**: the persistence-unawareness scan flagged `PaymentRouter.select` for sharing a verb with SQL and now checks *imports* via AST (which also catches `import select as pick`); the declared package layout gained three modules; `ScriptedOutcome` was made frozen because the aggregate-immutability assertion was right; and the tax migration test asserted `0025` was *last* when it only ever meant it was *present*. A name collision between M9.3's `RefundView` and a new one was caught by the linter and resolved by naming (`CommercialRefundView`). No domain was changed to satisfy a test. Ten limitations recorded — chiefly that no vendor adapter ships, that webhook cryptography belongs to the adapter, that dunning and dispute *execution* remain M9.2 declarations, that reconciliation is on-demand rather than scheduled, that provider *failover* is deliberately absent, and that the AR-subledger decision is now depended on by a document, a determination, and a payment. 133 new tests including 25 named demonstrations of the dangerous cases; repository-wide 3734 passing, zero regressions. | Accepted |
| DL-0125 | 2026-08-09 | Technical Owner | **M9.15 — Tax Determination Engine.** Implemented tax treatment across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI: jurisdictions, classifications, supplier nexus and registrations, customer exemptions, effective-dated rate evidence, place-of-supply resolution, declared treatment precedence, integer-only calculation with recorded rounding, attributed overrides, immutable content-addressed determinations, self-explaining evidence, reconciliation, diagnostics, search and an append-only audit trail — 13 commands + 16 queries, 7 tables via migration 0025, 24 API paths under `/taxation`, and an 11-screen Studio. | Tax is the first Billing domain whose correctness is judged by someone **outside the company** — a tax authority, on a date years after the invoice was issued, against rules that have since changed. The question is never 'what is the tax?' but **'why was this amount applied, and on whose authority?'**, and the whole milestone is shaped to answer it. ADR-0023 TX1 is made structural rather than aspirational: **Billing declares no rate anywhere**. An AST scan forbids an integer literal reaching `rate_basis_points=` across all eight modules (excluding OpenAPI examples *by structure*, so a literal anywhere the code could reach still fails), the migration seeds nothing, and a fresh installation determines no tax at all until an operator declares one behind the `TaxRateProvider` adapter port. `NullTaxRateProvider` is the default and offers **nothing** rather than 0%, deliberately: a silent zero is indistinguishable from a correctly zero-rated export, and those two are the most expensive pair in the domain to confuse. **A determination self-explains**: `TaxExplainer` holds no provider and takes none, so 'why?' is answerable from stored evidence in five years when the provider's answer would be different and the invoice's would not — a structural guarantee, asserted by scanning the class for any mention of a provider. **An assessment is content-addressed by its basis** and the lifecycle has **no edge out of `final`**: a correction is a new assessment carrying `supersedes_assessment_id` and both stay readable, because an audit asks not what the tax is but what you charged and what you changed it to; an override is therefore not an edit either, travelling into a new determination while keeping the conclusion it replaced. **Rates are append-only effective-dated evidence**, so a retired rate stays readable and stops applying — an issued invoice must remain explainable against the rate in force when it was issued (TX2). Treatment precedence is **declared**, and cross-border sits deliberately *ahead* of the nexus test because an export is zero-rated by the supplier's own regime. | [tax-engine.md](../docs/architecture/epic9/tax-engine.md), ADR-0019, ADR-0021, ADR-0023, ADR-0031 | Additive: `BillingRepositories.tax_engine`/`BillingContext.tax_engine` default to `None`. M9.2's `tax.py` is unreshaped and `cp_billing_tax_profile`/`cp_billing_tax_determination` are untouched; `TaxRegime`, `TaxpayerKind`, `TaxTreatment`, `GstSplit`, `TaxRegistration`, `TaxProfile` and `TaxComponent` are **reused unchanged** (§A2.5). `TaxAssessment.to_determination()` is the **single** derivation of M9.2's thinner record, written only by `FinalizeTaxAssessment`, so the surface M9.5 and M9.14 already read is a projection of one truth rather than a second copy — and **M9.14 needed no change to start presenting tax**, resolving its limitation 3 without touching it. 14 new `billing.tax.*` events (192 total), 17 new diagnostic codes (194 total), foundation exports 714→788, application 552→620. Migration chain extended to 0025; no `ALTER`, no Epic 5–8 table touched, Billing still absent from `platform/bootstrap.py`. The **mandatory pre-implementation audit** of M9.2–M9.14 found one ownership overlap (M9.2's thin tax domain, resolved by additive extension), **no rate table anywhere in the repository**, and no duplicated jurisdiction, registration or calculation logic. **Five defects found and fixed, four by the end-to-end smoke and two financially material in opposite directions**: CGST and SGST collided on the derived rate id because it omitted the rate's *name*, so the second declaration was swallowed as a duplicate and every Indian intra-state invoice would have been taxed at 9% instead of 18%; inclusive pricing extracted per component rather than against the combined rate, over-collecting ₹165.12 where ₹152.54 was owed and breaking the identity that the parts sum back to the gross — fixed by extracting once and allocating by rate weight with `Money.allocate`; every export was reported `out_of_scope` because the nexus test ran before the cross-border test, losing the zero-rating from the evidence a return is filed from; `is_sourced` returned `True` when no rate was found at all, because `all()` over an empty tuple is `True`; and a factory `_emit` kwarg collided with its own positional `subject_id`. **Two frozen assertions sharpened, both strictly strengthened, neither weakened**: `test_controllers_never_catch_exceptions` scanned for the substring `try:`, which a parameter named `country` contains, and now walks the AST for `ast.Try`/`ast.ExceptHandler`; and M9.14's two invoice-surface scans matched `"/documents" in path`, which M9.15's tax *view* of a document at `/taxation/documents/…` satisfied, and are now scoped to the exact prefix they always meant. No domain was changed to satisfy a test. Ten limitations recorded — chiefly that there is no catalog→classification mapping (the classification is told, not looked up, because adding one to M9.6 would be the cross-domain coupling ADR-0023 forbids), that no external tax-engine adapter ships, that rate resolution does not compound across jurisdiction levels, and that the M9.8 attribution ceiling now blocks six milestones. 152 new tests including 19 named determination demonstrations; repository-wide 3601 passing, zero regressions. | Accepted |
| DL-0124 | 2026-08-08 | Technical Owner | **M9.14 — Invoice Engine.** Implemented customer documents across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI: invoices, credit notes and debit notes with gapless jurisdiction-aware numbering, immutable content-addressed snapshots, append-only revision history, corrections by credit and debit note, document relationships, deterministic HTML/PDF document-model rendering, delivery and attachment metadata, export, inspection, diagnostics, reconciliation against the ledger, and append-only history — 13 commands + 14 queries, 7 tables via migration 0024, 26 API paths under `/documents`, and an 11-screen Studio. | M9.13 was the first domain whose errors could not be corrected by re-running anything; M9.14 is the first whose output **leaves the building** — an issued invoice reaches a customer's AP system, an auditor's sample, and in several jurisdictions a statutory filing, and none of those parties can be asked to accept a corrected copy of a document they already hold. The milestone is organised around one sentence: **the Ledger owns financial truth, the Invoice owns presentation of it** — which is why a line carries a `journal_entry_id` rather than a recomputed amount, why nothing here folds a balance, and why rendering is downstream of a sealed snapshot. Four invariants are structural rather than procedural. **Content is addressed by digest**: the header carries no lines and no totals (asserted by enumerating its fields, as M9.13 asserts no aggregate carries a balance), content lives in an append-only snapshot store, issuance pins one revision's digest, and every read resolves *that digest* — so a later revision could not become the served document by being newer, because the lookup is by content address rather than by recency; selecting by 'latest' would make immutability depend on nobody appending. **Corrections are new documents**: no edge back to `draft`, no `VOID` out of any issued state (a number has been allocated and a gapless series has no hole to spare), no deletion state anywhere, and post-issue cancellation is a full credit note carrying `CANCELS` — which is what jurisdictions that forbid deleting an issued invoice actually require. Correction amounts are **mirrored, never recomputed**: asking pricing what the invoice should have said is the reach-back this milestone forbids, and would produce a credit note that disagrees with the invoice it credits the moment a price changed. **Numbering is gapless because allocation is transactional**: `(org, series, sequence)` is the primary key of an allocation ledger, so a duplicate number is an impossible row rather than a finding, and the sequence is drawn inside the issuing transaction so a worker that dies between allocating and committing rolls both back — a pre-allocated pool cannot offer this, and where a jurisdiction requires gapless numbering there is no legal way to fill a hole afterwards. **Rendering computes nothing**: templates receive a model whose every leaf is a string and never see a `Money`, because handing a renderer no numbers is cheaper than trusting it not to add them up. | [invoice-engine.md](../docs/architecture/epic9/invoice-engine.md), ADR-0019, ADR-0021, ADR-0023, ADR-0031 | Additive: `BillingRepositories.invoice_engine`/`BillingContext.invoice_engine` default to `None`. M9.2's `invoicing.py` is unreshaped and `cp_billing_invoice`, `cp_billing_credit_note` and `cp_billing_run` are untouched; `InvoiceState`, `InvoiceTrigger`, `IMMUTABLE_INVOICE_STATES`, `ChargeLineKind`, `InvoiceNumber` and `InvoiceTotals` are **reused unchanged** (§A2.5), as are `TaxComponent`/`TaxTreatment` and `BillingAddress`; tests assert `services.py` remains the **only** declaration of `InvoiceTotalsService` and `InvoiceNumberFormatter`. 10 new `billing.*` events (178 total; 6 M9.2 invoice facts reused), 15 new diagnostic codes (177 total), foundation exports 642→714, application 492→552. Migration chain extended to 0024; no `ALTER`, no Epic 5–8 table touched, Billing still absent from `platform/bootstrap.py`. The **mandatory pre-implementation audit** of M9.2–M9.13 found two genuine overlaps (M9.2's invoicing domain and its totalling/formatting services), both resolved by additive extension and delegation rather than reimplementation, and no previous limitation blocking Invoice. **Five defects found and fixed, two by the end-to-end smoke**: all three document kinds printed `VOX-KA-000001` because each counts in its own gapless series and the formatted number carried no kind token, so two documents stating opposite things would have shared one printed identifier; a revision silently discarded the supplier, template and payment instructions the draft was prepared with and would then have been *issued* that way; `EMPTY_INVOICE` was already declared at M9.2, which in a `StrEnum` is a `TypeError` at import rather than an alias; row identity was being derived in the persistence layer (caught by the frozen `hashlib` import ban — an identity is a domain fact, and the derivation moved to `commercial_invoicing.py`); and the number-series audit inferred sequences from the numbers printed on documents, which would have hidden precisely the case it exists to find — a sequence allocated to an issue that then failed — so it now reads the allocation ledger. **One frozen assertion sharpened, not weakened**: M9.3's `test_the_application_duplicates_no_foundation_rule` banned `basis_points` and `.allocate(` as substrings, and neither could distinguish *carrying* a Foundation value from *duplicating* a Foundation rule once a document had to transport a determined tax rate and draw an invoice number; following the M9.7 precedent in the adjacent test, `basis_points` is now banned inside an arithmetic expression and `.allocate(` by receiver, so `Money.allocate` remains forbidden by name *and* by structure. No domain was changed to satisfy a test. Ten limitations recorded — chiefly that the M9.8 attribution ceiling now blocks five milestones, that there is no supplier registry because organization legal identity is not Billing's to own, that PDF *bytes* need an adapter at the edge, and that a jurisdiction's numbering policy is passed per issue because there is nowhere to declare it once. 156 new tests including 19 named document demonstrations; repository-wide 3449 passing, zero regressions. | Accepted |
| DL-0123 | 2026-08-07 | Technical Owner | **M9.13 — Credit & Revenue Ledger.** Implemented financial recording across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI: immutable append-only journal entries, derived balances, accounting periods with a one-way close, reversals and adjustments, credits with derived positions, balance checkpoints, replay, reconciliation, inspection, diagnostics, and append-only history — 12 commands + 14 queries, 7 tables via migration 0023, 24 API paths under `/accounting`, and an 11-screen Studio. | The Ledger is the first Billing domain whose errors cannot be corrected by re-running anything: a duplicated usage record is corrected and a duplicated rating run is superseded, but a duplicated **posting** has already changed a balance, may already have reached a statement, and is remedied only by a reversal, a credit note, and a conversation with a customer who has been charged twice. Every invariant is therefore made structural rather than procedural. **Balances are derived**: `AccountBalance.of()` is the only constructor, no stored aggregate in the domain carries a balance field (asserted by enumerating them), and `BalanceCheckpoint` is a *cache with a proof* whose `verify()` recomputes from the entries it summarises — the moment a checkpoint becomes authoritative the ledger stops being verifiable. **Posting is exactly-once by derived identity**: the id hashes the cause, so a retry collides and a correction derives a *different* id and is structurally incapable of overwriting what it corrects; the landing period and the amount are deliberately excluded from the derivation, because including the first would let a re-run after a close double-post and including the second would let a corrected figure post beside the original. **History is never mutated**: no update method exists to call, no delete state exists anywhere, and the period FSM has no edge out of `closed` — a filed number cannot silently change, and a late fact lands in the next open period recording that it did. **Entries are totally ordered** by a durable per-account counter rather than `max+1`, because a reused position makes 'what was the balance after entry 47?' a question with two answers, and gaps are surfaced rather than hidden since an unexplained gap is what an audit can ask about. Declared explicitly as an **accounts-receivable subledger** rather than a general ledger, with `TrialBalance` (debits and credits kept separate, never netted) as the reconciliation surface — a chart of accounts belongs to an accounting system VoxVoice does not own (ADR-0023 §5). | [ledger-engine.md](../docs/architecture/epic9/ledger-engine.md), ADR-0019, ADR-0021, ADR-0023, ADR-0031 | Additive: `BillingRepositories.ledger_engine`/`BillingContext.ledger_engine` default to `None`. M9.2's `ledger.py` is unreshaped and `cp_billing_ledger_entry`, `cp_billing_credit_grant`, `cp_billing_credit_movement` are untouched; `LedgerEntryType`, `LedgerDirection`, `RecognitionState` and `CreditKind` are **reused unchanged** (§A2.5). 12 new `billing.*` events (168 total), 16 new diagnostic codes (162 total), foundation exports 574→642, application 433→492. Migration chain extended to 0023; no `ALTER`, no Epic 5–8 table touched, Billing still absent from `platform/bootstrap.py`. **ADR-0023 RS2 (the ledger is reproducible) is now demonstrated rather than asserted** via the close-digest replay. Implements the guard M9.12's spec recorded as missing: a provisional rating run is refused. **Four defects found and fixed, two by the end-to-end smoke**: the deferral path was *unreachable* because a posting request never named the period it belonged to, so the Application always passed the open period as the target and a late correction would have posted silently with `disposition=IN_PERIOD`, losing exactly the audit signal the field exists to carry; corrections shadowed their originals during reconciliation because a reversal carries the original's `charge_id`, making every corrected charge read as an `AMOUNT_MISMATCH` on precisely the accounts most worth reconciling; `GrantCreditBody` collided with M9.5's, emitting two qualified schema names into the public OpenAPI document (the same defect class M9.12 hit with `MoneyResponse`, now guarded generally); and two event members reused values M9.2 had already declared, which in a `StrEnum` silently creates an alias rather than a member. Eight limitations recorded — chiefly that the M9.8 attribution ceiling now blocks four milestones and is not fixable inside this boundary, that the subledger-vs-GL decision should be ratified into ADR-0023 before an invoice depends on it, that a balance read still folds full history rather than rolling forward from a checkpoint, and that 29 pre-existing qualified OpenAPI schema names are inherited from M9.5/M9.7–M9.11. 135 new tests including 19 named accounting demonstrations; repository-wide 3293 passing, zero regressions. | Accepted |
| DL-0122 | 2026-08-07 | Technical Owner | **M9.12 — Rating Engine.** Implemented commercial charge calculation across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI: flat, seat, per-unit, allowance/overage, graduated, volume, package and hybrid charges with a breakdown that sums exactly to the charge and provenance naming every frozen artefact that produced it; entitlement-over-price allowance precedence; minimum commitments; deterministic rating runs; idempotent rerating; adjustment against finalized runs; comparison, explanation, inspection, snapshots, diagnostics, search, and append-only history — 9 commands + 12 queries, 5 tables via migration 0022, 19 API paths under `/charges`, and an 11-screen Studio. | Rating is the first Epic 9 milestone whose output a customer **pays**, so every determinism claim is made checkable rather than asserted. The run id is **derived from the basis digest** rather than minted from the `IdGenerator` seam: rerating unchanged inputs becomes a no-op at the database's unique constraint, replay is byte-comparable including charge ids, and — the half that matters — a changed input produces a *different* id, so there is no code path that can overwrite the figure a customer was billed, not because nobody wrote one but because the id of a revised run is never the id of the run it revises. Rating **consumes a `ResolvedPrice` and never resolves one**: that single field is the whole M9.7/M9.12 boundary, and a test forbids the Foundation from naming `PriceResolver`, `select_book`, `PriceResolutionRequest`, or `eligible(`. The lifecycle has **no edge out of FINALIZED**, which is what makes 'a billed figure can never be edited' structural rather than procedural — a provisional predecessor is *superseded*, a finalized one is *adjusted* and left exactly as it was billed. Included-quantity precedence is **declared, not inferred** (entitlement beats price, and an explicit allowance of 0 beats a price allowance of 100), because taking the larger lets a promotion permanently raise a plan and taking the smaller lets a stale snapshot bill for included units. Commitments are applied **after** every other charge, since a shortfall computed against a partial set would depend on calculation order. All arithmetic is integer: package blocks use `-(-a // b)` rather than `math.ceil` on a float quotient, tested at 10^18 + 1 where the float route silently rounds down. | [rating-engine.md](../docs/architecture/epic9/rating-engine.md), ADR-0021, ADR-0023, ADR-0031 | Additive: `BillingRepositories.rating_engine`/`BillingContext.rating_engine` default to `None`. M9.2's `pricing.py` and `invoicing.py` are unreshaped, `ChargeLine`/`ChargeLineKind` stay exactly as they are, and `cp_billing_charge_line` is untouched. 10 new `billing.rating.*` events (157 total), 16 new diagnostic codes (146 total), foundation exports 510→574, application 380→433. Migration chain extended to 0022; no `ALTER`, no Epic 5–8 table touched, Billing still absent from `platform/bootstrap.py`. **Four defects found and fixed, one of them a revenue leak in a frozen milestone**: M9.7 folds *every* discount into the unit rate, including fixed sums, so a one-time '₹500 off this line' became '₹500 off each unit' the moment rating multiplied by quantity — ₹1,000 billed where ₹5,500 was owed on a 10-minute example. Fixed additively inside rating's own arithmetic (`DiscountRebasingPolicy`): re-base the rate, subtract the fixed sum once at charge level, show the correction as a visible component, and warn via `FIXED_DISCOUNT_ON_METERED_RATE`. M9.7 is unchanged and the result is arithmetically identical for every shape rating does not multiply, so the correction is invisible exactly where M9.7 was already right. Also fixed: a duplicate `MoneyResponse` that FastAPI surfaced as two qualified schema names in the public OpenAPI document (now reuses M9.5's); a lifecycle service raising the wrong error type against a diagnostic code that does not exist; and a repository read off the context by attribute name rather than injection. Four assertions sharpened, none weakened — notably network scanning moved from a substring search to an import scan, because `requests` is also this domain's own `Sequence[ChargeRequest]` parameter. Seven limitations recorded, chiefly that the M9.11 attribution ceiling is **not resolvable within Rating's boundary** (M9.8 models no scope→account attachment, and adding one here would be the cross-domain coupling ADR-0023 forbids) and that nothing yet refuses to finalize a run over an unsealed usage period. 141 tests including 19 named correctness demonstrations; repository-wide 3158 passing, zero regressions. | Accepted |
| DL-0121 | 2026-08-06 | Technical Owner | **M9.11 — Metering Engine.** Implemented the commercial-usage domain across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI: versioned meters with one authoritative source, exact integer normalization, total attribution with an alerting quarantine, idempotent ingestion keyed by source-event identity, a declared late-arrival policy, windowed aggregation, one-way period sealing with a content digest, append-only corrections, deterministic replay, continuous reconciliation, provenance, diagnostics, and byte-identical exports — 17 commands + 12 queries, 7 tables via migration 0021, 26 API paths under `/usage`, and an 11-screen Studio. | Metering is the one Epic 9 domain whose errors are **silent**, so every guarantee is made *checkable* rather than asserted. A usage id is **derived** — hashed from `(org, event, meter)` — rather than minted from the `IdGenerator` seam every other aggregate uses, because replay must produce byte-identical usage including ids or 'reprocessing a period yields identical records' is a claim nobody can verify; deriving it also means two racing processes compute the same primary key, so the database arbitrates instead of two half-written rows coexisting. A sealed period carries a **seal digest** over its billable content — covering ids, quantities, periods and statuses, and deliberately excluding ingestion timestamps and run ids, which legitimately differ between an original ingestion and a replay of it — so a replay *proves* the commercial outcome is identical. Exactly-once is defended four deep (derived key, read-before-write, DB unique constraint, `IntegrityError` re-read inside a **`SAVEPOINT`**); the savepoint is the part usually missing, because without it a caught integrity error poisons the session and the surrounding transaction dies with the activity entry and staged event. `MetricStreamPort` is an ABC declared inside the plane and implemented outside it, with **no emit, publish, or write method** — Billing is structurally incapable of producing an operational metric, never polls Runtime, and an unwired port ingests nothing rather than inventing usage. Normalization is an exact integer ratio with declared rounding: no float touches a quantity, and `HALF_EVEN` exists because rounding halves consistently upward biases every meter in VoxVoice's favour. A duplicate is treated as a **success** and recorded as its own fact, because at-least-once delivery makes replays normal and the recorded fact is the evidence idempotency worked. | [metering-engine.md](../docs/architecture/epic9/metering-engine.md), ADR-0023, ADR-0031 | Additive: `BillingRepositories.metering_engine`/`BillingContext.metering_engine` default to `None`. M9.2's `metering.py`, `cp_billing_usage_record`, `cp_billing_usage_aggregate` and `cp_billing_usage_quarantine` are untouched, and M9.5's `/metering` routes still answer. 18 new `billing.*` events, 20 new diagnostic codes, foundation exports 429→510, application 320→380. Migration chain extended to 0021; no `ALTER`, no Epic 5–8 table touched, Billing still absent from `platform/bootstrap.py`. **Five defects found and fixed**, four in the correctness core: usage could land in a *sealed* period (the period was looked up by scope id rather than the resolved account, so `period_state` silently defaulted to OPEN); an adjustment landed back in the period it was adjusting, breaking that period's seal digest; two overlapping meters could both reach ACTIVE because the conflict check compared only ingestible meters and activation never re-checked; and an append-only correction was silently swallowed by the exactly-once constraint until `corrects_usage_id` became part of the key. Four assertions sharpened, none weakened, including controller transport-only detection now resolving one level of helper delegation. Six limitations recorded — chiefly that attribution needs exactly one active account per organization, because M9.8 models no scope→account attachment; multi-account organizations quarantine rather than being guessed at, which is correct under M6 but is a real ceiling on who can be billed. 184 new tests including 14 explicit correctness demonstrations; repository-wide 3011 passing, zero regressions. | Accepted |
| DL-0120 | 2026-08-06 | Technical Owner | **M9.10 — Entitlement Engine.** Implemented the entitlement-decision domain across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI: a gate registry carrying a fail-open/fail-closed/degrade classification per capability, nestable bundles with cycle detection, precedence-resolved grants on a draft→active→suspended→revoked/expired→archived FSM, half-open effective dating, scheduled intent, deterministic snapshot composition with immutable versioned publishing, staleness directives, inspection and diagnostics, 19 commands + 12 queries, 5 tables via migration 0020, 28 API paths under `/capabilities`, and an 11-screen Studio. | The load-bearing Epic 9 invariant is implemented as **code shape rather than prose**: no module in the domain contains `principal`, `Permission`, `scope`, `has_role`, `check_permission`, `is_authorized`, `RoleAssignment`, or `RBAC`, and a test greps for every one — Billing decides commercial capability and §A11.2 RBAC keeps authorization (ADR-0023 §11.3). M9.2's `GateClassification`, `GrantSource`, and `GRANT_PRECEDENCE` are **reused rather than re-declared**, because two answers to 'which source wins?' is exactly the drift §A2.5 prevents — the lesson M9.9 learned when it briefly duplicated `INTERVAL_SECONDS`; and `EntitlementSet.to_snapshot()` projects onto M9.2's frozen `EntitlementSnapshot`, so a plane written against the M9.5 enforcement contract consumes an M9.10 decision without changing a line. **Effective dating is a resolution-time property, never a state change** — a closed window simply stops being composed, which is what lets the domain contain no scheduler at all; the `EXPIRED` state records the administrative fact instead. Revocation closes the grant's **window as well as its state**, or a snapshot for a later instant would re-admit it through effective dating. There is **no deletion state at all** and no `revoked→active` edge: re-granting is a new grant, because collapsing the two makes 'was this ever revoked?' unanswerable — precisely the question a disputed entitlement turns on. Resolution is fully ordered (declared precedence → generosity → grant id) and never consults insertion order, asserted by rotating the grant list through every offset; every answer carries a `resolution_path` and inspection keeps the **losing** grants, because 'why does this account *not* have X?' is unanswerable from the winner alone. Snapshots are returned **as published, never recomputed** — a decision an enforcing plane acted on must read back exactly as it was — with a pure `preview` for 'what would be published'. Grants live inside the profile aggregate because precedence is only correct when it sees all of an account's grants at once; a row-per-grant table invites a partial read that resolves wrongly in silence. | [entitlement-engine.md](../docs/architecture/epic9/entitlement-engine.md), ADR-0019, ADR-0021, ADR-0023, ADR-0031 | Additive: `BillingRepositories.entitlements_engine`/`BillingContext.entitlements_engine` default to `None`, so without wiring the Application is exactly what M9.9 assembled. M9.2's `entitlement.py`, `cp_billing_entitlement_grant`, and `cp_billing_entitlement_snapshot` are untouched, and M9.5's `/entitlements` routes still answer; M9.10's commands are named distinctly (`GrantCommercialEntitlement`, `RevokeCommercialEntitlement`, `PublishCommercialEntitlementSnapshot`, `GetCommercialEntitlementSnapshot`). 18 new `billing.*` event types, 22 new diagnostic codes, foundation exports 371→429, application exports 261→320. Migration chain extended to 0020; no `ALTER`, no Epic 5–8 table touched, Billing still absent from `platform/bootstrap.py`. Three defects found and fixed, one of them pre-existing in a frozen milestone: `build_account_repositories` and `build_subscription_repositories` were declared in `billing.persistence.__all__` at M9.8/M9.9 but never imported, so both were unimportable by name — fixed additively. Six limitations recorded in the spec, chiefly that M9.5's minimal entitlement write surface still coexists with the engine. 199 new tests; repository-wide 2827 passing, zero regressions. | Accepted |
| DL-0119 | 2026-08-04 | Technical Owner | **M9.9 — Subscription Engine.** Implemented the commercial-agreement domain across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI: `CommercialSubscription` pinned to an account + published catalog version + published pricing version, a draft→trialing/active→past_due/suspended/paused→cancelled→expired→archived FSM, six item kinds on one agreement, billing cycles, trial/renewal/pause/cancellation/upgrade/downgrade policies, scheduled changes, immutable versions, 23 commands + 6 queries, 4 tables via migration 0019, 25 API paths under `/agreements`, and a 10-screen Studio. | A subscription is the fact that binds the three domains before it, so its three references are **immutable integer pins into frozen snapshots** rather than live objects — and deliberately not foreign keys, because a foreign key would make the catalog and pricing version lines a coupling the database enforces when the entire point of M9.6 and M9.7 is that they version independently; tests assert both directions of that non-interference. There is **no deletion state at all** — stronger than the catalog, pricing, and account lifecycles — because a subscription *is* the commercial agreement and ADR-0023 RS4 makes its record permanent. **Renewal is not an FSM edge**: it advances the billing cycle rather than changing state, and modelling it as a self-loop would have hidden a period computation inside a state machine; a test asserts the machine has no self-loops at all. **Scheduled changes are intent with no scheduler** — reading a due change applies nothing, which is what keeps 'what we agreed' auditable independently of 'what the system managed to run'. The domain carries **no money**: an agreement records what was agreed, and turning that into a charge is rating (M9.11), asserted by the absence of `Money` and every money operation across all eight modules. Period arithmetic is delegated to M9.2's `PeriodService` rather than duplicated — two answers to 'how long is a month' is exactly the drift §A2.5 prevents. | [subscription-engine.md](../docs/architecture/epic9/subscription-engine.md), ADR-0023, ADR-0031 | Additive: `BillingRepositories.subscriptions_engine`/`BillingContext.subscriptions_engine` default to `None`, so without wiring the Application is exactly what M9.8 assembled. M9.2's `Subscription` and `cp_billing_subscription` are untouched; the M9.3 command/query names are reused unchanged and M9.9's are named distinctly. 13 new `billing.*` event types, 16 new diagnostic codes, foundation exports 323→371. Migration chain extended to 0019; no `ALTER`, no Epic 5–8 table touched, Billing still absent from `platform/bootstrap.py`. Three defects found and fixed (item ids un-minted at creation; pricing-only change rejected; duplicated interval table). Roadmap labels M9.9 'Entitlement Engine' — the delivered order differs in label only, not in dependency order; recorded in the spec rather than silently reconciled. 165 new tests; repository-wide 2628 passing, zero regressions. | Accepted |
| DL-0118 | 2026-08-04 | Technical Owner | **M9.8 — Billing Accounts.** Implemented the commercial-account domain across all four Billing layers plus a Studio UI: `CommercialAccount` with a draft→active→suspended→closed→archived→deleted FSM, separate hierarchy and payer resolvers, a never-raising validator, deterministic search, a derived commercial profile, 14 commands + 5 queries on the existing buses, 3 tables via migration 0018, 17 API paths under `/billing-accounts`, and a 9-screen Studio at `/studio/accounts.html`. | The billing hierarchy is **not** organization tenancy (ADR-0023 §5.2): `org_id` is a string this domain scopes on and never dereferences, asserted by a test that forbids any Epic 8 import or org/workspace/project field — which is what makes reseller, OEM, and white-label a configuration exercise rather than a change to Epic 8. The **payer graph is modelled separately from the parent graph** because 'who consolidates the invoices' and 'who actually pays' are different questions in every reseller and enterprise arrangement; collapsing them is the modelling error that forces a redesign the first time a customer pays for a subsidiary it does not own, so a test builds exactly that case and asserts neither graph is malformed. There is **exactly one edge into DELETED and it starts at DRAFT** — a draft has billed nobody, everything past it has revenue attached, and ADR-0023 RS4 says revenue outlives the customer record; the test enumerates the whole FSM rather than checking one transition. `restore` deliberately returns an archived account to *closed*, not active: un-filing a record and reopening a commercial relationship are separate decisions, and conflating them lets the second happen by accident. Account numbers come from a stored per-org sequence, not `max + 1`, so deleting a draft never hands its number back to a different customer. A credit limit is a policy figure and this domain owns no balance, so `permits()` takes the exposure as an argument and no money arithmetic exists here at all. | [billing-accounts.md](../docs/architecture/epic9/billing-accounts.md), ADR-0023, ADR-0031 | Additive: `BillingRepositories.commercial_accounts`/`BillingContext.commercial_accounts` default to `None`, so without wiring the Application is exactly what M9.7 assembled.
