| DL-0109 | 2026-08-01 | Technical Owner | **M9.0C — Billing Build-vs-Buy & Provider Strategy accepted: Option C (own commercial truth, rent payment/tax edges).** Architecture governance only (no code/API/schema/repository/DB/migration/UI); moves **no** ownership boundary — it selects the implementation strategy *inside* the boundary ADR-0023 already fixed. Evaluated Stripe, Paddle, Lemon Squeezy, Chargebee, Recurly, custom billing, and hybrid — each twice, as an **owner** of the commercial model and as a **rented edge** — and compared Options A/B/C across all 22 requested dimensions (ownership, vendor lock-in, migration risk, enterprise contracts, offline licensing, marketplace, white-label, reseller, usage metering, credits, seat billing, hybrid billing, multi-provider, international taxation, payment methods, compliance, scalability, cost, developer experience, operational complexity, failure isolation, long-term flexibility). **Decision:** VoxVoice Billing owns commercial truth permanently in every market and channel (catalog, plans, price books, billing accounts, subscriptions, metering, usage records, rating, credits, discounts, entitlements, invoices/credit notes, ledger, revenue recognition, contracts, licenses); payment movement, tax determination, document rendering/delivery/e-invoicing, and accounting export are **rented edges behind adapters** (§A6 capability-plugin pattern, ADR-0002/ADR-0022 precedent). Ratified: providers own **payment movement only** and never own subscriptions/plans/pricing/entitlements/commercial catalog/revenue state; Billing stays authoritative **even while every provider is unavailable**; four adapter families (Payment/Tax/Document/Export) with one contract each and many implementations, **no vendor type crossing the seam**, capability flags + declared degradation, mandatory idempotency keyed by a Billing-generated operation identity, verified/deduplicated/ordered callbacks, bounded queues + retry budgets + circuit breakers (§A10/§A11.1), credential-seam secrets (rotatable, never in code/logs/VCS), and a **deterministic offline adapter as the default outside production** (the Epic 8 `EchoTransport` / ADR-0022 Local-adapter posture — the billing suite opens no socket and moves no money); **multi-provider designed in from day one** (domestic-INR PSP + global PSP, deterministic recorded routing by market/currency/method/segment/health, per-provider reconciliation against **one** ledger); **merchant-of-record rented per market as a distribution *channel*, never as an owner**; **provider replacement must never migrate customers** (only instrument re-tokenization — subscriptions, prices, entitlements, invoice lineage, numbering, and billing anniversaries never move). | Per the M9.0C prompt (choose one permanent strategy; explain every rejection; support multi-provider, provider replacement, enterprise contracts, hybrid/usage/seat billing, credits, marketplace, reseller, white-label without future redesign). Key judgment calls: (a) **Option B is rejected as *unratifiable*, not merely suboptimal** — Stripe Billing/Paddle/Lemon Squeezy/Chargebee/Recurly as owners would write plans, pricing, subscriptions, entitlements, and catalog, which ADR-0023 §5–§7 and §A2.5 forbid; adopting it would require superseding ADR-0023 in the same quarter it was ratified, and it recreates the read-copy divergence ADR-0021 rejected for Studio. (b) **Option A is rejected on delivery reality, not architecture** — it is ADR-0023-compliant but puts a small team on tax-determination correctness, PCI scope, and payment-method breadth, none of which are the product, while buying **no ownership Option C lacks**. (c) **Lock-in asymmetry is the decision's spine**: renting money movement is reversible (re-tokenize instruments); renting commercial truth is not (leaving means migrating live customers, anniversaries, and invoice lineage) — so VoxVoice rents only what it can replace. (d) **MoR is retained as a channel, never an owner** — this preserves Option B's one genuine strength (global tax-registration absorption) without the ownership inversion, and is precisely what keeps **white-label** achievable (an MoR *is* the seller to the partner's customer, which would leak the vendor's brand into the partner's product). (e) **Enterprise revenue often has no PSP at all** (offline settlement against a net-N invoice with a PO) — a first-class path under C and an awkward exception under any vendor-owned model. (f) **Multi-provider and the offline deterministic adapter are built in from day one** because both are cheap now and re-architectures later. (g) **Vendor commercial terms are treated as directional planning inputs to re-verify at contract time**, never quoted as fact — the architecture is deliberately indifferent to which vendor wins. observe through existing event/metric seams; reuse Audit Platform / Runtime / Provider Binding / Knowledge / Deployment / Playground / Product Runtime; no Runtime/Deployment/Provider redesign; append-only; no polling; no direct Runtime access; no billing; no notifications; no autoscaling; must remain a **passive consumer**). Key judgment calls: (a) **Observability = a projection layer over `AuditReader.stream()`** (the M8.9 activity-feed pattern generalised) — every metric/log/trace/health/usage/cost/dashboard is computed on read from events the platform already emits, so it is a passive consumer by construction, append-only, no polling, and **adds no new events on read** (metrics/logs/traces are not new facts — they are views). This is the load-bearing simplification that makes the whole surface faithful *and* free of new durable state. (b) **Only alert rules are durable** — they are customer *configuration*; `AlertState`/`AlertEvent`/`AlertHistory` are **computed on demand** by evaluating a rule's metric over its window, never stored, so there is no background poller and no notification side effect (both explicitly out of scope). (c) **The log store is the audit store** — each `AuditRecord` projects to one `LogEntry` (level/category derived from the event type); Observability does not duplicate audit events (§Audit). (d) **Traces are a first-party distributed model** (correlate by session/deployment/resource → spans) with **no OpenTelemetry dependency**, keeping the boundary clean and offline-deterministic. (e) **Health consumes, never probes** — component status is derived from the success/failure event ratio + the Deployment/Runtime health events already emitted; a no-signal component never drags a healthy platform down. (f) **Cost is an estimate, not billing** — a transparent rate table over usage with a provider breakdown; no invoices/ledgers. (g) **Reuse-first RBAC** — reads authorize by org scope (a viewer may observe); the only writes (alert-rule config) require existing `ORG_MANAGE`; no new scope. (h) **Boundary discipline** — the trace hash uses `hashlib` rather than `product.ids` so the Foundation imports no frozen Epic. Frozen Epics 5/6/7 remain byte-for-byte unchanged. | [observability-platform](../docs/architecture/epic8/observability-platform.md), [audit-activity-platform](../docs/architecture/epic8/audit-activity-platform.md), [deployment-actuator](../docs/architecture/epic8/deployment-actuator.md), [knowledge-tool](../docs/architecture/epic8/knowledge-tool.md), [provider-adapter-binding](../docs/architecture/epic8/provider-adapter-binding.md), Constitution §A5/§A9/§A11.2 | **Observability shipped and green — the Definition of Done (observe conversations/deployments/provider-usage/knowledge-usage/tool-usage; view traces/logs/metrics/health/alerts/usage-analytics/estimated-costs, via Studio UI + Control Plane API) is met end-to-end** (`test_definition_of_done_end_to_end`), and a dedicated test proves reading every projection adds **zero** audit facts (true passive consumer). Recommend next: (i) a materialised metric store / rollup + a streaming projector behind the same read services when the audit stream grows; (ii) a scheduled evaluator + a `Notification` sink (email/Slack/webhook) if/when alerting-out-of-band is in scope; (iii) real provider pricing behind the cost rate table if billing is later pursued. OpenTelemetry/autoscaling/billing/notifications remain out of scope. Frozen Epics 5/6/7 remain untouched. | Accepted |

**no authorization** — identity only; dev `issue_token`), **authorization integration** (controllers forward `actor=context.user_id`+path `org_id` into Application commands; the Application authorizes against the Foundation authorizer — controllers never evaluate permissions), **request context** (`RequestContext`: request/correlation ids + user/org/workspace + trace, on `request.state`), **API modules** (thin routers grouped by domain: auth/organizations/workspaces/members/projects/environments/preferences/system — no agent/knowledge routes), **request models** (Pydantic HTTP DTOs distinct from Application commands + Foundation views; HTTP DTO→command→view DTO→response DTO, no domain object exposed), **error handling** (centralized handlers translating FoundationValidationError→422+diagnostics, Authorization/OrganizationIsolation→403, NotFound→404, Conflict→409, JWTError→401, NoHandler/ApplicationError→400, PlatformError/unexpected→500 with a uniform envelope; 5xx leak nothing), **middleware** (correlation ids, request timing, in-process `RequestMetrics`, structured access logging, security headers + GZip + CORS), **health** (`/health` liveness, `/ready` verifying configuration + DB connectivity via `SELECT 1` + Application wiring → 503 when unready, `/version`; **no runtime checks**), **OpenAPI** (auto-generated: tags, descriptions, examples, per-route error responses, HTTP bearer security scheme, v1.0.0), and **configuration** (`CustomerApiSettings` env-driven, validated — production may not run on the default JWT secret, §A2.6). 15 API integration tests (full HTTP flow, auth 401 cases, DI replaceability, error translation, health/version, OpenAPI generation, middleware headers, and architecture-compliance assertions); **1610/1610 repository-wide (prior 1595 + 15, zero regression); ruff clean across `api`; the M8.2 Foundation, M8.3 Application, M8.4 Persistence, and Epics 5/6/7 are byte-for-byte unchanged** (git-verified: additions under `api/customer/` + one new test; no architectural redesign; no new ADRs; no ownership changes" mandate. Key judgment calls: (a) **Separate `create_app()` rather than editing `api/main.py`** — the existing Control-Plane app is used by legacy tests (conftest imports `api.main.app`); a new factory under `api/customer/` keeps M8.5 additive and both apps independent (no new top-level package → no placement ADR). (b) **Dependency-free HS256 JWT** — hand-rolled with stdlib `hmac`/`hashlib`/`base64` rather than adding PyJWT, matching the repo's "no external SDK" ethos (product/skill validation precedent) and avoiding a dependency change. (c) **Authentication resolves identity, authorization stays in the Application** — the `authenticate` dependency never checks permissions; org-scoped routes take `org_id` from the path and the Application's use-case authz orchestrator (calling the Foundation authorizer) makes the ALLOW/DENY decision, so a controller passing an org the caller can't access yields a clean 403 with no controller-side permission logic (ADR-0021 honoured; test asserts domain routers contain no `Repository`/`UnitOfWork`/`PermissionEvaluator`/`session`/`authorize(`). (d) **Thin controllers by construction** — every controller is request-parse → `bus.dispatch(command|query)` → `Response.from_view(...)`; no transaction/repo/permission code, verified by a source-scan test (scoped to domain routers; the system/health router legitimately pings `database.session` for the mandated readiness check). (g) **Full DI replaceability** — tests build a `Container` over an in-memory SQLite with `SequentialIdGenerator`+`FixedClock` and inject it, so no production wiring runs; `TestClient(create_app(container=...))` drives real HTTP end-to-end. | [customer-platform-control-plane-api](../docs/architecture/epic8/customer-platform-control-plane-api.md), [customer-platform-persistence-layer](../docs/architecture/epic8/customer-platform-persistence-layer.md), [customer-platform-application-layer](../docs/architecture/epic8/customer-platform-application-layer.md), [customer-platform-foundation](../docs/architecture/epic8/customer-platform-foundation.md), [epic8-implementation-plan](../docs/roadmap/epic8-implementation-plan.md), [ADR-0021](../docs/adr/ADR-0021-studio-consumer-and-management-surface.md), Constitution §A2.6/§A4/§A11.2/§A11.3/§A11.4 | **Control Plane API shipped and green.** Recommend proceeding to **M8.6 (the `studio/` frontend — requires its CODING_STANDARDS §2 placement ADR at kickoff)** and/or hardening (real IdP/credential store; per-request `scoped_session` + connection pooling for multi-worker). | DL-0092 | 2026-07-27 | Technical Owner | **M8.4 — Customer Platform Persistence Layer (third Epic 8 implementation).** Implemented `platform/api/persistence/` (11 modules incl. a `migrations/` package + 1 test file, 9 tests) — the only production implementation of the M8.2 Foundation's repository interfaces and the M8.3 Application's `UnitOfWork` abstraction, **swapped in at a composition root with no interface change**. **Unit of Work** — `SqlUnitOfWork`/`SqlUnitOfWorkFactory` implementing the existing Application `UnitOfWork` abstraction over a real DB transaction (commit → `session.commit()` then publish staged application events; rollback → `session.rollback()` + discard) — giving **atomic rollback of repository writes**, which closes the honest limitation M8.3 documented (in-memory rolled back events but not data; SQL rolls back both); **Mapping** — pure `mapping.py` (Domain→Persistence Model→Domain), no `*Row`/`sqlmodel` type leaking into Foundation/Application; **Query Optimization** — reusable org-scoped SELECT helpers (`get_by_pk`/`first_where`/`all_where`) + indexed `cp_*` columns, no business logic; **Migrations** — a declarative `migrations/` structure (`0001` initial upgrade/downgrade over `CP_TABLES`, ordered append-only `MIGRATIONS`, README), no application logic; plus a **composition root** (`build_persistent_application`/`build_foundation`) wiring the SQL repos + Unit of Work into the Foundation/Application via their existing `create`/`UnitOfWorkFactory` seams. 9 persistence-backed integration tests + the existing in-memory unit tests kept; **1595/1595 repository-wide (prior 1586 + 9, zero regression); ruff clean across `api`; the M8.2 Foundation, M8.3 Application, and Epics 5/6/7 are byte-for-byte unchanged** (git-verified: all changes additions under `api/persistence/` + one new test; zero tracked-source modifications). | Per the M8.4 prompt's explicit "replace the in-memory repositories with production-grade persistence while keeping Application and Foundation unchanged; the Persistence Layer becomes the only implementation of the repository interfaces; no interface changes; maintain the existing UnitOfWork abstraction; no persistence leakage into Foundation; no FastAPI/HTTP" mandate. Key judgment calls: (a) **Composition-root direction** — persistence imports `api.foundation` + `api.application` + the ORM and is wired in by `build_persistent_application`; the Foundation and Application import **nothing** from persistence (AST-verified in the test), so both layers stay byte-for-byte unchanged and unaware of the DB — the same inversion `pipeline`/`integration` use. (b) **Delivered real atomicity** — because the Foundation services do multiple repo writes per operation (e.g. create_organization writes org+membership+role-binding) and the Application already wraps each write use case in `with uow:`, backing the Unit of Work with a real DB transaction over the shared session makes all those writes atomic; a focused test raises inside a Unit of Work and asserts the DB row is absent afterward — proving the capability M8.3's in-memory Unit of Work explicitly lacked. (c) **Repositories never commit** — the Unit of Work owns the boundary (prompt: "maintain the existing abstraction"); repos `flush()` so reads-within-tx see their writes but durability/rollback is the Unit of Work's, honouring "Application owns transaction boundaries". (d) **No persistence leakage** — `*Row` SQLModel types are confined to the package; repos return frozen domain aggregates (asserted by a test that the returned type is `Organization`, not a `*Row`), so the Foundation/Application never import `sqlmodel`. (e) **`cp_*` table namespacing + explicit `create_all(tables=...)`** — avoids any collision with the legacy Control-Plane tables that share `SQLModel.metadata`, and creates only the Customer-Platform tables; referential integrity stays at the domain layer (Foundation existence checks) so tables use indexed columns not DB FKs (decoupled creation; FKs deferred to a later migration). (f) **Kept, did not replace, the in-memory unit tests** — the default `ApplicationFactory.default()`/`FoundationFactory.default()` wiring is unchanged and still exercised; the new persistence tests are the integration layer, matching "keep all existing unit tests". (g) **Named scale limits plainly** — a single shared session is correct for the single-worker Control Plane (§A9 Stage 1–2); per-request `scoped_session` is the documented Stage-3+ multi-worker upgrade. | [customer-platform-persistence-layer](../docs/architecture/epic8/customer-platform-persistence-layer.md), [customer-platform-application-layer](../docs/architecture/epic8/customer-platform-application-layer.md), [customer-platform-foundation](../docs/architecture/epic8/customer-platform-foundation.md), [epic8-implementation-plan](../docs/roadmap/epic8-implementation-plan.md), Constitution §A2.4/§A7/§A9/§A11.2/§A11.3 | **Persistence Layer shipped and green; production repositories + transactional Unit of Work are the only implementation of the repository interfaces.** Recommend proceeding to **M8.5 (the FastAPI Control-Plane router/HTTP layer over the M8.3 buses — the API layer)**, then the `studio/` frontend (with its placement ADR). Frozen Epics 5/6/7, the M8.2 Foundation, and the M8.3 Application remain untouched. | Accepted |

Key judgment calls: (a) **Resolved the framing that makes a customer SaaS dashboard non-contradictory with a frozen runtime** — rather than inventing a new control surface that would own tenant/session/billing state (a §A2.5 "one writer per fact" and §A11.1 boundary violation), the blueprint defines Studio as a Consumer + management surface whose every noun is a projection of, or authoring input to, an already-frozen capability; this single decision (proposed as ADR-0021) is what keeps all 24 modules constitutional. (b) **Named the one honest gap rather than papering over it** — Epic 7 deliberately froze at deployment *intent* (`DeploymentPlan`, never actuates), so Studio's "Deploy" button requires a **new additive Deployment Actuator** Epic-8 milestone over Epic 7's existing scheduling/approval/GitOps/K8s/Terraform seams; flagged as risk R2 + recommended ADR-0022 instead of implying deploy already works (mirrors the M5.8/M6/M7 "state known limitations plainly" discipline). (c) **Bound Studio forms to capability flags, not vendors** (§A6) so the provider-portability promise (I3) can't be silently broken by the UI encoding vendor specifics (risk R6). (d) **Kept Coach an advisor, not an autonomous owner** — it composes existing runtimes and proposes artifacts applied only through the standard approval/audit path, preserving RBAC/audit and the frozen runtimes; opt-in autonomy deferred to Future with no privileged path. (e) **Recommended, never ratified, all four ADRs** — a design milestone produces recommendations for the ADR workflow, exactly as the Epic 6/7 Phase-0 design docs did (proposed-not-ratified precedent). | [product-blueprint](../docs/architecture/epic8/product-blueprint.md), [engineering-constitution-v2](../docs/architecture/engineering-constitution-v2.md), [epic7 freeze](../docs/releases/epic7-product-runtime-v1.0.md), [epic6 freeze](../docs/releases/epic6-execution-platform-v1.0.md), [epic5 freeze](../docs/releases/epic5-runtime-platform-v1.0.md), [INDEX](../docs/INDEX.md), Constitution §A0/§A1/§A4/§A6/§A7/§A11.2/§A12 | Product blueprint approved as the master reference for Epic 8 and downstream customer-platform Epics. Recommend proceeding to **M8.1** implementation planning after ADR-0021 ratification; ADR-0022/0023/0024 to be authored and ratified before their respective features implement. All frozen Epics (5/6/7) remain untouched. | Accepted (design) |

The repaired proposal is re-validated and re-checked against the full `evaluate_stop()` (now genuinely exercising the `convergence` branch — comparing the repaired proposal's content-hash to the proposal that started the iteration) before either stopping or becoming the next iteration's starting proposal. New `loop/replay/` subpackage (`domain.py`: `LoopReplayRecord`/`ReplayComparison`; `ids.py`; `replay.py`: `loop_replay_record_for()`; `replay_engine.py`: `LoopReplayEngine.validate`/`.compare`) — pure hash-chain re-verification over the ordered `LoopIteration` sequence via a new `iteration_hash_for()` helper (already present in Bundle 1's `loop.core.ids`, now genuinely exercised), never a live re-run; tamper detection test-proven via `dataclasses.replace` on a real record. | DL-0065 | 2026-07-25 | Technical Owner | **M6.6A Loop Engine Design (design-only, zero code)**. The complete Loop Engine architecture (`docs/architecture/epic6/loop-engine-design.md`), superseding Phase 0's brief sketch. Loop Engine is a deterministic propose→critique→repair→validate→stop-check orchestration **library** — never a Runtime/Agent/Workflow/Skill/Tool/Provider. **The first Epic 6 capability designed with no registrable identity at all**: no Registry, no Manifest, no Descriptor, no Lifecycle FSM, no Repository, no Runtime facade — every prior Epic 6 capability (Tool/Skill/Hooks/Workflow/Agent) is a registrable, discoverable, versioned closed runtime; Loop Engine is invoked directly by a caller instead, the same way a caller uses `core.fsm.StateMachine`/`core.policies.RetryPolicy` directly rather than through a registry. `LoopDefinition`/`LoopStoppingPolicy`/`LoopProposal`/`LoopCritique`/`LoopRepair`/`LoopValidation`/`LoopIteration`/`LoopResult`/`LoopStatistics`/`LoopReplayRecord`/`LoopExecutionContext` designed as data-only shapes. **Propose/critique/repair are ordinary `core.ports.reasoning.ReasoningPort.run` calls, deterministically parsed — no bespoke intelligence in `loop/` code**, extending Agent Runtime's own "planning is an ordinary `ReasoningPort.run` call" precedent (DL-0042 point b) a second time. **Validation is entirely delegated to an injected `LoopValidator` seam — Loop Engine never evaluates correctness itself**, the platform's clearest three-way split yet of structure (Loop Engine) vs. intelligence (Reasoning Runtime) vs. correctness (the caller's own domain logic). Stopping/convergence is a set of mechanical, deterministic checks over already-computed facts (`max_iterations` integer comparison, `LoopValidation.passed` read, confidence-threshold float comparison, structural proposal-content-hash equality for convergence, an external `LoopCancellationToken`) — mirrors `WorkflowFailurePolicy`'s own "policy over already-computed facts" discipline. `LoopExecutionContext` is designed as a **wholly independent shape, explicitly never extending or duplicating `AgentExecutionContext`** (the milestone's own explicit instruction) — reconciled with the platform's otherwise-consistent "extend by structural duplication" precedent by noting Loop Engine sits outside the Skill→Workflow→Agent delegation lineage entirely (a general-purpose primitive any future caller may adopt, not a callee one level below Agent Runtime specifically). `LoopReplayRecord`/replay engine: pure hash-chain re-verification over the ordered iteration-hash sequence, never a live re-run — the same discipline applied a fourth time after Workflow's and Agent's own replay records. **No persistence of any kind is designed into Loop Engine** — unlike every other Epic 6 capability (each ships at least a temporary repository), a future adopter's own repository would carry iteration history if resumability across a process restart is ever needed. `loop → core[, tenant]` only, with `core.ports.reasoning.ReasoningPort` as the sole external dependency (already-verified conformance, costs nothing extra, the same case as Workflow's `ToolPort` use and Agent's `ReasoningPort`/`ContextPort` use) — zero `tool`/`skill`/`hooks`/`workflow`/`agent` import designed anywhere. 6 ADRs recommended. Exactly 3 implementation bundles (M6.6B-1 core/loop-model/policies → M6.6B-2 execution/replay/observability/diagnostics → M6.6C production certification), per this milestone's explicit instruction. Zero Tool/Skill/Hook/Workflow/Agent/Epic 5 changes (`git status` verified: documentation-only diff). | Per the M6.6A prompt's explicit "design milestone, no implementation, Loop Engine MUST NOT modify any existing runtime, it is NOT a Runtime/Agent/Workflow/Skill/Tool/Provider, no Registry/Manifest/Descriptor/Lifecycle FSM/Repository, Reasoning must occur only through ReasoningPort, validation is delegated, never reuse AgentExecutionContext" mandate. Key calls: (a) **The "no registrable identity" call required explicitly naming why**, not just following the milestone's own architecture section literally — a Loop is a bounded, single-invocation computation with all its inputs supplied at the call site, structurally unlike a Tool/Skill/Hook/Workflow/Agent, each of which is a capability a caller discovers and reuses across many invocations by `(namespace, id, version)`. Recording this distinction explicitly (ADR item 1) guards against a future contributor "completing the pattern" with an unnecessary `LoopRegistry`. (b) **Validation-as-delegated required distinguishing it from critique, which is not delegated** — both "assess" a proposal, but critique is soft/LLM-judged (routes through `ReasoningPort`, feeds repair) while validation is a hard, domain-specific correctness check (routes through the caller's own `LoopValidator`, feeds the stop decision); conflating them would let Loop Engine's own reasoning silently stand in for the caller's correctness check, exactly the outcome the milestone's "Loop Engine never evaluates correctness itself" instruction forecloses. (c) **`LoopExecutionContext`'s independence from `AgentExecutionContext` required reconciling, not just obeying, the platform's own precedent** — every prior "extend a context" decision (`WorkflowExecutionContext`→`SkillExecutionContext`, `AgentExecutionContext`→`WorkflowExecutionContext`) extended a shape within the same delegation lineage (Skill→Workflow→Agent, a strict "caller above delegates to callee below" chain); Loop Engine has no place in that lineage, so the milestone's "never reuse `AgentExecutionContext`" instruction is the *correct* application of the same underlying principle, not an exception to it. (d) **No persistence, no repository, no factory** — since Loop Engine is invoked once per loop rather than composed into a long-lived object, there is nothing for a `LoopFactory`/`LoopRepository` to manage; any future adopter's own composition root and repository carry that responsibility instead. | [loop-engine-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/loop-engine-design.md), [epic6-agent-platform-architecture](../docs/architecture/epic6/epic6-agent-platform-architecture.md), [agent-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/agent-runtime-design.md), [workflow-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/workflow-runtime-design.md), Constitution §A6/§A12 | Loop Engine design approved. Key calls: (a) **The goal FSM is implemented as real, directly-testable structure without a live carrier record** — mirroring how Workflow Runtime's own Bundle 1 left its execution lifecycle entirely undefined, this bundle takes the milestone's own "Goal Lifecycle (structure only)" instruction literally by defining the full `AgentGoalState` FSM and a bare-value `advance_goal_state` function, while deliberately not giving `AgentGoal` a `state` field or any engine to drive it — a dedicated test proves both halves of this (the FSM transitions correctly in isolation; `AgentGoal` has no `state` field; no execution/planning module exists yet). `WorkflowFailurePolicy` PROPAGATE/COMPENSATE/IGNORE). **A retry-exhausted node failure pauses the execution at `RECOVERING`** — a real, caller-invocable, non-terminal state (mirroring exactly how an EVENT node pauses at `PAUSED`) — with two explicit, mutually-exclusive continuations: `recover()` (resumes from the snapshot's own last checkpoint, re-attempting only `snapshot.current_node`, never restarting from `graph.entry_node`) and the new `give_up()` (finalizes as terminally `FAILED`, applying `node.failure_policy`'s COMPENSATE/PROPAGATE split only at that point, never automatically). `WorkflowExecutionContext` implements the M6.4A design's own §8 structural-duplication shape field-for-field. `WorkflowCheckpoint`/`WorkflowSnapshot` (the latter gained an additive `pending_error` field to honestly carry *why* a RECOVERING pause happened, consumed by `give_up()`) + the durable `WorkflowExecutionRepository` (deliberately has no `*_ttl_s` config field anywhere — the absence itself is this bundle's durability signal, test-asserted). `WorkflowReplayRecord`/`WorkflowReplayEngine` (pure hash-chain re-verification over the checkpoint chain, never a live re-run). `InMemoryWorkflowObservabilitySink` — a first concrete `WorkflowObservabilitySink` that `WorkflowExecutionRuntime` actually calls from its own recording path, not just the durable repository. Diagnostics/statistics/serializer/config/factory. Dispatch never executes a Tool/Skill/Hook directly — Tool nodes route through the already-frozen `core.ports.tool.ToolPort`; Skill/Hook nodes route through the Workflow-owned, still-unimplemented `WorkflowSkillPort`/`WorkflowHookAdapter` seams declared in Bundle 1's (untouched) `extensions.py`. 6 additive `core.events.EventType` members (`workflow.failed`, `workflow.cancelled`, `workflow.replay.recorded`/`validated`/`mismatch`, `workflow.diagnostics.generated`), reusing Phase 0's pre-existing `WORKFLOW_STARTED`/`WORKFLOW_STEP`/`WORKFLOW_COMPLETED` scaffolding verbatim for the started/checkpoint/completed cases rather than duplicating them. `workflow → core[, tenant]` only — zero `tool`/`skill`/`hooks` import, confirmed by direct AST scan over the whole `workflow/` tree including `execution/`. 51 new tests (1097/1097 repository-wide, up from 1046); ruff clean. Bundle 1 (`workflow/domain.py`, `workflow/registry.py`, `workflow/graph.py`, `workflow/lifecycle.py`, `workflow/runtime.py`, ...) and every other certified runtime (Tool Runtime, Skill Runtime, Hook Framework) confirmed untouched via `git diff --stat`. | Per the M6.4B-2 prompt's explicit "implement ONLY Bundle 2, do not redesign approved architecture, never import Tool Runtime/Skill Runtime/Hook Framework, use only the WorkflowSkillPort/WorkflowHookAdapter interface seams" mandate. Key calls: (a) **A genuine design bug was caught and fixed during this bundle's own test-writing pass, before commit**: the first implementation of the failure-handling branch auto-advanced `RECOVERING` straight through to a terminal `FAILED`/`COMPENSATING→FAILED` transition within the same `_continue_loop` call, which made `engine.recover()` permanently unreachable (`core.fsm` correctly refuses to fire any trigger from a terminal state) and reduced "recovery" to dead code that could never actually run. The fix — pausing at `RECOVERING` and requiring an explicit `recover()` or `give_up()` call, exactly mirroring the already-correct EVENT/`PAUSED` pattern — was arrived at by taking the test failures at face value rather than weakening the test to fit the buggy behavior. (c) **`WorkflowExecutionContext` was implemented as a field-for-field structural duplicate of `SkillExecutionContext`, verified by direct construction rather than by convention alone** — every field name the M6.4A design's §8 committed to is present with the same type, and a dedicated test asserts this by introspecting the constructed object, not by re-reading the design doc's prose. | DL-0056 | 2026-07-24 | Technical Owner | **M6.4A Workflow Runtime Design (design-only, zero code)**. The complete Workflow Runtime architecture (`docs/architecture/epic6/workflow-runtime-design.md`), superseding Phase 0's brief §10 sketch. A Workflow is a deterministic orchestration graph — nodes and edges, frozen and content-hashed — that coordinates already-existing Skill/Tool/Hook capabilities; it owns orchestration only, never reasoning, tool execution, skill execution, provider routing, memory retrieval, or hook implementation. **Restores Tool Runtime's dual-FSM shape** (descriptor lifecycle DRAFT→REGISTERED→ACTIVE→DEPRECATED→RETIRED, plus a second execution lifecycle PENDING→RUNNING→{PAUSED,RECOVERING,COMPENSATING}→terminal) — a deliberate divergence from Skill/Hook's single-FSM asymmetry, since a Workflow, unlike a Skill or a Hook, genuinely executes over time. `WorkflowGraph` cycle detection is designed as a real, primary topological-sort/DFS algorithm, not defense-in-depth — because a whole graph arrives as one complete manifest at registration, unlike Skill Runtime's incrementally-built dependency graph where a true cycle is structurally unreachable through the public API. 7 node kinds (Skill/Tool/Conditional/Parallel/Merge/Delay/Event) modeled as one data-only `WorkflowNode` shape with a `kind` discriminator plus a generic `config` escape hatch — never a class hierarchy; edges carry a small, closed condition grammar (field/operator/value against accumulated execution variables), never arbitrary code or `eval()`; parallel fan-out/fan-in join order is edge-declaration order, never completion order, preserving replay determinism. Checkpoint strategy is checkpoint-after-every-completed-node, deterministic by construction (never wall-clock-sampled). Workflow state (`WorkflowSnapshot`, formalizing Phase 0's `WorkflowInstance`) is designed as the platform's one deliberate durable-repository exception (ratifies Phase 0's already-proposed ADR-0009), reusing `core.providers.stores`' existing `StoreCapabilities.durable` flag unchanged. `WorkflowExecutionContext` extends `SkillExecutionContext` by structural field duplication — every field name/type `SkillExecutionContext` already has, duplicated verbatim, plus additive Workflow-specific fields — never by inheritance or import, reconciling the milestone's "extend, preserve backward compatibility" requirement with "never import Skill Runtime's implementation packages." Hook integration reuses `HookPoint.BEFORE_WORKFLOW_STEP`/`AFTER_WORKFLOW_STEP` + `HookRuntimeScope.WORKFLOW` verbatim (zero Hook Framework change) for per-node hooks; recommends, but does not perform, an additive 3-member `HookPoint` extension (`before_workflow`/`after_workflow`/`workflow_failure`) for whole-execution hooks, exercising Hook Framework's own pre-declared AR-32 additive-extension mechanism rather than a redesign. Since neither Skill Runtime nor Hook Framework exposes a `core.ports` entry, Workflow declares its own seam interfaces (`WorkflowSkillPort`, `WorkflowHookAdapter`) rather than importing either package directly, keeping `workflow → core[, tenant]` exactly true with zero exception — including for Tool Runtime, whose interface already lives inside `core.ports.tool.ToolPort` and therefore costs nothing extra to reach. Retry reuses `core.policies.RetryPolicy` verbatim (confirmed present by direct inspection); cancellation duplicates `pipeline.PipelineCancellation`'s shape locally, per the established precedent. Policies/Observability/Replay: ownership declared only in `extensions.py`, zero implementation (explicitly Bundle 2 scope per this milestone's own instruction). 5 ADRs recommended. Exactly 3 implementation bundles (M6.4B-1 core/registry/graph/lifecycle → M6.4B-2 execution/checkpoints/recovery/replay/observability → M6.4C production certification), per this milestone's explicit instruction. deterministic `ordered_hooks` (priority descending — explicit, declared, never inferred — then the monotonic `sequence` as the sole tie-break); `HookExecutionPolicy` (`default_policy_for`: `before_*` points fail-closed with DENY-wins aggregation, `after_*`/observational points fail-open, isolation always mandatory — every hook runs in its own try/except, one failure never blocks siblings, and every isolated failure is recorded regardless of failure mode) + `aggregate_verdicts` (DENY_WINS/ALL_MUST_ALLOW/FIRST_MODIFY_WINS, pure and deterministic); `HookEngine` (collects verdicts, applies aggregation, never executes a Tool, never executes a Skill, never performs planning, never mutates state beyond returning a result); `HookEvent`/`HookVerdict` (ALLOW/DENY/MODIFY — `MODIFY` carries only `modified_payload_hash` + `metadata`, never inline mutation logic); `HookExecutionTrace` + `InMemoryHookObservabilitySink` (the first concrete `HookObservabilitySink` — planning-free, since the Hook Framework never plans anything); `HookReplayRecord` + `HookReplayEngine` (a pure hash-chain re-verification over stored documents, never a live re-run); `HookRuntimeDiagnostics`/`HookRuntimeStatistics`/`HookRepository` (temporary registration+replay+trace log — the registry itself is the durable-for-process catalog)/`HookSerializer`/`HookConfiguration`/`HookFactory`. `HookCategory` (validation/transformation/audit/notification/metrics) implemented exactly as descriptive manifest metadata — AST-verified, not just asserted, that no ordering/filtering/policy/engine module reads a `.category` attribute or references `HookCategory` by name. 5 additive `core.events.EventType` members (`hook.run.completed`, `hook.replay.recorded`/`validated`/`mismatch`, `hook.diagnostics.generated`) + one additive `EventSource.HOOKS` member. 75 new tests (996/996 repository-wide, up from 921); | DL-0053 | 2026-07-24 | Technical Owner | **M6.3A Hook Framework Design (design-only, zero code)**. The complete Hook Framework architecture (`docs/architecture/epic6/hook-framework-design.md`), superseding Phase 0's brief §9 sketch. A Hook is a deterministic, cross-cutting lifecycle-interception point with an explicit verdict contract (ALLOW/DENY/MODIFY) — never a Skill, Tool, Agent, Workflow, Provider, or business logic; the Framework owns the interception mechanism, never what a hook decides. Owns hook registration/discovery/manifests/descriptors/lifecycle/priority/ordering/filtering/execution-policy/context/metadata/observability/replay-metadata; never Tool execution, Skill planning, Workflow orchestration, Agent reasoning, or Provider routing. `HookManifest`/`HookDescriptor` apply the platform's now-standard manifest/descriptor/registry/lifecycle pattern (a new decision beyond Phase 0's original `HookEvent`/`HookVerdict`/`HookRegistry`/`HookEngine`-only sketch). One `core.fsm` lifecycle only (DRAFT→REGISTERED→ACTIVE→DEPRECATED→RETIRED) — the same deliberate asymmetry Skill Runtime established, since a hook never executes a multi-phase action, just returns one verdict. Ordering model: explicit, manifest-declared `priority` (descending), with a monotonic registration `sequence` (assigned by the registry) as the sole tie-break. `HookExecutionPolicy` formalizes Phase 0's fail-closed-`before_*`/fail-open-`after_*` posture as a concrete default configuration, with mandatory per-hook isolation (one hook's exception never prevents sibling hooks at the same point from running — collected, not first-wins). `HookEvent` (renamed from Phase 0's "event" to avoid confusion with `core.events.Event`) — immutable, provider-neutral, inline session/trace/org/workspace fields, no `tenant.TenantContext`/`pipeline.RequestContext` import, identical precedent to `ToolRequest`/`GovernanceIdentity`/`SkillExecutionContext`. `HookVerdict.MODIFY` carries a content-hash reference (`modified_payload_hash`) only — the Framework never applies the modification itself, that is always the calling runtime's own responsibility. `hooks → core[, tenant]` only — a library every future consumer runtime imports, never the reverse, the same shape `core.fsm`/`core.events` already have. Integration into Tool Runtime and Skill Runtime is explicitly **deferred** (both certified and frozen; wiring hooks in requires a future, explicit unfreeze decision, not a Hook Framework milestone reaching in); Workflow Runtime and Agent Runtime get hook call-sites for free since they don't exist yet, confirming Hook Framework's place in the milestone sequence is deliberate, not incidental. 5 ADRs recommended (formal ratification of Phase 0's already-proposed ADR-0010, plus 4 new: manifest/descriptor pattern applied to Hooks; priority-is-declared-never-inferred, reconciling Phase 0's own language; MODIFY-carries-a-hash-reference-only; no-direct-runtime-dependency-either-direction). Exactly 2 future phases per this milestone's own explicit instruction (M6.3B complete implementation → M6.3C production certification) — supersedes Phase 0's tentative "M6.3A design / M6.3B-1 core / M6.3B-2 intelligence" placeholder numbering. Zero Tool/Skill/Epic 5 changes (`git status` verified: documentation-only diff). | Per the M6.3A prompt's explicit "design milestone, no implementation, do NOT modify any certified runtime, hooks must integrate through published extension points only" mandate. Key calls: (a) **Hooks get the full manifest/descriptor/registry/lifecycle treatment even though Phase 0 only sketched `HookEvent`/`HookVerdict`/`HookRegistry`/`HookEngine`** — every other registrable entity in the repository (Tool, Skill) gets this treatment, and Hooks being independently registrable and lifecycle-managed (plausibly by third parties) makes omitting it a larger inconsistency than adding it. (b) **"Priority is declared, never inferred" resolves an apparent tension, not a contradiction** — Phase 0's "never priority-inferred" language was about the Framework never guessing importance from usage/scoring; an explicit, caller-supplied manifest field is data, not an inference, exactly like a `SkillDependency` is declared data rather than an inferred edge. Registration-order remains the sole tie-break, so Phase 0's determinism guarantee is fully preserved, not weakened. (c) **`MODIFY` verdicts stay a content-hash reference, never inline mutation code** — keeps `HookVerdict` serializable, replayable, and free of any live payload-mutation path inside a package that must remain provider- and runtime-neutral; applying a modification is deliberately left to whichever runtime owns the original payload. (d) **Tool/Skill integration is explicitly deferred, not silently assumed** — both runtimes are certified and frozen; the design records that wiring hook call-sites into them requires its own future, explicit unfreeze milestone, guarding against the likely shortcut of "just add one small hook call, it's tiny." (e) **Exactly two future phases, not three** — Hooks are, by design, the simplest extension shape in the platform (`Hook` is a one-method ABC); further splitting the implementation would be process overhead disproportionate to the actual surface area, unlike Tool Runtime's five-bundle or Skill Runtime's three-bundle breakdowns, which were sized to genuinely larger scopes. | [hook-framework-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/hook-framework-design.md), [epic6-agent-platform-architecture](../docs/architecture/epic6/epic6-agent-platform-architecture.md) §9, [skill-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/skill-runtime-design.md), [tool-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/tool-runtime-design.md), Constitution §A6/§A12 | Hook Framework design approved. Planning replay: `SkillPlanReplayRecord` + `PlanReplayEngine` — a pure hash-chain re-verification over the already-stored plan/context documents (tamper/determinism check), never a live re-plan against registry state. `PlanningDiagnostics`/`PlanningStatistics`/`PlanningRepository` (temporary, mirrors `SkillRepository`'s convention) /`PlanningSerializer`/`PlanningConfiguration`/`PlanningFactory` composition root. 8 additive `core.events.EventType` members (`skill.plan.created`, `skill.context.created`, `skill.policy.evaluated`, `skill.replay.recorded`, `skill.replay.validated`, `skill.replay.mismatch`, `skill.diagnostics.generated`, `skill.planning.failed`) + one additive `EventSource.SKILL` member — the first Skill-owned events (Bundle 1 added none). `CapabilityResolver` remains fully unimplemented and is never imported by `skill/planning/` (verified: neither `CapabilityResolver(` nor `can_satisfy` appears anywhere in the subpackage's source). `SkillPlanningRuntime`/`SkillExecutionPlanner` expose no `execute`/`invoke`/`run` method. 41 new tests (921/921 repository-wide, up from 880); ruff clean. Planning, execution context, policies, observability, and replay are explicit non-goals here (Bundle 2). | Per the M6.2B-1 prompt's explicit "implement ONLY Bundle 1, do not redesign approved architecture, never import Tool Runtime or any Epic 5 runtime" mandate; `git status` verified only `platform/skill/` + its test file were added, zero existing files touched (no `core/events.py` change this bundle — no new events are owned yet, observability is Bundle 2). Key calls: (a) **Dependency cycles are structurally impossible through the public registration API** — a `SkillDependency` must already be registered before the depending skill can register, so a true cycle in the concrete `(namespace, id, version)` graph cannot be constructed (a later version of the same id depending on something that depends on an earlier version of itself is a valid DAG, not a cycle — two distinct nodes). The DFS cycle guard in `registry._resolve_closure` is therefore defense-in-depth; verified live (not dead code) via a dedicated white-box test that directly poisons a descriptor's cached closure to force the guard to fire. (b) **The registry is the durable catalog; the repository is a temporary registration log, not the source of truth** — recorded explicitly per the M6.2A design's own Risk item, since copy-pasting Tool Runtime's "temporary only" repository convention onto what is actually a long-lived catalog (closer to Prompt's `TemplateRegistry`) would have been a real design mistake, not a neutral pattern reuse. (c) **`interface_version` is validated, `version` is not shape-checked the same way** — `interface_version` gates whether Skill Runtime's own parser/validator understands this manifest's *shape* at all (fail fast on an unsupported one); `version` is the skill's own semantic identity, resolved the same way Tool's version resolution already works. Conflating the two would have blurred a distinction the M6.2B-1 prompt explicitly asked for. (d) **Zero import of `tool`, verified by direct AST scan, not just by convention** — `skill/ids.py`, `skill/_run.py`, and `skill/stores/local_stores.py` are deliberate, local duplicates of the identical `tool/` primitives (content-hash ids, the sync/async bridge, the in-memory document store) rather than shared imports, the same "duplicate, don't cross-import" precedent every closed runtime already follows. | [skill-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/skill-runtime-design.md), [tool-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/tool-runtime-design.md), Constitution §A6/§A12 | Skill Runtime Bundle 1 landed. | DL-0049 | 2026-07-24 | Technical Owner | **M6.2A Skill Runtime Design (design-only, zero code)**. The complete Skill Runtime architecture (`docs/architecture/epic6/skill-runtime-design.md`), superseding Phase 0's brief §8 sketch with the full design the M6.2A prompt commissioned. A Skill is a reusable, versioned, declarative capability that *composes* Tools/Skills/runtime services — never an Agent, Workflow, Tool, Prompt, or executable code. Owns registration/discovery/manifests/descriptors/versioning/lifecycle/dependency-graph-validation/structural-validation/execution-*planning*/metadata/capability-*declaration*/composition/execution-context/observability-and-replay-*metadata* only; never Tool execution, Workflow execution, Agent planning, Memory, Reasoning, Provider routing, MCP, or A2A. `SkillManifest` = a hybrid decision (YAML for human authoring/interchange, a frozen Python dataclass as the runtime's canonical, content-hashed, validated value type — never executable code), justified against 3 rejected alternatives (JSON-only, YAML-only-at-read-time, code-package). `SkillDescriptor` mirrors `ToolDescriptor`'s manifest/descriptor separation, plus one Skill-specific addition: a cached `dependency_closure` (Skills, unlike Tools, can depend on other Skills). `SkillRegistry` mirrors `ToolRegistry`'s "intentionally dumb" discipline exactly, plus DFS dependency-cycle rejection at registration (the *data*-graph analog of the AST-verified *package*-graph acyclic rule). One `core.fsm` lifecycle only (DRAFT→REGISTERED→ACTIVE→DEPRECATED→RETIRED, identical shape to Tool's descriptor lifecycle including the direct RETIRE edges) — a deliberate, explicitly-flagged asymmetry from Tool Runtime's dual-FSM shape, since a Skill never executes and therefore needs no second, execution-lifecycle FSM. Capability model: `SkillCapabilityRequirement(kind ∈ {tool,memory,knowledge,provider,skill}, tag)` — declared and structurally validated by Skill Runtime, **never resolved** by it; resolution is deferred to a future Agent Runtime via an injected `CapabilityResolver` extension point, so `skill` imports none of `tool`/`memory`/`knowledge`/`provider`. Composition model: a two-tier system — hard, registry-validated `dependencies` (skill-to-skill, acyclic by construction) distinct from soft `skill:`-kind capability requirements (a preference, not a hard need). `SkillExecutionPlanner` (planning only — deterministic topological sort with a stable `(namespace, id, version)` tie-break, producing a replayable `SkillPlan` with an honest `RequirementVerdict.UNKNOWN` when no resolver was injected — never silently assumed satisfiable). `SkillExecutionContext` (immutable — designed now to become the standard execution contract future Workflow/Agent/MCP/A2A Runtimes will accept; inline session/trace/org/workspace fields, no `tenant.TenantContext`/`pipeline.RequestContext` import, identical precedent to `ToolRequest` and `GovernanceIdentity`). Skill Policies/Observability/Replay: ownership declared only (interfaces in `extensions.py` reusing the exact `PolicyVerdict` shape `tool.extensions.PolicyEngine` already established), zero implementation, deferred to Bundle 2. `skill → core[, tenant]` only — confirmed no `core.ports.skill` exists at M5.0 by direct inspection of `platform/core/ports/`. 4 ADRs recommended (Skills-are-a-manifest ratification; capability-requirements-declared-never-resolved boundary; two-tier composition model; no-SkillPort-at-this-stage). Exactly 3 implementation bundles (Core → Planning/Context/Policies/Observability/Replay → Production Certification), per the milestone's explicit "do not split further" instruction. Zero Tool/Provider/Memory/Knowledge/Context/Prompt/Reasoning/Pipeline changes (`git status` verified: documentation-only diff). | Per the M6.2A prompt's explicit "design milestone, no implementation, no redesign of any closed package, integrate through published Tool Runtime extension points only" mandate. Key calls: (a) **No `tool` import, by design, not by oversight** — matches the Phase-0 sketch's own `skill → core[, tenant]` dependency line, but this document is the first to *justify* it: Skill Runtime's capability requirements are pure data (namespaced tag strings) validated structurally only; a future composition root (Agent Runtime) is responsible for injecting a `CapabilityResolver` adapter that wraps Tool Runtime's already-published, read-only `ToolCapabilityCatalog.supports()` — the literal meaning of "integrate through published extension points only." (b) **Exactly one lifecycle FSM, not two** — Tool Runtime's second (execution) FSM exists because Tool Runtime executes; Skill Runtime never does, so a second FSM would be architecture built for a capability this runtime will never have. Recorded explicitly so a future contributor doesn't "complete the pattern" by adding an unused execution FSM merely because Tool Runtime has one. (c) **Hybrid manifest format, not YAML-only** — a raw YAML file is not a validated, replay-safe, content-hashable runtime value on its own; the frozen dataclass loaded from it is the only value type `skill/` code ever touches after registration, identical in spirit to how `ToolManifest.inputs`/`.outputs` are JSON-Schema-*shaped fields inside a Python object, never raw JSON at the domain layer. (d) **`RequirementVerdict.UNKNOWN` is a first-class verdict, not an omission** — since Skill Runtime has zero visibility into Tool/Memory/Knowledge/Provider registries unless a resolver is explicitly injected, silently defaulting an unresolvable requirement to `SATISFIABLE` would be a correctness bug waiting for a real consumer; recording `UNKNOWN` as a distinct state from the start prevents that shortcut. (e) **The registry is the durable-for-process catalog, not a temporary result store** — flagged explicitly in Risks (§18) because Tool Runtime's own `ToolRepository` convention ("temporary only") is correct for *execution results* but would be a design mistake if copy-pasted onto a skill *catalog*, which is conceptually closer to Prompt's long-lived `TemplateRegistry`. | [skill-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/skill-runtime-design.md), [tool-runtime-design](../docs/architecture/epic6/tool-runtime-design.md), [epic6-agent-platform-architecture](../docs/architecture/epic6/epic6-agent-platform-architecture.md) §8, Constitution §A6/§A12 | Skill Runtime design approved. The in-memory `VectorStore.search` is implemented (the ABC requires it) but **unused** by the runtime — semantic search stays deferred. Content-event ingestion is deferred: the runtime subscribes to nothing (empty type filter) + `should_handle` returns False, attaching only to publish. `forget` emits `MEMORY_CLEARED` (closest existing type; no new EventType added — "use existing Event Types"). | [memory-runtime-design (M5.1A)](../docs/architecture/memory-runtime.md), [ai-runtime-foundation](../docs/architecture/ai-runtime-foundation.md), Epic-5 summit, Constitution §A5/§A6 | Retrieval Engine + ranking + policies + expiration + cache + content-event ingestion + real store adapters (Postgres/pgvector/Qdrant/Redis) are M5.1B-2+ (all additive behind the same contracts) | Accepted |

Placement: `core/fsm` (deterministic FSM — replaces the 5 Turn-engine hand-rolled tables, *migration deferred*), `core/module` (RuntimeModule whose lifecycle **dogfoods** `core/fsm`; BusConsumer that publishes standard envelopes so **Replay/Gateway observe Memory/Knowledge events for free** — the summit's "automatic" integration), `core/ports` (8 query interfaces + neutral DTOs — consumers depend on the port, never another runtime package → acyclic), `core/policies` (generic Ranking/Selection/Retry/Timeout/Budget/Compression/Validation — no business rules), `core/providers/stores` (Object/Document/Metadata/Vector/Embedder contracts — no DB coupling). `telemetry/` + `tenant/` are **floor packages importing nothing above them** (not even `core`); `core.module` uses a structural `Meter` protocol so telemetry is injected without a `core→telemetry` import (dependency inversion keeps the graph acyclic — enforced by a test). | DL-0019 | 2026-07-17 | Technical Owner | M4.3 Replay & Observability is a **purely additive read-only** package (`replay/`, imports `core`+`events`+stdlib — never `runtime`/`api`/`gateway`); replay is **deterministic + visualization-only** (no Runtime logic); metrics with no upstream events are surfaced **honestly** as `pending_metrics`, never faked — and the UI mirrors that | The milestone: inspect/replay every conversation, "recorder subscribes ONLY to the Event Bus", "must never modify Runtime", "replay must be deterministic, no Runtime logic executes during replay". Delivery is **non-blocking for the Runtime**: `_on_event` (run synchronously during bus dispatch) serializes once and `offer`s to each matching client's **bounded** `EventStream` — never awaits, never does I/O, fully isolated (serialization/offer/network failures → metrics + local disconnect, never an exception into the bus; the bus already isolates subscribers, so this double-guards). Slow-consumer protection is a per-stream overflow policy (drop-oldest default / drop-newest / disconnect). | DL-0017 | 2026-07-17 | Technical Owner | M4.1 Event Bus: `core.events` **refined** (unreleased M1 scaffolding → execution-ready) with additive envelope fields + a **deterministic synchronous** `EventBus` contract; delivery is in-process **run-to-completion** with a reentrancy guard; engines publish via a composition-layer **bridge** (they never import the bus) | (1) `core.events` (envelope + `EventType` + `EventBus` ABC) was M1 scaffolding with **zero consumers** (verified) — refining it (additive fields: source/correlation/conversation/metadata; sync `publish/subscribe/unsubscribe/shutdown`; `publish` returns the stamped envelope) is a refinement not a versioned break (DL-0009 precedent), honouring ADR-0004's "one typed envelope" rather than forking a parallel model. (2) Delivery is **synchronous run-to-completion**: `publish` stamps a monotonic `seq`, enqueues, and drains FIFO — deterministic, ordered, exactly-once in-process, replay-friendly, with per-subscriber isolation. This is the deterministic in-process choice the milestone prioritises; AR-31 ("producers never block on consumers") is about async decoupling/durability for *distributed* delivery — deferred to the M4.2 **outbox**. Even now a subscriber that publishes re-entrantly is **queued** (never blocked, never recursive), so the reentrant/cross-component case is non-blocking. (3) Engines keep their sync `on_event` hook; `runtime.event_bridge.RuntimeEventBridge` maps native events → envelopes and publishes them, so **no engine imports the bus or knows its consumers** (AR-30) and ownership is unchanged. The bus lives in `events/` (imports `core` only — never `runtime`/`api`, the M1 cycle rule); the bridge lives in `runtime/` (injected bus) → clean DAG `core ← events ← runtime`. | [event-platform spec](../docs/runtime/event-platform.md), Constitution §A5/§D4, ADR-0004/0006 | Durable outbox + retry/DLQ + async/non-blocking delivery + WS/SSE gateway + distributed broker are M4.2+ (implement the same `EventBus` contract — no producer change) | Accepted |
| DL-0016 | 2026-07-17 | Technical Owner | M3.5 Smart Turn is an **opt-in arbiter** over the sub-frameworks (they keep owning their state); the **semantic extension** is a registry-resolved `SEMANTIC_TURN` capability with a **neutral float-only** contract; the adaptive `DELAY_RESPONSE` hold is **recorded, not slept** in-process | (1) Ownership: the milestone says "existing frameworks remain independent, ownership unchanged" AND "the pipeline must consume Smart Turn decisions." So Smart Turn *reads* the endpoint/interrupt decisions and returns a final `SmartTurnDecision`; the pipeline routes its two decision points (turn-finish, barge-in-preempt) through it. The sub-engines still own their state — no overlap. Gated behind `AgentSpec.smart_turn` with an identity default policy so the prior 202 tests are byte-identical (verified). (2) Semantic extension "requires no runtime changes": modelled as a core capability `core.providers.semantic_turn.SemanticTurnEvaluator` resolved via the registry (`CapabilityKind.SEMANTIC_TURN`) — a future model (incl. LLM-backed) registers and the orchestrator picks it up with zero code change. To keep `core` free of runtime deps and stay provider-neutral, the contract takes a **named-float feature map** and returns a **float-only `SemanticHint`** (no runtime enums cross the boundary). The runtime never calls an LLM — it calls the contract; a misbehaving evaluator degrades to the identity hint (logged), never breaking the deterministic core. (3) Conflicts resolved by fixed precedence (interrupt > endpoint) — deterministic, not an error; invalid policies raise `InvalidSmartTurnPolicy` at construction (no silent config). (4) The adaptive silence hold (`DELAY_RESPONSE`) is exposed as `pending_delay_frames` for a real transport scheduler; the in-process local profile records it rather than wall-clock sleeping — keeps tests deterministic (frame-based, consistent with the VAD/endpoint/interrupt convention) and avoids optimizing beyond architectural readiness. | [smart-turn-framework spec](../docs/runtime/smart-turn-framework.md), Constitution §A6/§A4.2/§A12 | Real semantic EOT/intent models + prosody-scaled adaptive policies + wall-clock delay scheduling are post-Epic-3 (additive: register a provider / add a policy field / a scheduler — no runtime change) | Accepted |
| DL-0015 | 2026-07-17 | Technical Owner | M3.4 Interrupt Intelligence is **opt-in** (`AgentSpec.allow_interrupt` + a VAD provider), enabling a **concurrent interruptible pipeline path**; the interrupt engine consumes the VAF's **per-frame speech verdict** (not its state transition) | Barge-in requires (a) a speech signal *during assistant playback* and (b) playback that can be pre-empted. (a): the VAF only notifies turns/endpoint on the SPEAKING *transition*, but during barge-in the caller is often already in SPEAKING (no silence between utterances), so no transition fires — the interrupt engine therefore consumes the raw per-frame `VoiceActivitySample.speech` verdict (it no-ops unless the assistant is speaking). (b): the M3.3 pipeline plays the assistant response inline (blocking the STT loop), so nothing can be observed mid-playback; when interrupt is active the pipeline runs the response as a concurrent task while it keeps reading inbound, cancelling the task on a confirmed interrupt. This concurrency adds risk, so it is **gated** behind an explicit per-agent opt-in AND VAD presence — the default local profile keeps the exact M3.3 sequential path (verified: 178 prior tests unchanged). The interrupt engine is the sole decider (policy-driven); the VAF/pipeline only supply neutral signals. Interrupt state-machine errors root in `RuntimeConflict` (like turn/endpoint/VAD); `AssistantStopError` is a lifecycle conflict, not a provider error (the framework has no provider). `InterruptDecision` is a new barge-in concept — no conflict with `TurnPhase`/`TurnState`, no reconciliation debt. `LocalTTS` emits one frame, so barge-in is exercised with a controllable multi-frame TTS test double (sanctioned per DL-0009). | [interrupt-framework spec](../docs/runtime/interrupt-framework.md), Constitution §A6/§A4.2 | Semantic/predictive interruption, speaker-aware barge-in, adaptive grace/hold, and real-VAD sub-150ms latency validation are M3.5+ (additive: alternative engine or policy behind the same coordinator) | Accepted |
| DL-0014 | 2026-07-17 | Technical Owner | M3.3 Endpoint Intelligence Framework decides turn completion via a configurable policy, but the completion **trigger** stays at the STT-final segment boundary; VAD signals **enrich** the decision, they never independently trigger a response | The prompt requires "replace the STT-final heuristic … completion must now be driven through the Endpoint framework" AND "the local profile should continue to function." The local STT emits discrete text frames with **no acoustic silence** between utterances, so a pure VAD-silence endpointer would never fire — the transcript boundary (an STT `Final`) is the only place text is available. So the pipeline feeds each segment to `EndpointEngine.note_segment`, and the *engine* (governed by `EndpointPolicy`) decides: the default policy confirms on-segment immediately (behaviour identical to pre-M3.3, no regression — verified end-to-end), while `silence_hold_frames`/`min_silence_frames` > 0 defer confirmation to VAD silence for a streaming profile. VAD `SPEAKING`/`SILENCE_CONFIRMED` feed the engine's speech/silence runs (enrichment) but the response trigger is the segment decision, avoiding desync with transcript availability. The endpoint framework is **always wired** (unlike the optional VAF). Both endpoint errors root in `RuntimeConflict` (lifecycle violation), consistent with the turn/VAD/conversation machines; the framework has no provider, so no `ProviderError` variant. `EndpointDecision` is a new completion concept — no conflict with `TurnPhase`/`TurnState`, no reconciliation debt. | [endpoint-framework spec](../docs/runtime/endpoint-framework.md), Constitution §A6/§A4.2 | Fully independent acoustic-silence-triggered completion in the live pipeline (no STT segment needed), semantic/smart-turn EOT, and adaptive delay are M3.4+ (additive: alternative engine or policy behind the same coordinator) | Accepted |
| DL-0013 | 2026-07-17 | Technical Owner | M3.2 Voice Activity Framework is **auxiliary**: a VAD provider start/detect/stop failure degrades gracefully (logged, conversation continues without VAD) rather than failing the call; the activity **state machine** stays strict (illegal transitions raise) | The prompt requires "gracefully fall back to existing behaviour" when no VAD provider is configured, and to "handle provider startup failure / shutdown / runtime cancellation." VAD enhances turn detection but is not required for a conversation to run (STT still drives turns, M2C.2). So provider *execution* failures are wrapped as `VoiceActivityProviderError` (rooted in `core.errors.ProviderError`) and the pipeline logs + continues untapped — matching the transcript-sink "must never break the pipeline" pattern. This is explicit, logged degradation, not silent recovery; the *state machine* (`VoiceActivityEngine`) remains strict (`InvalidVoiceActivityTransition`), and duplicate same-verdict frames are deterministically suppressed (no transition), not errors. `VoiceActivityState` is a new acoustic-activity concept — it does **not** conflict with `TurnPhase`/`TurnState`, so no reconciliation debt (unlike DL-0012). | [voice-activity-framework spec](../docs/runtime/voice-activity-framework.md), Constitution §A6/§A10 | Real VAD engines register additively (no runtime change); endpoint detection consuming `SILENCE_CONFIRMED` to drive `on_user_final` is M3.3 | Accepted |

Per the DL-0007 precedent + "do not modify the Constitution", `TurnPhase` lives in `runtime/turn/` (runtime-internal, like `WorkerState`); `core.states.TurnState` is left untouched. The finer TurnState states plug into the lifecycle as VAD/endpointing/tools land (M3.2+); merging them (or promoting TurnPhase to core) is a future ADR. | [turn-engine spec](../docs/runtime/turn-engine.md), Constitution §A4.4/§A0 | Two turn concepts coexist until M3.2+ reconciles them; documented, not silent | Accepted |

**Rules:** IDs are sequential and never reused · entries are never edited after acceptance — status updates append a note (`Status: Closed 2026-08-01, see DL-0009`) · every `DEFERRED(DL-xxx)` in code must resolve to an entry here with an Open status and an owner · Open entries are reviewed at every release (GOVERNANCE §10).
